A pick wins, Auto included, and the server applies nothing ([D-145](decisions.md#d-145--a-cut-off-flag-is-a-fact-about-one-reply-the-offer-keeps-the-cards-scope-and-the-server-never-applies-the-accounts-default-depth-2026-11-05)).

#### S-1.15.4 🟢 Per-ask answer depth on the Q&A pages
**Filed and shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#395](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/395) (batch 4 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-125](decisions.md#d-125--a-knowledge-reports-depth-applies-d-064-without-a-bracket-and-belongs-to-the-shared-artifact-and-a-failed-synthesis-never-reaches-it-2026-11-04). It had no board entry: since T-1.15.2.3 the job and library Q&A requests accepted `output_length`, but no page sent it.
- [x] T-1.15.4.1 `utils/depth.ts` and `components/common/DepthSelect.tsx`: an "Answer depth" select (Default, Brief, Standard, Deep) on the job Q&A panel and the library Q&A page. The request carries `output_length` only when a depth is picked, on every ask path (streaming, its 404 fallback, Confirm, Skip, a Shift-clicked suggestion), so the router's request, else job, else bracket precedence is unchanged. On the job page Default names the run's depth, e.g. "Default (deep)"
- [x] T-1.15.4.2 A pick is for one ask (a review fix, `finishAsk`): it clears when the answer lands, a failed ask keeps it for the retry, and "Edit question" keeps it because that is still the same ask
- [x] T-1.15.4.3 The select sits with each surface's other per-ask option rather than beside Ask as the spec said, because on a 320 px phone it would leave the input about 65 px. On phones a picked depth also shows inside the docked composer (`ComposerDepthNote`: "Answer depth: Standard" with a 44 px Clear). It renders nothing on Default, so the `job-qa-*.png` baselines are unchanged. Device test in `tests/mobile.spec.ts`
- Shelf Q&A has no control: the spec adds it only if shelf Q&A shares the composer, and `ShelfQAPanel` has its own form (a review fix removed the first attempt). Adding it later is small, since it shares the request type.
- [x] T-1.15.4.4 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #395)* Q&A brief still runs at 2,025 tokens on the same model, with the same thinking arithmetic T-1.15.3.2 fixed for knowledge reports. It overwrites nothing shared, so #395 left it
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#406](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/406) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-132](decisions.md#d-132--qa-brief-keeps-the-whole-thinking-reserve-a-cut-off-report-offers-deep-a-citation-with-no-document-casts-no-vote-and-clarify-is-one-optional-route-2026-11-04). It can truncate, shown by arithmetic and not by a live call: brief is 2,025 tokens, thinking counts inside `max_tokens`, and the reserve for medium is 4,096. `qa_agent._answer_budget` keeps the whole reserve on top of a budget the multiplier shrinks, for job, library and shelf answers: brief on the default model goes from 2,025 to 6,121, and with reasoning off it stays 2,025. Unset, standard and deep do not move, so on a thinking model brief's ceiling sits above standard's (OQ-29). How often it truncated is unmeasured, and the brief has not run on a live model (T-1.15.3.6). Tests: `test_qa_thinking_budget.py`, through the job, library and shelf routes with the real agents.
- [x] T-1.15.4.5 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #406)* `formulate_answer` does not read the finish reason, so a cut answer on standard or unset is stored silently, with no log line
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#419](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/419) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-145](decisions.md#d-145--a-cut-off-flag-is-a-fact-about-one-reply-the-offer-keeps-the-cards-scope-and-the-server-never-applies-the-accounts-default-depth-2026-11-05). `formulate_answer` and the library path read the finish reason through `llm_service.stopped_for_length` and log a warning naming the use case and the model, never the text. The fresh reply carries `answer_cut_off`, and job, library and shelf cards show a note in the style of `KnowledgeCutOffNote`, with **Ask again at Deep depth**. The offer fills the composer, stays on the card's own scope, and waits for clarify. Not run on a live model.
- [x] T-1.15.4.6 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #406)* The shelf panel has no depth select, though the route accepts `output_length`
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#419](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/419) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-145](decisions.md#d-145--a-cut-off-flag-is-a-fact-about-one-reply-the-offer-keeps-the-cards-scope-and-the-server-never-applies-the-accounts-default-depth-2026-11-05). `ShelfQAPanel` has the job and library panels' `DepthSelect`, with the same rules: the request carries `output_length` only when a depth shows, and a pick is for one ask.
- [x] T-1.15.4.7 🟢 *(filed and shipped 2026-11-05, from #419)* `stopped_for_length` listed `max_output_tokens` but nothing read it. OpenAI's Responses API, which langchain uses for its responses-only models, sets neither `finish_reason` nor `stop_reason`; it reports `status: incomplete` and `incomplete_details.reason`. The function now reads that, which also corrects the report and knowledge agents on those models. `test_llm_service_length_stops.py` runs each SDK's own objects through the langchain conversion offline.
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#419](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/419) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04).
- [x] T-1.15.4.8 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-05, from #419)* The cut-off note does not survive a reload. Storing it needs a column on both exchange tables, and the spec asked for no migration
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#426](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/426) (batch 7 of 2026-11-05). Linked decision: [D-150](decisions.md#d-150--the-cut-off-flag-is-stored-as-what-the-check-found-a-cut-answer-is-never-a-saved-searchs-baseline-and-a-cut-first-answer-stands-in-on-the-card-2026-11-09). `answer_cut_off` is a nullable boolean on `qa_exchanges`, `library_qa_exchanges` and `qa_history_exchanges` (migration `7bc07679d355`, no backfill), and each writer stores what the check found, `false` included. `GET /jobs/{id}/qa`, `GET /library/qa`, `GET /shelves/{id}/qa` and `GET /qa-history/exchanges` send `true` on a cut answer and no key otherwise, so an uncut row's JSON is unchanged and an older row reads as unknown. The job, library and shelf cards and the Echo panel's Ask read the note from the stored value. `qaCutOffStore.ts` now holds only the depth of the ask, so after a reload the note stays and the Deep offer goes (OQ-39). The fresh replies of `POST /jobs/{id}/qa` and `POST /library/qa` still send `false`, byte for byte (OQ-40). Mocked models only. **Owner step:** the migration applies on the next API start, before the worker.
- [x] T-1.15.4.9 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-05, from #419)* Q&A-history answers are not checked for a cut, and saved-search runs log the warning but show nothing
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#426](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/426) (batch 7 of 2026-11-05). Linked decision: [D-150](decisions.md#d-150--the-cut-off-flag-is-stored-as-what-the-check-found-a-cut-answer-is-never-a-saved-searchs-baseline-and-a-cut-first-answer-stands-in-on-the-card-2026-11-09). Q&A-history answers go through the same `answer_cut_off` check, with the same warning, and show the note on `/qa-history` and in the Echo panel, with no depth to offer. A saved search's cut run reads **Cut off** in its run history, stored as `decided_by="cut_off"` with no new column, and says it raised no alert. **Found on the way:** a cut answer fed the change detector and read as a change both ways, a false "No longer cites X" alert and email, then a false "Now cites N new sources" once it had become the baseline. A cut answer is now not compared and writes no baseline, while the gate still moves, and a real change after a cut is still found. A cut first answer stands in on the card, with the note (`last_answer_cut_off`). A baseline cut before #426 cannot be told apart and may raise one false alert.
- [ ] T-1.15.4.10 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #426)* The Q&A dataset exports still include cut answers. The stored flag lets them be filtered; whether to is a separate call

**Open questions**
- OQ-22 (from S-1.15.4): is the Q&A placement acceptable? On phones the select stays above the thread, with a note in the docked composer once a depth is picked, so changing to a different depth still means scrolling up. Putting the control in the docked row adds composer height and re-records the `job-qa-*.png` baselines. The owner's call.
- OQ-23 (from S-1.15.3): the knowledge drawer now asks before it spends, which reverses a deliberate earlier behaviour. The alternative keeps auto-start and offers depth only on Regenerate, at the cost of a second metered report for any non-default depth. The owner may want to rule on it.
- OQ-29 (from T-1.15.4.4): should standard and unset Q&A keep the thinking reserve too? On the default model, from a `large` corpus up, both are 4,500 with thinking inside, so a full 4,096 of thinking leaves 404 tokens. The spec keeps unset byte-identical, so #406 left them, which puts brief's ceiling (6,121) above standard's. It is a ceiling, not a length, since the brief does the shortening. The owner's call.
- OQ-39 (from T-1.15.4.8): should the Deep offer survive a reload? The stored flag is one boolean, so after a reload a card cannot tell whether its answer was asked at Deep, and it shows the note alone, the safe side under D-145. A second nullable column for the ask's depth would bring the offer back and retire `qaCutOffStore.ts`. The owner's call.
- OQ-40 (from T-1.15.4.8): `POST /jobs/{id}/qa` and `POST /library/qa` still send `answer_cut_off: false` on an uncut answer, as #419 shipped and `docs/api-reference.md` documents. The spec's "a reply carries the flag only when an answer was cut" and "stays byte-identical" conflict on those two routes, and #426 kept the bytes; `POST /qa-history/chat` sends the key only on a cut. Should the two routes match it? The owner's call.
- OQ-37 (from T-1.15.3.9): a report generated from the knowledge drawer's preset stores that depth on the shared artifact, as a manual pick does (D-125), and a Regenerate can overwrite a shared report at the preset depth. The spec names the drawer, the value in `users` reaches nobody, and the drawer still starts nothing on open. This is the closest the preference gets to something shared. The owner's call.

### E-1.16 🟡 Linguistic agnosticism with English output (R5)

**Scope-changed 2026-11-05:** stays 🟡 for T-1.16.2.7 and T-1.16.2.9. T-1.16.2.16 shipped in PR [#418](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/418) ([D-144](decisions.md#d-144--a-fifth-voice-label-other-names-no-language-to-write-in-a-job-fills-gaps-and-only-the-backfill-rewrites-and-the-declared-audio-language-is-a-key-on-the-document-2026-11-05)), with T-3.3.2.11 on the Echo side. T-1.16.1.11 still waits for the owner's backfill, and T-1.16.2.17 needs a model.

**Scope-changed 2026-11-04:** stays 🟡 for T-1.16.2.7 and T-1.16.2.9. PR [#403](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/403) ([D-133](decisions.md#d-133--the-tagger-abstains-without-hindi-evidence-the-presence-floor-reads-grammar-an-offline-lexicon-sees-english-in-devanagari-and-scores-are-quoted-from-the-production-row-2026-11-04)) closed T-1.16.2.8, T-1.16.2.14, T-1.16.2.15 and T-1.16.1.10. T-1.16.1.11, T-1.16.2.16 and T-1.16.2.17 are proposed, with T-3.3.2.11 on the Echo side.

**Why it exists.** Search should favour English, but topics like Hindu/Indian subjects legitimately surface Hindi sources. Every intermediate and final output must nonetheless be English, with quotes preserved in original script + transliteration + translation.
**Linked decisions:** [D-065](decisions.md#d-065--channel-subscriptions-become-per-tenant-transliteration-reversed-2026-07-31), [D-066](decisions.md#d-066--language-contract-temporal-awareness-and-corpus-aware-qa-length-2026-07-31), [D-080](decisions.md#d-080--code-mixing-detection-a-lexicon-tagger-not-an-llm-pass-2026-10-19), [D-084](decisions.md#d-084--code-mixed-retrieval-embed-an-english-rendering-keep-the-original-as-the-stored-text-2026-10-19), [D-120](decisions.md#d-120--older-chunks-gain-their-era-and-publish-fields-by-a-metadata-only-pass-amends-d-072-the-language-profile-is-stored-on-the-document-2026-11-04), [D-123](decisions.md#d-123--unused-read-routes-are-removed-an-outputs-sources-list-groups-by-source-and-a-language-badge-names-a-language-only-on-evidence-2026-11-04), [D-133](decisions.md#d-133--the-tagger-abstains-without-hindi-evidence-the-presence-floor-reads-grammar-an-offline-lexicon-sees-english-in-devanagari-and-scores-are-quoted-from-the-production-row-2026-11-04)

#### S-1.16.1 🟢 Language contract + script profiling — shipped 2026-07-31
- [x] T-1.16.1.1 `prompts/shared.py` — one definition of the English contract, quote rules and code-mixing note, injected per stage (map gets no quote rules: it emits JSON)
- [x] T-1.16.1.2 English contract added to report + job-Q&A prompts. The audit found NONE existed — a Hindi corpus produced a Devanagari report
- [x] T-1.16.1.3 `language_service.py` — Unicode script profiling, `switch_points`, `needs_translation`. Combining-mark bug fixed (Devanagari was undercounted ~40%, hiding language switches)
- [x] T-1.16.1.4 Anti-transliteration instruction reversed to the three-part form (D-065)
- [x] T-1.16.1.5 12 tests, including one asserting the romanised-Hinglish gap PERSISTS by design
- [x] T-1.16.1.6 Wire `language_service.describe_for_prompt` into the per-job prompt path — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#227](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/227)): per-batch profile into every map call, corpus-level profile (stored as `statistics.language_profile`) into every compose section and the summary. Empty for plain English, so the common case costs no tokens
- [x] T-1.16.1.7 Persist the per-document language profile (`documents.language_mix`) so the per-video label stops being the only signal
  - Scope-changed 2026-10-19: S-1.16.2 now computes a per-document language profile at chunk time (`code_mixing_service.profile_transcript`) and carries it on every chunk as `code_mix_*` metadata, so prompts and retrieval no longer depend on the per-video label. What remains is a `documents` column holding the same profile, so the library, the job page and the reading room can show a source's language without reading Chroma, and older documents can be profiled without re-chunking
  - **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#390](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/390) (batch 3 of 2026-11-03). Linked decision: [D-120](decisions.md#d-120--older-chunks-gain-their-era-and-publish-fields-by-a-metadata-only-pass-amends-d-072-the-language-profile-is-stored-on-the-document-2026-11-04). Migration `0253eef57358` adds the nullable JSON-text column. Every chunking path (the six sites in the two job loops, PDF upload, pasted URLs, `refetch_dubbed_transcripts.py` and `retry_unavailable_transcripts.py`) calls `language_mix_service.ensure(document, segments)` and hands the result to `chunk_transcript(..., language_profile=...)`, so the text is profiled once and the column describes the text the chunks were built from; a re-fetched transcript replaces it, and unchanged text writes nothing. `language_mix` is a read-only field on `GET /library/videos` (and shelves), `GET /jobs/{id}/videos` and `GET /channels/{id}/videos`. Its `code_mixed` is the chunker's 3% rule applied to the measured text, not a fact about the stored chunks, and the OpenAPI description says so. `scripts/backfill_language_mix.py` (a dry run by default; `--write`, `--all`, `--document-ids`) profiles older documents from `transcript_cache`, else from their chunks put back together, with no LLM. On a snapshot of the live library it would store 254 profiles (220 `en`, 32 `hi`, 2 other; 36 not plain English). Behaviour change: on an R1 visual run, chunk `code_mix_*` comes from the speech alone. Tests at the callers (both job tasks through the real YouTube connector, the PDF and paste routes, both scripts), mutation-checked. **Owner step:** apply the migration (a restart does), then, with no job ingesting, run the script's dry run and then `--write`.
  - **Owner steps 2026-11-04:** migration `0253eef57358` is applied (the main session pulled master into the main checkout and restarted the services; the database is at `a68953e57c63`). Dry run on the live store: `backfill_language_mix.py` would store 254 profiles (`en` 220, `hi` 32, other 2; 36 not plain English). The `--write` run waits for the owner's go-ahead.
- [x] T-1.16.1.8 English-preference in `search_rank_and_curate` ranking — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#227](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/227)). A `LANGUAGE (a preference, never a filter)` block in the ranking prompt plus a `Script:` hint on titles not in Latin script (exact via `language_service.dominant_script`; the model infers the language). A test confirms a Devanagari-titled pick survives untouched
- [x] T-1.16.1.9 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #390)* Show `documents.language_mix` in the UI, for example a "Hindi + English" badge beside `transcript_language` in `VideoRow`. The API field and the TypeScript type (`LanguageMix` in `types/video.ts`) shipped with T-1.16.1.7; the copy and design are a separate change
  - **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#396](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/396) (batch 4 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-123](decisions.md#d-123--unused-read-routes-are-removed-an-outputs-sources-list-groups-by-source-and-a-language-badge-names-a-language-only-on-evidence-2026-11-04). `VideoRow` shows a second pill, so the library, channel and shelf pages all get it (`utils/languageMix.ts`); the job page's approved list does not render `VideoRow` and has no badge. Whether a badge exists follows the server's `code_mixed`. What it may say needs evidence, because on Latin-script text the profile proves "not English", never "Hindi" (measured: Spanish, Portuguese, Italian, Indonesian, Tagalog and Turkish all read primary `hi`). So "Hindi (romanised) + English" needs a `hi` or `ur` label, or a video captioned `en` with at least 5% Hindi grammar (the library's 219 English transcripts read 0 to 0.4%); anything else reads as the labelled language or a neutral name such as "English + other words". Accepted gaps: a Hinglish Reddit thread or PDF reads "Other language + English", and a Spanish video really labelled `en` would read as romanised Hindi; no such document is in the library today. Tests: `videoRowLanguageMix.test.tsx`, 15 review-fix mutations caught. Seen in a browser only before the review fixes. **No badge can appear on the live library until the `backfill_language_mix.py --write` run above.**
- [x] T-1.16.1.10 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #396)* The badge does not map ISO 639-2 labels: `eng`, `hin` and `urd` read as third languages, so a mixed document labelled `eng` would say "English". None exist in the library today
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#403](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/403) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-133](decisions.md#d-133--the-tagger-abstains-without-hindi-evidence-the-presence-floor-reads-grammar-an-offline-lexicon-sees-english-in-devanagari-and-scores-are-quoted-from-the-production-row-2026-11-04). `utils/languageMix.ts` reads `eng`, `hin` and `urd` as `en`, `hi` and `ur`. The #396 guard stays, narrower: `other` on Latin text and a low `named_share` are now trusted, so an `en` label no longer makes an abstained source "Hindi (romanised)". The badge reads "Other language", "Other language + English" where English frames most of the speech, or "English + other words" for an English video with a passage the tagger cannot name. `language_mix` now serves `named_share`.

Not done in #403: a profile stored before it says `hi` for Spanish, and nothing in the API tells old from new

#### S-1.16.2 🟢 Proportion-driven rendering for code-mixed speech — implements [N-002](inventions.md#n-002--proportion-driven-rendering-policy-for-code-mixed-speech-2026-07-31)
The shipped script profiler is exact for script mixing and **blind to romanised code-mixing** — the user's own example ("...taaki baadme koi kasar koi zarra na reh jaye...") is entirely Latin and reads as English today. This story closes that gap.
**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — PRs [#258](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/258), [#260](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/260), [#262](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/262), [#264](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/264), [#268](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/268), [#271](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/271), [#276](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/276); architecture/requirements docs in [#278](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/278); quote-fidelity fixes from the R1 replays in [#294](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/294), [#307](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/307) and [#309](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/309). T-1.16.2.1–6 are done; T-1.16.2.7 and T-1.16.2.9 are follow-ups that stay open (T-1.16.2.8 shipped 2026-11-04 in PR [#403](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/403)), along with the user's two open questions in D-080 (what the 60% threshold rests on, and Devanagari words in Roman-mode quotes).
**Linked decisions:** [D-080](decisions.md#d-080--code-mixing-detection-a-lexicon-tagger-not-an-llm-pass-2026-10-19), [D-084](decisions.md#d-084--code-mixed-retrieval-embed-an-english-rendering-keep-the-original-as-the-stored-text-2026-10-19), [D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25) (the threshold stays at 0.6 until T-1.16.2.7), [D-133](decisions.md#d-133--the-tagger-abstains-without-hindi-evidence-the-presence-floor-reads-grammar-an-offline-lexicon-sees-english-in-devanagari-and-scores-are-quoted-from-the-production-row-2026-11-04)
**Owner steps (from #403, none run):** with no job ingesting, from `backend/`: (1) restart the services so the worker loads the new tagger; (2) `scripts/backfill_language_mix.py`, then `--all --write`, which also refreshes any profile a job stored with the old tagger; (3) `scripts/backfill_chunk_language_fields.py`, then `--write --clear-stale`, which reads what step 2 stored, so it must follow it; (4) `scripts/backfill_chunk_temporal_fields.py --write`, independent of the others. On a scratch copy of the live store: 254 profiled, 34 not plain English, 2,829 of 8,567 chunks updated, 80 stale chunks cleared, 648 staying embedded as their original text, and a second run found nothing to do. The same two commands of steps 2 and 3 carry any later tagger change to stored data. *2026-11-05: the sequence still waits on the owner. #418 adds no write run and keeps this order; a job now fills only the gaps (T-1.16.2.16), so a refreshed profile still needs step 3.*
- [x] T-1.16.2.1 Detect romanised non-English spans — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#258](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/258), [D-080](decisions.md#d-080--code-mixing-detection-a-lexicon-tagger-not-an-llm-pass-2026-10-19)). Measured a lexicon + n-gram + Viterbi tagger against a `gpt-5.6-luna` pass on 300 hand-labelled units: real comments and captions from three Hinglish jobs, split DEV/TEST. On held-out romanised text the lexicon tagger is more precise at equal recall (token P 0.929 vs 0.803, R 0.890 vs 0.895), free, and runs at chunk time; the LLM is better only on English written in Devanagari (F1 0.965 vs 0.868) and stays registered as the reference `code_mixing_detect`. First finding: YouTube captions Hinglish speech in Devanagari, not romanised; romanised Hinglish arrives through comments and text sources. [Method and results](notes/2026-10-19-code-mixing-detection.md)
- [x] T-1.16.2.2 Estimate matrix vs embedded language per span — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#260](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/260), [D-080 addendum](decisions.md#d-080--code-mixing-detection-a-lexicon-tagger-not-an-llm-pass-2026-10-19)). `analyse_span` returns `{hi_share, matrix, embedded}`; the matrix comes from a Matrix Language Frame vote of system morphemes (auxiliaries, light verbs, postpositions), so content words never decide it. Held-out mixed units: 0.952 vs 0.855 for word majority (LLM reference 0.968). Every baseline miss was Hindi grammar with mostly English words — 11% of all mixed units in the labelled set. A closed list of English function words in Devanagari (इज, टू, द) now tags English, so an English-matrix sentence in Devanagari is recognisable
- [x] T-1.16.2.3 Proportion thresholds as a RENDERING policy — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#262](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/262)). `rendering_mode(analysis)` → `native_script` (Hindi grammar, Hindi share ≥ `CODE_MIX_DOMINANT_SHARE` 0.6) / `plain_english` (English grammar, ≤ `CODE_MIX_BORROWING_MAX_SHARE` 0.2) / `roman` between; the matrix gates both ends. Defaults = centres of the DEV plateaus where detector and gold decisions agree; TEST agreement 0.970 Latin, 0.823 mixed. **Open question for the user** (D-080): what the 60% rests on — robustness favours 50% (Devanagari agreement 0.912 vs 0.824) while the tagger cannot see English nouns in Devanagari. *Ruled 2026-10-25 (D-112): 0.6 stays until the native-reader spot check (T-1.16.2.7), since those figures rest on labels Claude made*
- [x] T-1.16.2.4 Transcript-level "primary language of communication" — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#264](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/264)). `profile_transcript` → `{primary_language, native_script, hi_share, matrix_hi_share, quote_mode, flicker_unresolved}`; primary = the language framing ≥ half the speech; `resolve_quote_mode` lets a span decide only whether it is English, the source decides the script. Removes 25 script changes across 7 Devanagari Hinglish transcripts and 128 across 11 romanised comment threads. **Found and fixed:** only Devanagari/Arabic/Gurmukhi had counted as non-English — two Bengali and two Kannada transcripts in the library measured 0% non-English
- [x] T-1.16.2.5 Bidirectional split: analysis/extraction/ranking run on the English rendering; quotation retains original + transliteration + translation — shipped 2026-10-19 in two PRs
  - [x] Part A, quotation — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#268](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/268)). `code_mix_*` chunk metadata (none for English); `speech: <language> — <quote form>` on every non-English source header in the report map, job and library Q&A; map items carry `original` wording beside English `content`, compose attaches the source's `quote_form`; knowledge extraction gets a measured `SOURCE LANGUAGE` line. The report pipeline previously kept no original wording at all
  - [x] Part B, ranking on English — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#271](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/271), [D-084](decisions.md#d-084--code-mixed-retrieval-embed-an-english-rendering-keep-the-original-as-the-stored-text-2026-10-19)). Non-English chunks are embedded as an English rendering (`code_mixing_render_english`, metadata `text_en`) inside `chroma_service.insert_chunks`; stored text stays the original. Measured first: MRR 0.565 Devanagari / 0.402 romanised / 0.805 English rendering (55 independent questions, 132 real chunks). No backfill
- [x] T-1.16.2.6 Evaluate on real code-mixed transcripts before claiming it works — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#276](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/276)). Held-out sources only: 80 unseen comments (17/18 Hindi-bearing caught), the map stage before/after on a held-out lecture and two comment threads (**0 Hinglish passed through in six runs, with or without N-002**; originals kept 0 → 25 on the lecture), the production composer (8/8 three-part quotes), retrieval through the production renderer (MRR 0.554 → 0.762). The predicted failure happened at FETCH instead: 2 of 13 Hindi videos arrived as auto-dubbed tracks (S-1.11.11). Two fixes forced: a 3% presence floor so English-primary sources with real Hindi carry the profile, and per-item quote resolution in compose. [Section 11](notes/2026-10-19-code-mixing-detection.md#11-end-to-end-evaluation-on-held-out-sources-t-11626). *The fetch gap closed 2026-10-29 with S-1.11.11 (PR [#386](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/386)), and both dubbed videos from this study, `1v3_AQ26jZ0` and `v6CuYULP3tM`, were re-fetched in their spoken language on 2026-11-03.*
- [x] T-1.16.2.10 Verify kept `original` wording in code — fixed 2026-10-19 (PR [#294](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/294)). Regression from #268 found in R1 run 5: Hindi speakers quoted in Bengali script, strings in no transcript. Map originals are now checked against their own source with `quote_fidelity` (the run-6 gate's rule): mixed-script words, wrong script and unaligned wording are dropped, the English point kept, the drop disclosed. Drops exactly what the audit fails on run 5 (4/37 off, 12/51 on). Compose material no longer JSON-escapes non-Latin text. [Section 12](notes/2026-10-19-code-mixing-detection.md#12-regression-invented-original-wording-and-the-fix-2026-10-19)
- [x] T-1.16.2.12 Keep the speech inside an over-full `original` — fixed 2026-10-19 (PR [#307](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/307)). R1 run 7: one-source map calls (D-090) wrote speech + transliteration + translation into `original`, and 68/176 real Bengali quotes dropped as `not_in_speech`. `quote_fidelity.keepable` keeps the lines in the source's script, strips Latin glosses the speaker did not say, records the rest as `original_stripped`, and the reading must still pass in full. Run 7 re-verified: 66 of 68 recovered, 2 still dropped, no kept quote lost. Map prompt: `original` is the speaker's words only

No LLM. TEST Devanagari precision 0.792 to 0.940, recall stays 1.000, share MAE 0.204 to 0.051. All 32 Devanagari documents in the library stay `hi` and `native_script`. D-080 open question 3: TEST Devanagari agreement at 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.8 was 0.912 / 0.824 / 0.647 and is 0.971 / 0.912 / 0.882, so the robustness case for 50% is much weaker but not gone, and D-112's 0.6 stands until T-1.16.2.7. The blind spot is narrowed, not closed, and the labels are still Claude's.

Cheap, but those chunks would still be embedded as their original text, since the English rendering ([D-084](decisions.md#d-084--code-mixed-retrieval-embed-an-english-rendering-keep-the-original-as-the-stored-text-2026-10-19)) needs the LLM and a re-embed. A separate decision
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#403](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/403) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-133](decisions.md#d-133--the-tagger-abstains-without-hindi-evidence-the-presence-floor-reads-grammar-an-offline-lexicon-sees-english-in-devanagari-and-scores-are-quoted-from-the-production-row-2026-11-04). `scripts/backfill_chunk_language_fields.py` stamps the document-level `code_mix_*` fields from `documents.language_mix`: a dry run by default, `--write`, no LLM, no re-embedding, idempotent. `--write --clear-stale` also removes those fields from chunks of documents now plain English, which would otherwise keep them for good, since no normal path re-chunks an embedded document. It is opt-in because it is the only step that removes data. **Those chunks stay embedded as their original text:** the script labels them for the prompts and changes nothing about what a question retrieves. The live database has no stored profile yet, so a dry run there says to run the profile step first; counts from a scratch copy are under the owner steps above.
- [x] T-1.16.2.15 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #396)* The prompts keep the flaw the language badge fixed: `code_mixing_service.source_note` tells the models a Spanish source is "Hindi (romanised)", and `quote_mode` follows the same measure. The real fix is in the tagger: abstain on Latin-script text that shows no Hindi evidence. It needs its own measurement against the labelled set (D-080), so it belongs to the R5 lane
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#403](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/403) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-133](decisions.md#d-133--the-tagger-abstains-without-hindi-evidence-the-presence-floor-reads-grammar-an-offline-lexicon-sees-english-in-devanagari-and-scores-are-quoted-from-the-production-row-2026-11-04). `tag_tokens` abstains (`OTHER`, `xx`) on Latin text with no Hindi evidence. `OTHER` still counts as non-English for every share and rendering decision, so a Spanish chunk is still rendered for retrieval (D-084). `profile_transcript` reports `named_share`, such a source reads `primary_language: other`, and `source_note` never calls it Hindi. Chunks carry `code_mix_named_share`, and the presence floor tells a passage from stray names by grammar (a review fix). The acceptance rule went into the PR before anything was measured. Foreign sample: 6 of 6 tuning and 5 of 5 unseen documents read `other`, and romanised Hindi controls stay Hindi. On the production row the held-out figures match master, because on the labelled sets abstention changes the name a word gets and `named_share`, not the shares; the PR's first claim of a cost and a gain was wrong, and a second scoring row now keeps the two apart. In the live library, profiled in memory, no primary language changed in 254 documents and two name-heavy English talks stop reading as code-mixed. The library holds no foreign-language transcript, so only the fixture shows the fix.

Both reuse branches (`resume_job_after_approval`, `_run_extraction_and_rag`) add the document-level `code_mix_*` fields to chunks that lack them, through `stamp_chunk_language_fields` and the era stamp's metadata-only update. A chunk with `code_mix_primary` is left alone even if today's profile differs, so only the backfill rewrites a value. Stamped chunks keep their vectors and gain no `text_en`. A plain-English document costs no Chroma read, and any failure is logged and the job carries on.

R1 run 7 failed its quote gate on "वेरी इंपॉर्टेंट लाइब्रेरी (very important library)": the composer back-translated an item's English into Devanagari next to a verbatim `original`. `quote_fidelity.scrub_report` runs after compose with the gate's run finder and verdict (the gate now imports both). It removes each failing run and its transliteration, keeps the English, admits titles and channel names, and counts the removals in `processing_notes.report_quotes_removed` and the processing note. Removes exactly what the gate fails on runs 5–7, leaving 0 failing. Compose prompt: non-English wording only from `original`, copied exactly. Original problem as filed: N-002 says such a source is quoted in Devanagari with its romanised original as the transliteration, but the R1 report-level audit (`audit_quote_fidelity.py --check`) looks every Devanagari run up in the corpus and would fail a re-scripted quote as `absent`. Either the audit learns that a Devanagari quote may stand for a romanised original (transliterate before matching), or the quote form keeps Roman for romanised sources. Raised 2026-10-19 with the fix in #294; not hit by job 419e691d, which has no romanised sources

### E-1.18 🔴 Visual understanding (R1)

**Scope-changed 2026-10-25:** 🟡 → 🔴, parked by the owner ([D-109](decisions.md#d-109--r1-is-parked-visual-analysis-stays-hidden-its-measurement-stops-the-code-stays-2026-10-25)). Visual analysis stays hidden and R1 measurement stops. The backend, the frames and the attribution guard stay in the code. Deferred with it: T-1.18.9.4, T-1.18.9.9, T-1.18.1.10, T-1.18.1.11, T-1.18.2.5, T-1.18.5.5, T-1.14.18.6, T-1.14.19.6 and S-1.18.4; T-1.18.8.4 also waits, because R1 measurement stops. Two ways back if the owner asks: (c) annotate only sources whose speech is English in Latin script and pre-register a pair on an English-only corpus, or (e) the post-compose LLM audit D-075 deferred, checked offline on runs 1 to 10 first. Either needs a new pre-registered pair under the D-107 rules. The D-096 capture caps stay undecided until R1 returns.

**Scope.** Work out where something visually important happens in a video, capture stills there, describe them in transcript context, and merge the descriptions back as clearly-marked annotations that downstream prompts read as visual aid and never as speech.
**Linked decision:** [D-067](decisions.md#d-067--visual-understanding-separate-frames-table-marker-in-text-annotation-opt-in-per-job-2026-07-31)
**Roadmap:** [L6 — Visual understanding](feature-roadmap.md#l6--visual-understanding-)
**Architecture:** [Visual Agent](architecture.md#visual-agent-visual_agentpy) · **Requirement:** FR-19

#### S-1.18.1 🟢 Backend pipeline — select, capture, describe, merge — shipped 2026-07-31

- [x] T-1.18.1.2 `frame_service` — one yt-dlp download, N ffmpeg seeks; `worstvideo[height>=360]`; size cap, wall-clock budget, path-traversal guard

- [x] T-1.18.1.4 `visual_agent` — select then capture then describe then persist, fail-soft at every level

- [ ] T-1.18.1.10 🔴 **Annotations never reach Chroma for a video that was already embedded** (found 2026-10-18 while designing the R1 measurement). `job_tasks.py` re-chunks a reused video through `_with_visuals` so the *report* sees the spans, but the RAG phase skips "already-embedded chunks", so Q&A retrieval over a previously ingested video never sees a `[VISUAL @ …]` span. Visual analysis therefore only helps Q&A on videos new to the library. Fix options: re-embed a document's chunks when frames exist and its stored chunks carry no visual markers, or store annotated chunks under distinct ids. Decide after S-1.18.3 says whether annotations help at all. *Deferred 2026-10-25 with R1 (D-109).*
- [ ] T-1.18.1.11 🔴 **Knowledge extraction never sees annotations either.** `routers/knowledge.py` builds its transcript straight from `transcript_cache.segments_json`, so the `VISUAL_ANNOTATION_CONTRACT` injected into `knowledge_agent.py` describes spans the model will never receive. Same decision point as T-1.18.1.10. *Deferred 2026-10-25 with R1 (D-109).*
- [x] T-1.18.1.12 🟢 *Fixed 2026-10-19: `{visual_annotations}` in `CHANNEL_MAP_PROMPT`, passed at the call site, and added to both parametrised lists plus a stubbed-LLM test that a span reaches the channel map prompt with the rule in force.* **The channel-report map prompt carries no visual contract** (found 2026-10-19 while landing S-1.18.6, PR [#234](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/234); [D-067](decisions.md#d-067--visual-understanding-separate-frames-table-marker-in-text-annotation-opt-in-per-job-2026-07-31) amended the same day). `_with_visuals` in `job_tasks.py` gates frame capture on `VISUAL_ENABLED`, `jobs.visual_analysis` and `source_type == "video"`, never on job type, so a channel job that opts in gets annotated chunks and `CHANNEL_MAP_PROMPT` formats them with no `{visual_annotations}` placeholder (T-1.18.1.7 never listed the channel-report family). Fix: placeholder + call-site kwarg in `report_agent.py`, and add `CHANNEL_MAP_PROMPT` to both parametrised lists in `tests/test_agents/test_prompt_contracts.py`.
- [x] T-1.18.1.13 🟢 *Fixed 2026-10-19, ahead of T-1.18.1.10 because the gap is live today for videos new to the library: both refine prompts take `REFINE_VISUAL_RULES` (the contract plus `VISUAL_SPAN_EXTRACT_RULE`, which tells the extractor to copy a span whole, marker included, and never fold it into a speech extract). Pinned by the call-site, rule-once and stubbed-LLM tests; mutation-checked.* **The two Q&A refine prompts carry no visual contract.** `REFINE_CONTEXT_PROMPT` and `LIBRARY_REFINE_CONTEXT_PROMPT` rewrite raw chunks into extracts before the answer prompt sees them, so a span can be paraphrased into plain prose before the answer-stage contract applies. Masked today by T-1.18.1.10 (spans rarely reach Chroma for an already-embedded video); land together with it.

Built after R1 run 8 passed rules 1, 2 and 4. By the pre-registration, both remaining outcomes of rule 3 build the UI, so only the quality claim waits on Gemini Pro's vote (protocol note, run 8). Linked decision: [D-096](decisions.md).

Q&A answers do not show stills yet (T-1.18.2.5)

Verified in the browser on job `419e691d`: 11 frame-list requests and 0 images at load; hovering a citation fetched exactly one still and showed it
- [x] Run 8's final vote (rule 2 failed, after the UI shipped): the opt-in copy now discloses that a report can occasionally credit an on-screen detail to the speaker. Whether to keep surfacing R1 is the user's call (protocol note, eight-run summary)

#### S-1.18.3 🟢 Measure whether visual annotations improve output — measured 2026-10-19
Protocol and results: [docs/notes/2026-10-18-r1-visual-measurement.md](notes/2026-10-18-r1-visual-measurement.md). Decision: [D-071](decisions.md#d-071--r1-verdict-annotations-win-on-substance-fail-on-attribution-and-cost-ui-deferred-2026-10-19). Sequenced before S-1.18.2 on 2026-10-18, by the user's instruction to test before building.

Deterministic replay of job `419e691d` (11 videos, 120 frames) via `backend/scripts/replay_visual_ab.py`; three judge families via `backend/scripts/judge_pair.py`; 87-agent adversarial verification. **ON preferred on substance 3 of 3 (8.33 vs 7.00); 2 attribution errors in ON found by the sweep that every judge missed; ON 0 confirmed fabrications vs OFF 5.**

#### S-1.18.6 🟢 Fix the speaker-attribution gap in the visual contract, then re-run — rule and tests landed 2026-10-19
Both confirmed errors bundle a screen-only detail into a sentence credited to a named speaker. `VISUAL_ANNOTATION_CONTRACT` forbade reading spans as speech but said nothing about mixing the two inside one attributed clause. **Sequenced after PR #227**, which rewrote `prompts/shared.py`.
**Rule landed 2026-10-19 (PR [#234](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/234)).** One `ATTRIBUTION BOUNDARY` bullet inside `VISUAL_ANNOTATION_CONTRACT`: a sentence credited to a speaker ("X says / notes / explains", quoted speech, "— Name (mm:ss)") may contain only what that speaker said; a detail that exists only in an annotation goes in a separate clause marked as shown on screen; a spoken figure noted as "also visible on screen" stays correct, since the sweep judged that pattern right every time. Stated once in `prompts/shared.py`, so the six transcript-reading prompts (report map / section / summary, both Q&A system prompts, knowledge extraction) inherit it with no call-site change; the D-066 language fragments and the D-072 temporal fragments are untouched. Still open: T-1.18.6.3, the protocol re-run.
- [x] T-1.18.6.1 Contract rule: a sentence attributed to a speaker ("X says", "— Name (mm:ss)") may contain only what was spoken; screen-only details go in a separate clause marked as shown — 2026-10-19, PR [#234](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/234)
- [x] T-1.18.6.2 Pin it with a prompt-level test on a synthetic transcript where the span and the speech disagree — 2026-10-19, PR [#234](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/234). `tests/test_agents/test_prompt_contracts.py`: the rule appears exactly once in each of the six composed prompts; and a two-segment transcript whose span alone carries "shaded 95 percent confidence band" runs through `annotate_segments` → `chunk_transcript` → `map_chunks` with a stubbed LLM, asserting the prompt the model receives holds the speech, the span intact, the rule, and the screen-only detail nowhere outside the span
- [x] T-1.18.6.3 Re-run the S-1.18.3 protocol unchanged; the rule needs zero confirmed attribution errors after the sweep. **Ran 2026-10-19: failed.** Run 2 (Flash-Lite descriptions) had 1 confirmed error and won 1/3 judges; run 3 (gpt-5.5 descriptions) won 3/3 at matched length but had 2 confirmed errors, now in the opposite direction (spoken details presented as on screen). The rule moved the error rather than removing it. See [D-075](decisions.md#d-075--r1-after-three-runs-the-modality-boundary-needs-provenance-not-prompt-rules-2026-10-19) and the protocol note.

#### S-1.18.7 🟢 Deterministic modality check — measured 2026-10-19, not shipped
`backend/scripts/modality_check.py` checks a report's "on screen" claims and speaker-attributed details against timestamped speech and frame descriptions. `eval_modality_check.py` scores it against the verified labels from runs 1–3. Best setting: **2 of 5 confirmed errors caught, about 8 other flags per report**, mostly false positives. Three causes: four of the eleven videos are spoken in Hindi or Bengali, ASR renders identifiers differently from the screen, and the composer cites every sentence "— Source (mm:ss)". It stays a research tool; [D-075](decisions.md#d-075--r1-after-three-runs-the-modality-boundary-needs-provenance-not-prompt-rules-2026-10-19) explains why it is not a gate.

#### S-1.18.9 🔴 Deterministic attribution guard — the gate for showing visual analysis again: built and measured 2026-10-24; runs 9 and 10 failed, R1 stays hidden
Accepted 2026-10-20: the user hid R1's UI ([D-105](decisions.md#d-105--visual-analysis-hidden-in-the-ui-until-a-deterministic-attribution-guard-removes-the-residual-error-2026-10-20)) until this removes the residual error. **The error shape** is measured across runs 1 to 8: a sentence credited to a speaker ("X reads / says / reports ...", or a clause ending "— Name (mm:ss)") that contains a specific detail present only in a `[VISUAL @]` span near the cited moment. It was weekday totals in run 8, a confidence band in run 1, and a version number credited across sources in run 6. The converse also occurs: speech presented as on screen.
**Outcome 2026-10-24** ([D-106](decisions.md#d-106--the-deterministic-attribution-guard-ships-in-the-report-pipeline-r1-stays-hidden-after-run-9-2026-10-24)). The guard ships in the report pipeline. On the stored ON reports of runs 1 to 8 it catches 8 of the 10 confirmed errors, against 2 of 5 for `modality_check.py`; that check is in-sample. Run 9 failed rules 2 and 3 of the pre-registered rule:
- rule 2: one confirmed error, a lone "10" from the screen inside a speaker's examples, below the guard's action threshold;
- rule 3: a substance tie, 8.67 against 8.67.

As pre-registered, run 10 was not run and R1 stays hidden. All seven of the guard's own run-9 changes held up under verification.
**Outcome 2026-10-25.** After the user's rule-3 decision ([D-107](decisions.md#d-107--r1s-substance-rule-a-tie-passes-any-loss-fails-attribution-stays-zero-error-2026-10-25)) and the follow-ups (PR [#367](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/367)), run 10 failed rules 2, 3, 4 and 5 (protocol note, "Run 10: results"). Run 11 was not run, and R1 stays hidden. Four of the guard's eleven run-10 changes were confirmed attribution errors, all from treating a detail not found in the transcript as not said: a caption homophone ("one to one" for 121), an inflection ("get started"), and two words spoken in Bengali. Rule 3 failed independently of the guard: mean substance 8.0 against 8.33. The user then chose T-1.18.9.11 ([D-108](decisions.md#d-108--the-attribution-guard-never-infers-that-a-detail-went-unsaid-it-checks-only-english-speech-in-latin-script-2026-10-25), PR [#372](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/372)): the guard no longer infers that a detail went unsaid. No new runs were made.
**Shipped:** 2026-10-24: the guard and run 9, PR [#363](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/363); the architecture and requirement docs and invention [N-003](inventions.md#n-003--true-by-construction-modality-marks-for-reports-written-from-speech-plus-machine-described-screens-2026-10-24), PR [#364](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/364). The story was 🟡 for the follow-ups below until it was parked on 2026-10-25 (D-109).
**Parked 2026-10-25** by the owner ([D-109](decisions.md#d-109--r1-is-parked-visual-analysis-stays-hidden-its-measurement-stops-the-code-stays-2026-10-25)): R1 measurement stops, so T-1.18.9.4 and T-1.18.9.9 are deferred and the story is 🔴. The guard stays in the code, on by default, and does nothing on a report without visual spans. Reopening R1 starts with a new pre-registered pair under the D-107 rules.
**Linked decisions:** [D-105](decisions.md#d-105--visual-analysis-hidden-in-the-ui-until-a-deterministic-attribution-guard-removes-the-residual-error-2026-10-20), [D-106](decisions.md#d-106--the-deterministic-attribution-guard-ships-in-the-report-pipeline-r1-stays-hidden-after-run-9-2026-10-24), [D-107](decisions.md#d-107--r1s-substance-rule-a-tie-passes-any-loss-fails-attribution-stays-zero-error-2026-10-25), [D-108](decisions.md#d-108--the-attribution-guard-never-infers-that-a-detail-went-unsaid-it-checks-only-english-speech-in-latin-script-2026-10-25), [D-109](decisions.md#d-109--r1-is-parked-visual-analysis-stays-hidden-its-measurement-stops-the-code-stays-2026-10-25)
- [x] T-1.18.9.1 Design: guard at the map item and at compose — 2026-10-24, `services/attribution_guard.py`. A map item's details found in a span of its own source near its moment, and not in the speech around it, are screen-only. If all its details are screen-only, the item becomes `shown`. Screen-only clauses are split off into a `shown` item. Otherwise the details are named in the compose tag (`[SAID+SHOWN; shown on screen: ...]`). Before this, run 8's weekday totals reached compose as `[SAID+SHOWN]`, which tells the composer to credit the speaker. *Since T-1.18.9.11 an item is never relabelled `shown` or split; its screen-only details are only named in the compose tag*
- [x] T-1.18.9.2 Post-compose check in code — 2026-10-24. Details in speaker-credited parts are marked in place, "(on screen at mm:ss)", with the span's own timestamp, and never deleted. "Not spoken" is added only for figures and multi-word quotes absent from Latin-script speech, with "15 000", "fifteen thousand" and "one two two" read as numbers. The converse: an unsupported "also visible on screen" is removed, and "the video displays X" is marked as said when only the speech has X. Sections are guarded before the summary reads them. Each change is counted, disclosed in the processing note, and listed in the intermediates. The guard is fail-soft and switched by `REPORT_ATTRIBUTION_GUARD_ENABLED`. Tests: 19 service and 8 wiring tests, eight mutations, all caught. `replay_visual_ab.py` gains `--env-file`. *Since T-1.18.9.11 no mark says "not spoken", and only sources whose speech is English in Latin script are examined*
- [x] T-1.18.9.3 Pass rule pre-registered 2026-10-24 in the protocol note, in commit `05e0fd8`, before either run. Rules 1 to 4 are unchanged. Rule 5 checks every sentence the guard changed. Rule 6: no detail deleted, and ON mean substance ≥ 7.67, which is run 8's 8.33 minus the largest measured consecutive-run swing, 0.67
- [ ] T-1.18.9.4 🔴 If it passes: set `featureFlags.visualAnalysis = true` and record the decision superseding D-105. *Not met: run 9 failed rules 2 and 3 (protocol note, "Run 9: results"); the flag stays `false`*. *Deferred 2026-10-25: R1 is parked (D-109), so no run can meet it until the owner reopens R1.*
- [x] T-1.18.9.5 Lone figures in speaker credits — 2026-10-25. Run 9's residual error was a single two-digit figure ("10", from the handwritten "10%"), below the one-strong-or-two-weak threshold. A lone figure is now acted on inside a list of three or more figures when the source says another of them (two digits or more) around that moment, and a possessive ("his examples", "Vivek's figures") credits the speaker without a speech verb. Measured on runs 1 to 9 before merging (in-sample): confirmed errors caught 9 of 11, was 8 of 11, the new one being run 9's; besides run 9's, the possessive adds four marks (two in run 7, two in run 9), each on a code identifier shown in a frame within the cited window. At the map stage run 9's item now names its "10" for compose. *Since T-1.18.9.11 this applies to English sources only; run 9's own Hindi sentence is no longer examined*
- [x] T-1.18.9.6 Quote transliterations are quotation — 2026-10-25. Run 9 put a mark inside the romanised transliteration that follows a Bengali quote. A bracket directly after Devanagari or Bengali text, across the danda, is now skipped like the quote itself; the translation after it was already skipped as a quotation. On run 9's report the mark inside the transliteration is gone
- [x] T-1.18.9.7 An accurate processing note — 2026-10-25. Where the speech is Devanagari or Bengali, or the captions garble the word, the guard cannot read it, so the note no longer says "found only on screen". It says what was matched: details that "appear in a description of the screen near the cited moment but could not be matched to the speech there". The converse note says "matched the speech but no captured frame of that source"
- [x] T-1.18.9.8 Rule 3 failed by a tie that no guard can change. Whether a tie should keep R1 hidden is the user's question, and it comes before another pair of runs. *Resolved 2026-10-25 by the user ([D-107](decisions.md#d-107--r1s-substance-rule-a-tie-passes-any-loss-fails-attribution-stays-zero-error-2026-10-25)): for the next pair, a tie passes rule 3 and any loss fails, measured on both mean substance and preferences, with no margin; attribution stays zero-error*

Use the same job, descriptions and verification as run 9, and verify every judge claim. Accepted 2026-10-25. *Ran 2026-10-25 on `cc388de`: run 10 failed rules 2, 3, 4 and 5 with every judge claim verified (110 verdicts; protocol note, "Run 10: results"). As pre-registered, run 11 was not run, and `featureFlags.visualAnalysis` stays `false`*. *Unticked and deferred 2026-10-25 (D-109): only run 10 of the pair ran. Run 11, or any new pair, waits for the owner to reopen R1 and is pre-registered again first.*
- ~~T-1.18.9.10 "Also visible on screen" after a screen-only detail — 2026-10-25, added before runs 10 and 11. Run 9's one upheld guard-sentence vote: after the guard marked Onur Baltaci's confidence band "(on screen at 11:14)", the composer's "the confidence band is also visible on screen" still said the speaker gave it too. When every detail of such a phrase is in a span but not in the speech around the cited moment, the word "also" is removed, counted (`report_screen_also_dropped`) and disclosed. On runs 1 to 9 it fires 7 times, each leaving a true "visible on screen". *Run 10: one of its two removals was a confirmed error (NumPy, spoken in Bengali)*~~ Reverted 2026-10-25 by T-1.18.9.11 ([D-108](decisions.md#d-108--the-attribution-guard-never-infers-that-a-detail-went-unsaid-it-checks-only-english-speech-in-latin-script-2026-10-25)): removing "also" infers that the speaker did not say the detail
- [x] T-1.18.9.11 The guard stops inferring that a detail was not said — 2026-10-25, PR [#372](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/372), the user's choice ([D-108](decisions.md#d-108--the-attribution-guard-never-infers-that-a-detail-went-unsaid-it-checks-only-english-speech-in-latin-script-2026-10-25)). Run 10's four confirmed guard errors all came from that inference: a caption homophone, an inflected form, and two words spoken in Bengali.
  - **What changed.** No "not spoken" verdict and no removal of "also". The map stage never relabels an item `shown` or splits it. A source is examined only when its speech is English in Latin script (`Source.readable`: Latin share ≥ 0.6, no R5 code-mix profile), and the processing note says how many sources were not checked.
  - **What stays.** Marks on speech presented as screen, which rest on finding the detail in the speech, Bengali digits included.
  - **Tests.** 25 service tests and 9 wiring tests. Eight mutations of the new pieces were all caught.
  - **Offline on runs 1 to 10** (in-sample): confirmed errors caught 6 of 11, down from 9. The three lost are real errors in Hindi or Bengali sources (dataset-name lists in runs 2 and 4, run 9's "10"). Report changes fall from 93 to 43, none of them new.
  - No new R1 runs: whether R1 gets another pair is the user's call, and rule 3 (substance) failed in runs 9 and 10 for reasons the guard cannot change

#### S-1.18.8 🔴 Modality provenance through the map/reduce pipeline — T-1 to T-3 shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#277](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/277)); T-4 open
Decided by [D-075](decisions.md#d-075--r1-after-three-runs-the-modality-boundary-needs-provenance-not-prompt-rules-2026-10-19): the composer currently infers whether a fact was said or shown from interleaved text, and prompt rules have not stopped it mixing the two. **Sequenced after S-1.14.6** (persisted map items make the labels auditable) and after the R5 session's prompt changes.
*Evidence added 2026-10-19:* four of the five confirmed attribution errors after the S-1.18.6 rule are in the one Bengali-language video (1 of 11). The composer misjudges modality where it cannot read the speech; the map stage reads each chunk's native speech, so that is where the label must be fixed. This ties S-1.18.8 to R5 (E-1.16).
- [x] T-1.18.8.1 Map stage: every extracted item carries `modality: said | shown | both`, decided from whether its evidence sits in speech, in a `[VISUAL @]` span, or both. The rule (`MODALITY_FIELD_RULE`, `prompts/shared.py`) tells the model to read the speech in its own language — a figure spoken in Bengali, native digits included, is `said`. Code then fixes what code can know: an item from a source with no visual annotation is `said` whatever the model wrote (the model's value kept as `modality_model`); a missing or unusable label is `unknown`, never a guess. Per-run counts land in `processing_notes.map_modality`
- [x] T-1.18.8.2 Reduce preserves the label (D-056); when the same item arrives twice with different evidence (overlapping chunks), the union is `both`. Compose renders each item as `[SAID]` / `[SHOWN]` / `[SAID+SHOWN]` / `[UNLABELLED]` with `MODALITY_TAGS_NOTE` telling the composer to attribute from the tag and never print it. Tags appear only when the corpus has a shown or both item, so a corpus without visual evidence pays nothing
- [x] T-1.18.8.3 Converse rule added once to `VISUAL_ANNOTATION_CONTRACT`: "on screen" only where an annotation shows it or the item is tagged shown; a spoken detail is never "on screen", in any language. A ride-along, not relied on
- Tests: `test_modality_provenance.py` (15, stub LLMs) — a Bengali chunk with a version number spoken in native digits and a ValueError visible only on screen, labelled said / shown / both at map; forced to said without frames; synonyms and missing labels; reduce keeps and unions labels; compose carries the tags, the note, and no raw keys; no tags without shown items; prompt bindings; labels in the persisted S-1.14.6 record and the audit. Mutation-checked: removing map labelling, the tags, or the union each fails a test
- [ ] T-1.18.8.4 🔴 Check the composed report against item provenance (deterministic, now that the provenance exists), then re-run the S-1.18.3 protocol with the multilingual-verification rule from D-075. *Deferred 2026-10-25: it re-runs the R1 protocol, and R1 measurement stops (D-109).*
  - *Run 5, 2026-10-19:* **0 confirmed attribution errors** in the annotated report, the first run of five. Substance judging was confounded by an R5 regression (#268's map-stage `original` quotes, fabricated in the wrong script) and by a Gemini Pro quota fallback. Run 6 follows the R5 fix. See the protocol note.
  - *Run 6, 2026-10-19:* fails rules 2 (two confirmed attribution errors) and 3 (both available judges prefer the plain arm; Gemini 3.1 Pro quota-blocked); passes 1 (quote fidelity, 0 failing) and 4 (0 fabrications vs 4). Both failures trace to map batching, not to the annotations: two channels got zero items, and one error is a span from Simplilearn's video credited to CodeWithMunnaX. That led to S-1.14.19 / D-090. Run 7 is pre-registered in the map-coverage note.
  - *Run 7, 2026-10-19:* **invalid as a confirmatory test**. It fails rule 1 on a single report quote the composer back-translated from English (a compose-level gap, now with R5). Exploratory reads: GPT-5.5 prefers OFF, Sonnet ties. Run 8 waits on R5's report-level quote enforcement.
  - *Run 8, 2026-10-19 (final):* rules 1, 3 and 4 pass; **rule 2 fails**. One confirmed ON attribution error (screen-only weekday totals inside a speaker-credited sentence), found by Gemini 3.1 Pro and missed by the sweep. As pre-registered, the quality claim stays unproven and the UI decision goes to the user; the eight-run summary is in the protocol note.

#### S-1.18.5 🔴 Re-tier the two vision use cases, then re-measure cost
Filed 2026-10-19 from the S-1.18.3 run: at the registry defaults (`visual_select_moments` gpt-5.5 medium, `visual_describe_frame` gpt-5.5 low) the estimated vision spend was **$0.198 per video** (11 videos, 120 frames at 640×360; selection ≈ 75K input tokens, description ≈ 159K input tokens), double the $0.10 ceiling the protocol fixed in advance. Both cheaper candidates are already on `VISION_CAPABLE_MODELS`.
- [x] T-1.18.5.1 `visual_select_moments` **stays on gpt-5.5, reasoning medium → low** (measured 2026-10-19, `scripts/retier_vision.py`). Agreement with the stored picks within 20 s: gpt-5.5 medium against itself 85% (the noise floor), gpt-5.5 low 76%, gpt-5.5 off 76%, gpt-5.6-luna 59%, gpt-5.4-mini 24%, gemini-3.1-flash-lite 23%. Real cost per video: medium $0.097, low $0.067, off $0.064, luna $0.002. No cheaper model is close; low keeps the model and most of its picks.
- [x] T-1.18.5.2 `visual_describe_frame` → **gemini-3.1-flash-lite** ([D-073](decisions.md#d-073--frame-description-moves-to-gemini-31-flash-lite-on-image-grounded-grading-selection-stays-on-gpt-55-2026-10-19)). Same 120 frames re-described by each model; 33 frames × 2 graders looked at the image, blind: gpt-5.5 mean 4.52/5, best in 54 of 66, 3 unusable; flash-lite 4.21, 5 unusable; luna 3.97, **22 unusable** (misread dates, axis values, mistook a code cell for output). Real cost $0.133 → $0.008 per video.
- [x] T-1.18.5.3 Measured total for the visual pass: **$0.075 per video** (select $0.067 + describe $0.008) against $0.230 before, under the $0.10 ceiling; pinned by `tests/test_services/test_vision_tier.py` at the measured token counts. The first pass of these numbers divided by 12 approved videos instead of the 11 that produced calls and understated everything by 8%; the test caught it.
- ~~T-1.18.5.4 Revisit the S-1.18.2 UI decision after S-1.18.6's re-run (T-1.18.6.3) passes the attribution criterion with these tiers. *2026-10-19: the re-run failed (D-075); now waits on S-1.18.8.*~~ Superseded 2026-10-24: D-105 moved the UI gate to the S-1.18.9 guard (T-1.18.9.4)
- [x] T-1.18.5.6 🟢 **Describer chosen at report level, not per frame** — *closed 2026-10-19: the user chose gpt-5.5 (D-081), visual pass about $0.20 per video; ceiling pinned by `test_vision_tier.py`.* (D-075). Flash-Lite graded only 0.3 below gpt-5.5 per frame, yet the report-level benefit disappeared (run 2: 1/3 vs run 3: 3/3). Grade three mid-priced describers per frame (gpt-5.4-mini $0.018/video measured, claude-haiku-4-5, gemini-3.5-flash), then confirm the best one with a report-level run before changing the D-073 default. **Measured 2026-10-19:** per frame, gemini-3.5-flash 4.70 (0/62 unusable) > gpt-5.5 4.54 > gpt-5.4-mini 4.37 >> claude-haiku-4-5 2.39 (55/62 unusable). Report level (run 4, Flash descriptions): 2/3 judges, substance level, 2 attribution errors. The report-level ranking follows how often graders picked a description as most useful, not its accuracy. The default awaits the user's spend-vs-quality call in [D-081](decisions.md#d-081--frame-describer-default-spend-against-report-level-quality-2026-10-19).
- [ ] T-1.18.5.5 🔴 Frame resolution: every model misread small text at 640×360 (`worstvideo[height>=360]`); graders had to upscale to read labels. Try `height>=720` on the same 11 videos, re-grade, and weigh download size (D-051 bot-wall traffic) against the accuracy gain. *Deferred 2026-10-25 with R1 (D-109).*

*Deferred 2026-10-25 with R1 ([D-109](decisions.md#d-109--r1-is-parked-visual-analysis-stays-hidden-its-measurement-stops-the-code-stays-2026-10-25)).*
- [ ] T-1.18.4.1 PDFs: page images are already available without any download
