### E-1.17 🟢 Temporal / era awareness (R3) — closed 2026-10-19

**Why it exists.** A corpus spans time. A claim made in 2024 is not the same claim in 2026; relevance and meaning shift with publication date.
**Linked decisions:** [D-066](decisions.md#d-066--language-contract-temporal-awareness-and-corpus-aware-qa-length-2026-07-31), [D-072](decisions.md#d-072--two-dates-per-source-era-at-ingest-time-as-a-ranking-preference-qa-statistics-from-the-database-2026-10-19), [D-120](decisions.md#d-120--older-chunks-gain-their-era-and-publish-fields-by-a-metadata-only-pass-amends-d-072-the-language-profile-is-stored-on-the-document-2026-11-04)

**Closed 2026-10-19 (PR [#227](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/227)).** Both halves of R3 now exist: publication date (S-1.17.1, 2026-07-31) and the era a source DISCUSSES (S-1.17.2), in knowledge extraction, search ranking, Q&A and the report. Chunks embedded before 2026-10-19 carry no era and no numeric publish fields; they read as unknown by design (no backfill — D-072). *Amended 2026-11-04 ([D-120](decisions.md#d-120--older-chunks-gain-their-era-and-publish-fields-by-a-metadata-only-pass-amends-d-072-the-language-profile-is-stored-on-the-document-2026-11-04)): S-1.17.3 shipped in PR [#390](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/390), and its metadata-only pass brings those chunks up to date when the owner runs it. The epic stays 🟢.*

*2026-10-25 ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): S-1.17.4 is accepted as a follow-up outside the closed R3 acceptance; the epic stays 🟢.*

**SCOPE CORRECTION 2026-07-31.** The requirement has TWO dimensions and only one was built. The user's words: *"the time when the videos were posted, AND which era/timeperiod they are talking about"*, affecting *"knowledge extraction, search ranking, qna, reporting"*. Publication date is done. **The era a video DISCUSSES is not**, and **search ranking received no temporal signal at all**. E-1.17 stays 🟡 until S-1.17.2 lands; describing R3 as "core shipped" was an overstatement.

**Correction 2026-10-18.** The claim above that "search ranking received no temporal signal at all" was itself wrong. Since PR #2 (2026-04-19), the ranking prompt has shown each candidate's publish date with a generic hint: "more recent is usually better unless the topic calls for foundational content". What ranking still lacks is the discussed era and a deliberate per-topic recency decision. Separately, the Q&A piece below (T-1.17.1.4) turned out to be unwired; see S-1.15.2.

#### S-1.17.1 🟢 Publication date reaches the prompts — shipped 2026-07-31; last three tasks 2026-10-19
- [x] T-1.17.1.1 `compute_statistics` derives earliest/latest/median publish date + per-year counts
- [x] T-1.17.1.2 Map chunk headers carry `published <date>`
- [x] T-1.17.1.3 `TEMPORAL_AWARENESS` / `TEMPORAL_EXTRACTION_NOTE` fragments; guardrail conflict resolved by SUPPLYING verified dates rather than relaxing D-062's anti-invention rule
- [x] T-1.17.1.4 Q&A receives publication span and weights recency for time-sensitive questions — live since 2026-10-19, when the routers started passing corpus statistics (S-1.15.2). The CORPUS note also carries the discussed-era span
- [x] T-1.17.1.5 Date into the Q&A allowed-sources list — `- "Title" by Channel | published 2025-03-12 | discusses 2019–2021 (retrospective)`, for job and library Q&A; the raw-context headers the refiner reads carry the same, and the system prompts say the suffix is never part of a citation. Pinned against the citation sanitizer
- [x] T-1.17.1.6 `published_ts` / `published_year` (ints) in chunk metadata, omitted when the date is unknown so a `$gte` filter excludes undated chunks rather than matching 1970. Pre-existing chunks are not backfilled
- [x] ~~T-1.17.1.7 Optional `temporal_scope_extract`~~ — promoted out of "optional" into S-1.17.2; it is half the requirement, not a nice-to-have

#### S-1.17.2 🟢 The era a source DISCUSSES, and temporal signal in ranking — shipped 2026-10-19 (PR [#227](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/227))
A 2026 video about a 2019 paper is a 2026 source about a 2019 event. Linked decision: [D-072](decisions.md#d-072--two-dates-per-source-era-at-ingest-time-as-a-ranking-preference-qa-statistics-from-the-database-2026-10-19).
- [x] T-1.17.2.1 `temporal_scope_extract` use case (registry entry 23; `gpt-5.6-luna`, reasoning OFF, 400-token cap). Prompt in `prompts/temporal_prompts.py`; title + description + publish date + three sampled transcript windows → `{era_start, era_end, era_confidence, is_retrospective, era_label}`, integer years, negative = BCE. `temporal_service.parse_era_payload` tolerates fences, swaps reversed bounds, forces zero confidence on null years
- [x] T-1.17.2.2 Persisted as `documents.discussed_era_json` (migration `b1a2c3d4e5f8`), extracted by `job_tasks._ensure_era` before every `chunk_transcript` site in both ingest loops (a source-level test counts the sites), once per document and reused. Flattened into chunk metadata as `era_start` / `era_end` / `era_confidence` / `is_retrospective`, omitted when unknown. `TEMPORAL_SCOPE_ENABLED` kill switch
- [x] T-1.17.2.3 Temporal signal in ranking — the planner now emits a `temporal_profile` (`recency_weight` dominant / secondary / minimal, `target_era`, rationale) decided ONCE per topic; the ranking prompt receives the matching guidance block, each candidate's `Era:` when the library has dated it, and the explicit rule that no candidate is excluded for its date. Default when the planner is silent: secondary (relevance leads, date breaks ties) — the strength of "dominant" is the open design question recorded in D-072
- [x] T-1.17.2.4 Knowledge extraction anchors relative time to the publish date (`_temporal_note` in both knowledge prompts; "keep relative phrases verbatim" when the date is unknown); synthesis writes time-sensitive claims "as of <date>"
- [x] T-1.17.2.5 Report: `TEMPORAL_AWARENESS` now distinguishes "true AS OF <date>" from "true now" and requires supersession to be named; map headers carry `discusses <era>`; the corpus-facts block carries the discussed-era span
- [x] T-1.17.2.6 Q&A: the CORPUS note tells the answer model to weight recency for time-sensitive questions, the discussed era for period questions, and to say so with dates when sources disagree along a time axis; the refiner is told to keep dates with extracts so the answer step can see the axis

#### S-1.17.3 🟢 Backfill era + numeric publish fields into pre-R3 Chroma chunks
Deferred by [D-072](decisions.md#d-072--two-dates-per-source-era-at-ingest-time-as-a-ranking-preference-qa-statistics-from-the-database-2026-10-19): chunks embedded before 2026-10-19 carry no `era_*` / `published_ts` metadata and read as unknown. A document gains its era the next time a job re-chunks it, but its OLD chunks in Chroma do not. Worth doing only once something reads the fields at retrieval time (recency-weighted retrieval, era filters). Chroma supports metadata updates without re-embedding, so this is a metadata-only pass, not a re-index.
*2026-10-25 ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): this story may reverse D-072's no-backfill rule, with an amendment to D-072 in its PR.*
**Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#390](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/390) (batch 3 of 2026-11-03). Linked decision: [D-120](decisions.md#d-120--older-chunks-gain-their-era-and-publish-fields-by-a-metadata-only-pass-amends-d-072-the-language-profile-is-stored-on-the-document-2026-11-04), which amends D-072's no-backfill rule as D-112 allowed. `scripts/backfill_chunk_temporal_fields.py` pages through the global collection (metadata only), resolves each chunk to its document (`document_id`, else `(source_type, source_id)` or `video_id`), and adds whatever is missing or different of the four era fields, `published_ts` and `published_year`. It rewrites the chunk's `published_at` display string with them (a review fix, so the date shown and the date filtered on agree). It uses the chunker's own functions, omits unknown fields, removes nothing and re-embeds nothing. It re-reads each batch and sends the whole metadata back, so it holds whether Chroma merges (1.5.5 does) or replaces; a test runs against a store that replaces. A dry run by default; `--write`, `--document-ids`, `--json`. On a snapshot of the live library, 5,549 of 8,567 chunks would be updated: 5,111 gain the era and 5,549 the publish fields (counted before the `published_at` fix). **Owner step:** with no job ingesting, run the dry run, then `--write` (OQ-20 first).
**Owner steps 2026-11-04:** OQ-20 is answered (under S-1.17.4). Dry run on the live store: `backfill_chunk_temporal_fields.py` would update 5,549 of 8,567 chunks (era fields on 5,111; `published_ts` and `published_year` on 5,549). The `--write` run waits for the owner's go-ahead.
- [x] T-1.17.3.1 Script: for every document with `discussed_era_json` or `published_at`, update the metadata of its existing chunks in `videoresearchpro_global` (no re-embedding) — *shipped 2026-11-04 (#390) as `scripts/backfill_chunk_temporal_fields.py`; it reads the collection the settings name*
- [x] T-1.17.3.2 Idempotent and resumable; logs how many chunks gained each field — *shipped 2026-11-04 (#390): a second run changes nothing, the first failed batch stops the pass (exit 1) and a re-run resumes; counts per field, both gained and changed*
- [x] T-1.17.3.3 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #390)* When a job reuses an embedded document and extracts its era for the first time, stamp the document's existing chunks then. The reuse path re-chunks without re-inserting, so those chunks wait for the next run of the script, which is cheap to re-run
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#403](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/403) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-133](decisions.md#d-133--the-tagger-abstains-without-hindi-evidence-the-presence-floor-reads-grammar-an-offline-lexicon-sees-english-in-devanagari-and-scores-are-quoted-from-the-production-row-2026-11-04). `job_tasks._ensure_era` stamps an embedded document's stored chunks when this run extracted its era for the first time, through the backfill's own update. Fail-soft. Tested through `resume_job_after_approval` against an in-memory Chroma.

#### S-1.17.4 🔵 Measure the temporal ranking signal
**Accepted 2026-10-25** ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): it runs before OQ-9 is decided. The comparison's pass rule (T-1.14.20.4) is pre-registered before it runs; S-1.14.18 and S-1.17.4 are scheduled with it because all three spend the shared Gemini 3.1 Pro judge quota.
*2026-11-04 (#408): whether a real model honours the relative publish window's line is unmeasured. This story is the natural place for it, and it shares the judge quota.*
The planner's `temporal_profile` and the per-candidate `Era:` line are reasoned preferences, not measured ones. Replay the D-057 reference pool (S-1.14.6 persisted the rejected candidates) with and without the temporal guidance and judge the selections blind, the E-1.14 way. Also decides OQ-9.
- [ ] T-1.17.4.1 Re-rank the persisted candidate pool of job `0d4db8c3` with `temporal_profile` forced to each of `dominant` / `secondary` / `minimal`; diff the selections
- [ ] T-1.17.4.2 Blind-judge the three selections for topical fit and vintage balance

**Open questions**
- OQ-9: how strongly should `dominant` recency outweigh relevance for a fast-moving topic? Today: "strongly prefer the most recent among on-topic candidates; keep an older one only when clearly foundational". Stricter behaves like a filter; looser collapses into `secondary`. Raised in [D-072](decisions.md#d-072--two-dates-per-source-era-at-ingest-time-as-a-ranking-preference-qa-statistics-from-the-database-2026-10-19); for the user to decide, ideally after S-1.17.4. **Ruled 2026-10-25** ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): S-1.17.4 runs first and decides it; today's wording stays meanwhile.
- OQ-20 ✅ (from S-1.17.3): while building #390, its builder found that the Chroma store on disk holds a collection named `pratidhvani_global`, and that the live `CHROMA_GLOBAL_COLLECTION_NAME` matched neither that nor the default `videoresearchpro_global` (compared without printing it). The backfill scripts use whatever the settings name. Which collection do the services read? Worth confirming before running either script with `--write`. The owner's check. **Answered 2026-11-04 (checked by the main session):** `app/config.py` defaults `CHROMA_GLOBAL_COLLECTION_NAME` to `pratidhvani_global`, `backend/.env` does not set it, and the live store holds `pratidhvani_global` (8,567 chunks) and `qa_library_global` (222). Both backfill scripts read the collection through `chroma_service.get_global_collection()`, the path the services use, so they write the live collection. D-032's and this board's statements that the default is legacy were stale since PR #106 (corrected 2026-11-04).

## I-2 🟢 Brand & visual identity rollout

**Closed 2026-05-03.** All 6 epics now 🟢: E-2.1 tokens layer, E-2.2 primitives library, E-2.3 page migration, E-2.4 sidebar nav (all shipped earlier — verified 2026-04-26), E-2.5 marketing landing page (PR [#136](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/136), 2026-05-03), E-2.6 code identifier rename (this session — runbook in `docs/migration-code-identifiers.md` covers the operator-coordinated data-bearing renames).

**Status reconciliation 2026-04-26 (historical).** A backlog audit on 2026-04-26 revealed I-2 was substantially shipped — 4 of 6 epics were already 🟢. The earlier characterization in feature-roadmap.md ("documented but zero code shifted") was inaccurate; the warm-editorial migration largely landed in earlier sessions and was not reflected here. After E-2.5 + E-2.6 closed on 2026-05-03, I-2 is fully closed.

*2026-10-25 ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): E-2.8, the brand-asset remainder filed after the close, is accepted (🔵) as a follow-up outside the rollout's scope; the initiative stays 🟢.*

**Scope-changed 2026-11-05:** stays 🟢. E-2.8's accepted work shipped (🔵 → 🟢): the full logo and the marketing logos in PR [#404](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/404) ([D-130](decisions.md#d-130--the-brand-lockup-follows-branding-section-3-dark-logos-are-separate-files-and-the-outline-generators-tools-are-not-dependencies-2026-11-04)). The owner has not seen the artwork, so it stands as a proposal until they look (T-2.8.5). T-2.6.7 shipped in PR [#412](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/412) ([D-139](decisions.md#d-139--alembic-uses-the-url-a-caller-names-else-the-setting-and-alembicini-holds-none-2026-11-05)).

**Scope-changed 2026-11-05 (II):** stays 🟢. E-2.2's remaining primitives are settled in PR [#414](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/414): `Tabs`, `IconButton` and `Toast` moved in, and `Tooltip` closed with no primitive (T-2.2.1 to T-2.2.4). T-2.8.6 shipped in PR [#421](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/421) ([D-147](decisions.md#d-147--the-rendering-parser-decides-what-is-a-heading-in-book-markdown-a-rewrite-parses-what-it-will-emit-and-the-suites-tiktoken-file-lives-at-a-fixed-path-2026-11-05)). T-2.2.5 is proposed.

**Scope-changed 2026-11-05 (III):** stays 🟢. T-2.2.5 shipped in PR [#423](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/423) ([D-148](decisions.md#d-148--a-401-ends-a-session-in-react-and-keeps-the-page-in-this-tab-a-waiting-share-gets-a-link-a-mutation-that-answers-its-own-failures-says-so-and-tabs-opens-on-focus-2026-11-05)): `Tabs` takes arrows, Home and End.

**Why it exists.** Switch the running app from generic-AI-SaaS aesthetics (purple-blue gradient, default sans) to warm-editorial Pratidhvani identity (paper background, oxblood / forest-teal / vintage gold, Fraunces / Source Serif). Visual identity should match the personal-library / research-journal vision.

Arrows, Home and End with wrapping, and a roving tabindex. A tab opens as it gets focus, through its own `onClick`, since every panel swaps in place and spends nothing. With a modifier held the key stays the browser's (Alt+Left is Back). A real-browser test on `/library` drives the keys. `role="tabpanel"` and `aria-controls` were left out, because they are the parents' state, and so was Echo's `ModeTab`.

The GitHub repo rename (T-2.6.4) is genuinely outside-codebase and listed in the runbook for completeness. All codebase-side work is done.

**Linked decision:** [D-032 — Operator-coordinated runbook (vs automatic startup migration) for data-bearing identifier renames](decisions.md#d-032--operator-coordinated-runbook-vs-automatic-startup-migration-for-data-bearing-identifier-renames-2026-05-03)

The default itself stays at `videoresearchpro_global` so existing self-hosters who pull master don't get surprise data motion; operators flip the env var after running the backfill script described in the runbook.* *Corrected 2026-11-04 (found by #401): the sentence above was already stale when it was written. The default moved to `pratidhvani_global` in PR [#106](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/106), and `app/config.py` has it today; `videoresearchpro_global` lives on only in an install that pins it. Intentional non-changes for future migration tasks documented in PR body)*

Three sections — Chroma collection rename, SQLite file rename, optional GitHub repo rename — with pre-flight checklist, idempotent backfill script, post-migration verification, and rollback procedure for each. Promise: never destroys data, every step is reversible.)*
- [x] T-2.6.7 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #401)* The runbook's section B does not tell an operator to change `backend/alembic.ini`, which repeats `DATABASE_URL` and which Alembic reads instead (`backend/alembic/env.py` never reads settings). Anyone renaming the database file by that section alone leaves Alembic migrating the old file. The runbook's table row now states the gap; whether section B should change is the owner's call
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#412](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/412) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-139](decisions.md#d-139--alembic-uses-the-url-a-caller-names-else-the-setting-and-alembicini-holds-none-2026-11-05). A note was not enough: it leaves two copies of one value in a tracked file, and the failure is silent, since after a rename a manual `alembic upgrade head` builds an empty database at the old path and reports success. `backend/alembic/env.py` now uses the URL a caller names, else `settings.DATABASE_URL`. `alembic.ini` holds no URL, and a `sqlalchemy.url` line an operator adds still wins. The caller's URL has to win, because the app's startup and the migration tests name one, and some of those tests downgrade to base. Section B of the runbook says so and adds `alembic current` as a check. Found on the way: the documented `./venv/Scripts/alembic` could not run at all (`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'app'`) until `prepend_sys_path = .` went in the ini. The owner's `DATABASE_URL` equals the old ini value, so nothing moves. Tests: `test_alembic_database_url.py`.

**Sequencing rationale (closed).** Brand copy moved immediately in PR #97 because it's pure cosmetic with no data-motion risk. Data-bearing identifier renames stayed deferred because they're production-data-mutating and need operator coordination. The runbook closes that gap by giving operators a safe-execution checklist; the codebase keeps the legacy defaults so pulling master never causes surprise motion. Operators who want the new names follow the runbook on their own schedule.

### E-2.7 🟢 Research-flow UX polish (from 200-video test)

**Filed 2026-07-22** from the same 200-video deep-research test that produced [E-1.11](#e-111--transcript-pipeline-resilience-at-scale) (job `0d4db8c3`). UX findings at 200-row scale; all stories shipping in the same branch's PR. Linked decision: [D-051](decisions.md#d-051--transcript-pipeline-resilience-bundle-circuit-breaker--segmented-whisper--yt-dlp-client-fallback-2026-07-22) (context only — the UX work carries no ADR of its own).

#### S-2.7.1 🟢 Approval-list UX at scale

**Scope.** Default-select-all on the approval list (was 0/200 selected while the helper copy said "Deselect any candidates you don't want" — contradictory); copy made consistent with the default; `content-visibility` virtualization so 200+ row lists stay responsive (500 would strain the current flat render).

#### S-2.7.2 🟢 Q&A streaming

**Scope.** SSE stage events on the Q&A path — `retrieving` / `refining` / `formulating` / `extracting_references` — so the user sees pipeline progress instead of a spinner. Token-level answer streaming ⚪ deferred.

#### S-2.7.3 🟢 Per-tier `num_videos` ceilings

**Scope.** Per-tier ceilings for topic-job `num_videos`: free 100 / pro 250 / studio 500. Schema absolute cap raised to 500 per PR [#188](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/188) (was `le=100` backend + `max={100}` frontend, which silently clamped a 200-video request).

## I-3 🟡 Echo (personal-brain L3)

**Why it exists.** Long-horizon north star — an app that ingests the user's likes / WhatsApp / Google Keep / quotes / activity / location / interests over time and develops a personality matching them. Eventually capable of "speaking on the user's behalf".
**North-star doc:** [personal-brain.md](personal-brain.md) · [vision.md](vision.md) Ring 3 · **Plan:** [`docs/plans/l3-echo-personal-brain.md`](plans/l3-echo-personal-brain.md) · **Sequence:** [`docs/plans/build-order.md`](plans/build-order.md)
**Decision links:** [D-003](decisions.md#d-003--echo--personal-brain-as-the-long-horizon-north-star-2026-04-24), [D-049](decisions.md#d-049--echo-cold-start-readiness-threshold-shipped-triple-is-v1-personal-brainmd-sub-table-is-forward-looking-spec-resolves-oq-6-2026-05-06), [D-069](decisions.md#d-069--shipped-backend-surfaces-ingest-as-a-library-tab-author-studio-as-one-page-readiness-as-a-gate-mean-2026-10-19)
**Status:** 🟡 foundation shipped 2026-05-05; **frontend Echo surface shipped 2026-10-19** (PR [#224](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/224)). Schema + service-layer + cold-start gate + REST surface all live, and `/echo` now exercises all of it: a readiness meter ("you're N% there", the mean of the three gates with each gate shown against its threshold, and an honest line that the sources gate cannot be met while the connector registry is empty), a context browser grouped by the 10 kinds with add / edit (edit = upsert on the same `(kind, key)`; labels cannot be renamed) / forget, a sources-on-record list with revoke (`DELETE /echo/sources/{source}`, confirm first), and an empty-state connectors card. Pure logic in `frontend/src/utils/echoReadiness.ts`. Concrete activity-stream connectors (YouTube watch / Spotify / email / etc.) and the "speak as me" agent are E-3.2 / E-3.4 follow-ups. Per [D-044](decisions.md#d-044--foundation-first-then-concrete-implementations-deferred-for-i-3--i-6-2026-05-05), the foundation ships **without** any concrete connector — the registry is empty in v1 — so each connector PR is independently scoped. Known edge, closed 2026-11-10: a key containing `/` could not be deleted by `DELETE /echo/context/{kind}/{key}`; the route now takes the key as the rest of the path (`{key:path}`) and the form accepts such labels ([S-3.1.1](#s-311--deleting-a-fact-whose-key-holds-), PR [#462](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/462)). A label that is exactly `.` or `..` still cannot be deleted from a page, since a browser resolves it before sending ([S-3.1.2](#s-312--deleting-a-fact-labelled--or-), the owner's call). Linked decision: [D-069](decisions.md#d-069--shipped-backend-surfaces-ingest-as-a-library-tab-author-studio-as-one-page-readiness-as-a-gate-mean-2026-10-19).

**Scope-changed 2026-11-10 (IV):** batch 14 of 2026-11-10. A fact whose key holds `/` is deleted by its route: `DELETE /echo/context/{kind}/{key:path}` takes the key as the rest of the path, the access log writes all of it by its length, and the Echo page's form accepts `/` in a label (T-3.1.1.1; PR [#462](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/462), [D-174](decisions.md#d-174--a-facts-key-is-the-rest-of-its-delete-routes-path-the-access-log-writes-all-of-it-and-the-echo-pages-form-accepts--in-a-label-2026-11-10)). Three follow-ups are proposed: T-3.2.1.11, T-3.2.1.12 and T-3.3.1.7. I-3 stays 🟡.

**Planned 2026-10-18.** The roadmap's five components now map onto epics in the roadmap's own order: constant-stream intake (new E-3.7) → manual personality signals and retrieval (E-3.3) → activity connectors (E-3.2) → "speak as me" plus the always-evokable surface (E-3.4, new E-3.8) → opt-in fine-tuning (new E-3.9), with the privacy model as its own epic (new E-3.10). Findings from the code audit that change the plan:
- The foundation stores everything in `personal_context(user_id, kind, key, value, source, confidence, captured_at, expires_at)` with a unique `(user_id, kind, key)`. That shape fits *facts*; it cannot hold *events* (many rows per kind) or *shares*. The `activity_events`, `shares_inbox` and `voice_signals` tables from `personal-brain.md` do not exist and are not derivable from the foundation; each lands with its epic.
- `personal_context` carries `user_id` only, not the `tenant_id` + `user_id` pair `personal-brain.md` §"What this means for today's PRs" asks for. Today `tenant_id == user.id` everywhere (E-5.1), so nothing breaks; the pair is added when the first new Echo table lands (S-3.7.1) and backfilled on `personal_context` in the same revision.
- No Chroma collection exists for personal context; `is_ready` counts rows, sources and one `personality_trait` only (the D-049 v1 triple).
- No `backend/app/connectors/` package exists; the `EchoConnector` Protocol lives in `echo_service.py` with `authorize_url / revoke / sync / supported_kinds`, which is narrower than the `personal-brain.md` contract (no `initial_backfill`, `incremental_sync`, `consent_disclosure`, `privacy_weight`). S-3.2.1 widens it before the first connector.
- **YouTube watch history is not obtainable through the YouTube Data API**; only liked videos, subscriptions and playlists are. Watch history arrives only as a Google Takeout export. T-3.2.1 is therefore split into an API connector (likes, subscriptions) and a Takeout importer (S-3.7.5).
- **Several connectors in the roadmap list are export-file imports, not APIs.** Spotify's 2026 developer-mode rules (Premium owner, five allow-listed users, 250k-MAU business for more) make it a self-host-only live tail, with the lifetime streaming-history export as the real source; Strava's 2026 API policy forbids feeding API data to any AI application; Goodreads' API is gone and Letterboxd's refuses LLM and personal projects; Apple Health has no web API. The plan records sources. Consequence: the connector registry gains a second kind, the export-archive importer (`accepts(filename) / parse(stream)`), and S-3.7.5 carries most of the roadmap's Domain 2 catalogue.
- **Google verification is per project.** Adding `youtube.readonly` and `calendar.readonly` (both *sensitive*, reviewed in days, no security assessment) to the login client puts the whole consent screen under review, and unverified sensitive-scope apps carry an irreversible 100-user lifetime cap. Gmail read-only is *restricted* (annual CASA assessment) and stays deferred.
- The `/echo` page (PR #224) is the context store, the readiness meter and the sources list; there is no floating bubble, no shortcut and no chat — that is E-3.8.

### E-3.1 🟢 Personal context store schema

**Scope.** Separate-from-sources table holding location, interests, hobbies, work, talents, skills, personality, life events. Designed for opt-in, scoped, revocable bundles.
**Shipped 2026-05-05** in PR #172. New `personal_context` table (Alembic `a4b5c6d7e8f9_personal_context_table.py`) — `(user_id, kind, key)` unique, value JSON-or-string in TEXT, source attribution, confidence 0-1, optional expires_at for stale-aware data ("current employer" expires after N months without re-confirmation). Service layer in `backend/app/services/echo_service.py`: `record_context` (upsert), `get_context`, `list_context` (kind/source filter; expired-by-default hidden), `delete_context`, `revoke_source` (deletes everything from a connector — opt-out path).

10 supported kinds: `location` / `interest` / `hobby` / `work` / `talent` / `skill` / `personality_trait` / `life_event` / `daily_routine` / `place`. Set is closed at the API layer (unknown kinds → 400); extend by editing `SUPPORTED_KINDS` + adding a test.

#### S-3.1.1 🟢 Deleting a fact whose key holds `/`
**Filed 2026-11-10** from PR [#459](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/459) (batch 13 of 2026-11-10), which found it and left it unchanged; its real-server test pins the 404. It had no board entry beyond the "known edge" in I-3's status line.
**Shipped 2026-11-10** in PR [#462](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/462) (batch 14 of 2026-11-10). Linked decision: [D-174](decisions.md#d-174--a-facts-key-is-the-rest-of-its-delete-routes-path-the-access-log-writes-all-of-it-and-the-echo-pages-form-accepts--in-a-label-2026-11-10).
- [x] T-3.1.1.1 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #459)* `DELETE /echo/context/{kind}/{key}` answers 404 for a key holding `/`: uvicorn and Starlette decode `%2F` before matching, so the key arrives as one segment too many. The Echo page's form refuses such a label (`utils/echoReadiness.ts`), but the API does not, so a key written any other way cannot be deleted by its route. Possible fixes: a `{key:path}` convertor, a body, or refusing `/` in a key at write time. With `{key:path}`, `log_redaction.WORDS_PATH_PARAMS` and its guard must name the new template; the access-log filter already treats a `:path` parameter as spanning ([D-171](decisions.md#d-171--a-users-words-in-a-url-path-are-chosen-by-route-template-in-the-same-filter-a-path-no-template-matches-is-written-whole-and-the-import-script-answers-a-stop-on-one-line-and-names-what-a-part-way-stop-kept-2026-11-10))
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#462](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/462) (batch 14 of 2026-11-10). Linked decision: [D-174](decisions.md#d-174--a-facts-key-is-the-rest-of-its-delete-routes-path-the-access-log-writes-all-of-it-and-the-echo-pages-form-accepts--in-a-label-2026-11-10). The route is `DELETE /api/v1/echo/context/{kind}/{key:path}` (`routers/echo.py`), so the key is the rest of the path, every `/` included; a key with no `/` is matched and answered as before, and a row already stored with a `/` can now be deleted. **Who writes keys:** every write goes through `echo_service.record_context`. `POST /echo/context` accepts any key of 1 to 128 characters, `/` included; the GitHub connector derives `"{language} (GitHub)"`, and nothing stops a language name holding `/`; Apple Health's keys are fixed, and no other importer derives facts. The live database, opened read-only, held 0 `personal_context` rows and 0 tombstone keys with a `/`. Refusing `/` at write time was turned down, since the API already accepts such keys and a refusal would strand stored rows. `{key:path}` is safe only while no other route sits under `/echo/context/`, which a test now pins. **Access log:** `log_redaction.WORDS_PATH_PARAMS` names the new template, and a words `:path` parameter takes the rest of the path as Starlette's `.*` does, a trailing `/` included, so the whole key is written by its length however many `/` it holds; before, a key `k/` was written `[1 character]/`. The Echo page's form no longer refuses a label holding `/` (`utils/echoReadiness.ts`), whose only reason was this bug; `echoApi.deleteContext` already encoded the key whole. A derived fact holding `/` keeps its whole key on its tombstone, the next sync skips it, and "Allow again" clears by id (T-3.2.4.4). Tested by `tests/test_routers/test_echo_context_keys.py` (new, 22 tests: one, several, leading and trailing `/`, `/` alone and `//`, dot segments, Devanagari, `?` and `#`, `%2F` and `%25` typed as text through `httpx.ASGITransport`, which decodes a path once as uvicorn does where Starlette's `TestClient` decodes it twice, sibling keys left alone, and another user's key and an empty key answering 404), the tombstone round trip in `test_echo_connectors.py`, 15 keys through the real filter in `test_access_log_words.py`, and #459's real-server test, which now records and deletes all 15 under the real uvicorn CLI, 200 for each, with only their lengths in the log; the frontend has `echoContextKey.test.ts` and `echoFactSlashKey.test.tsx`. The mutation checks are in the PR. The full backend suite passed 6,220 locally. `docs/api-reference.md` and CLAUDE.md's API line are updated; CLAUDE.md's access-log convention still names the route as `{key}`, which stays true. **Owner step:** restart the API. Left out: a label that is exactly `.` or `..` ([S-3.1.2](#s-312--deleting-a-fact-labelled--or-)).

#### S-3.1.2 ⚪ Deleting a fact labelled `.` or `..`
**Filed 2026-11-10** from PR [#462](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/462) (batch 14 of 2026-11-10), which found it and left it unchanged; `docs/api-reference.md` notes the limit.
- [ ] T-3.1.2.1 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #462; the owner's call)* A fact whose key is exactly `.` or `..` cannot be deleted from a page: a browser resolves such a path segment before it sends the request, written or percent-encoded (WHATWG's dot segments), so `DELETE .../context/interest/..` goes out as `.../context/`. Unrelated to `/`, and true before #462. The choice is refusing those two labels when a fact is written, or a delete that carries the key in a body

### E-3.2 🟡 Activity-stream connectors (Domain 2)

**Scope.** Pluggable opt-in connectors. Recommended order (per [feature-roadmap.md L3](feature-roadmap.md#l3--echo-the-personal-brain-)): YouTube watch history → Spotify history → email (read-only) → calendar → browser history → Apple Health.

**Foundation shipped 2026-05-05** in PR #172. `EchoConnector` Protocol + `register_connector` / `get_connector` / `list_connectors` registry in `echo_service.py`. Each connector implements `authorize_url` / `revoke` / `sync` / `supported_kinds`. Concrete connectors are future PRs:

- [ ] T-3.2.1 ⚪ YouTube watch history connector. *Split 2026-10-18: likes + subscriptions via the Data API (S-3.2.2); watch history only via Takeout import (S-3.7.5). The Takeout half shipped 2026-10-19 in [#349](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/349).* *Board note 2026-11-09 (batch 8 census): nothing is left to build on this line. The API half is [S-3.2.2](#s-322--youtube-likes-and-subscriptions-connector), blocked on the owner's Google OAuth client, and the Takeout half shipped. It stays open as a pointer to S-3.2.2.*
- ~~T-3.2.2 Spotify history connector. *Now S-3.2.3.*~~ ✖ Won't do 2026-10-25 with S-3.2.3 (D-110): the history arrives through the S-3.7.5 import.
- [ ] T-3.2.3 🔴 Email (read-only OAuth) connector. *Deferred: highest privacy weight and Google's restricted-scope assessment; see plan.* *Ruled 2026-10-25 (D-110): never on SaaS before revenue, since the restricted scope needs a yearly security assessment; self-hosters use the IMAP inbox (S-3.7.4).*
- [ ] T-3.2.4 ⚪ Calendar connector. *Now S-3.2.5.* *Board note 2026-11-09 (batch 8 census): this line only points to [S-3.2.5](#s-325--calendar-connector), blocked on the same Google OAuth client. It stays open as a pointer.*
- ~~T-3.2.5 Browser history connector. *Deferred to the browser extension in S-3.7.3. There, T-3.7.3.3 is itself deferred (2026-10-19) until the user decides Echo's privacy promise.*~~ ✖ Won't do 2026-10-25: Echo only sees what the user hands it, so nothing captures browsing in the background (D-110). A browser-history export the user hands over would be an S-3.7.5 importer; not ruled on.
- [x] T-3.2.6 Apple Health connector. *Deferred: export-file import only; no web API exists. The import shipped 2026-10-19 in [#349](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/349) (S-3.7.5).* *Closed 2026-11-09 (batch 8 census): Apple Health has no web API, so there is no connector to build. The export import is the whole of it, and it shipped in #349 as `app/connectors/apple_health/importer.py` under [S-3.7.5](#s-375--export-file-importers).*

**Scope-changed 2026-10-18:** connectors ship as stories below on a widened contract with two kinds, OAuth pull connectors and export-archive importers; the ordering follows the plan's privacy-weight-first rule (cheapest, lowest-sensitivity signals first): GitHub → YouTube likes + Calendar (one Google review) → Spotify live tail (self-host only). Email, browser history and health are deferred rather than scheduled; Strava, Goodreads, Letterboxd and Apple Health are importers under S-3.7.5 because no permitted API exists.
**Acceptance.** A user connects an account from the Echo page, sees exactly what will be ingested before consenting, watches a first sync land events, sees them in a timeline, and revokes with every row from that connector deleted within the same request.

#### S-3.2.1 🟢 Connector runtime and `activity_events`
**PRs:** [#332](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/332) (storage, contracts, registries, privacy registration, the share event) and [#336](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/336) (runtime, endpoints, sync task, consent screen, timeline), both merged 2026-10-19.
**Linked decision:** [D-098](decisions.md#d-098--echo-connectors-events-unique-per-reader-tokens-in-a-secret-store-that-is-erased-with-the-brain-but-never-exported-2026-10-19)
- [x] T-3.2.1.1 *(shipped in [#332](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/332); unique on `(tenant_id, connector, external_id)` rather than `(connector, external_id)` so two readers of one account never collide, [D-098](decisions.md#d-098--echo-connectors-events-unique-per-reader-tokens-in-a-secret-store-that-is-erased-with-the-brain-but-never-exported-2026-10-19))* `activity_events(id, tenant_id, user_id, connector, event_type, occurred_at, ingested_at, payload_json, text_content, linked_document_id, embedding_id)` with index `(user_id, connector, occurred_at)`; `(connector, external_id)` unique for idempotent re-sync
- [x] T-3.2.1.2 *(shipped in [#332](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/332): `app/connectors/base.py` `ActivityConnector` with `display_name`, `consent_version` and `schedule` added, `ConnectorTokens`, `SyncContext`, `DerivedFact`, `ConnectorError(auth=)`; `echo_service` re-exports the old names)* Widen `EchoConnector` to the `personal-brain.md` contract: `name`, `privacy_weight`, `consent_disclosure`, `authorize_url`, `handle_callback`, `revoke`, `initial_backfill`, `incremental_sync`, `event_types`; connectors live under `backend/app/connectors/<name>/`
- [x] T-3.2.1.3 OAuth tokens stored through the existing Fernet credential store (`byok_service` pattern, [D-043](decisions.md#d-043--single-shared-fernet-key-for-all-encrypted-at-rest-credentials-2026-05-05)); `GET /echo/connectors/{name}/authorize`, `GET /echo/connectors/{name}/callback`, `POST /echo/connectors/{name}/sync`, `DELETE /echo/connectors/{name}` (revoke deletes events and tokens). Google connectors use a **separate OAuth client** (`OAUTH_GOOGLE_CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID/SECRET`) so a rejected sensitive-scope review never breaks sign-in. *Shipped in [#336](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/336) with two changes for a single-page app whose token is not a cookie: `POST /echo/connectors/{name}/authorize` (body `consent_version`) returns the URL rather than redirecting, and the provider returns the browser to the frontend route `/echo/connectors/{name}/callback`, which posts `code` and `state` to `POST /echo/connectors/{name}/callback` with the reader's token. The state is tenant-bound, single use and expires in ten minutes; the redirect URI is built from `APP_BASE_URL`, never taken from the client. `OAUTH_GITHUB_CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID/SECRET` and `OAUTH_GOOGLE_CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID/SECRET` exist as settings; the Google pair has no consumer until S-3.2.2 / S-3.2.5*
- [x] T-3.2.1.7 *(shipped in [#332](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/332): `ArchiveImporter` + `ImportedShare`, `registry.register_importer` / `importer_for(filename)`)* Second registry kind for export-archive importers (`name`, `accepts(filename)`, `parse(stream) -> events | shares | facts`), used by S-3.7.5; file importers never fake `authorize_url`
- [x] T-3.2.1.4 `sync_echo_connector` Celery task; scheduler kind `echo_sync` on the S-8.3.1 tick for incremental syncs. *Shipped in [#336](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/336): `echo.sync_connector` in `tasks/echo_connector_tasks.py` registers `echo_sync` (D-077) and is autodiscovered; connecting schedules the grant on the connector's own cadence and queues the backfill; `ECHO_CONNECTOR_SYNC` = `celery` | `inline` | `off` decides how connect and "Sync now" run*
- [x] T-3.2.1.5 Consent screen component showing `consent_disclosure` and the event types before the OAuth redirect. *Shipped in [#336](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/336): `ConsentDialog` on the Echo page shows the privacy weight, the disclosure and what is recorded, and sends the disclosure version it showed (a changed disclosure is a 409); a grant made under an older version shows "Review and reconnect"*
- [x] T-3.2.1.6 Tests with a fake connector: backfill, idempotent incremental, revoke cascade, isolation. *Shipped in [#336](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/336): `tests/test_routers/test_echo_connectors.py` (13: consent text and version, encrypted token, PKCE, server-built redirect, single-use reader-bound state, expired state and refused code, backfill once then incremental with the cursor, idempotent counts, provider failure recorded not raised and cleared on the next good sync, revoke deleting events, facts, tokens, states, grant and schedule with the provider down while the other reader keeps everything, timeline paging and isolation, the full delete revoking at the provider, tier gate, kind registered) and `connectors.test.tsx` (6)*
- [x] T-3.2.1.9 *(shipped in [#332](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/332): metadata only, `text_content` empty under D-089; fail-soft; a failed share writes none; deleting the share deletes it)* Each processed share writes one `activity_events` row (`connector=internal`, `event_type=shared_to_pratidhvani`) from `echo_processing_service.process_share` (moved from T-3.7.2.3)
- [x] T-3.2.1.8 *(shipped in [#332](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/332): `activity_events` and `echo_connector_grants` in `ECHO_TABLES`; `echo_connector_credentials` and `echo_connector_oauth_states` in the new `ECHO_SECRET_TABLES`, erased with the brain and never exported, the manifest's `withheld` block saying how many and why; revoking at the provider on the full delete comes with the runtime PR)* Register `activity_events` and the connector-grant table for export and erasure (`echo_privacy_service.ECHO_TABLES`, `echo_vectors.echo_collection_names()`; [D-089](decisions.md#d-089--echos-privacy-contract-context-only-by-default-the-brain-is-a-registry-the-archive-is-the-users-whole-copy-delete-in-a-fixed-order-2026-10-19) item 2). The full delete must also revoke and remove stored OAuth tokens: the first Echo credential store, which T-3.10.2.1 already names
- [x] T-3.2.1.10 *(filed and shipped 2026-10-25)* A stored token this install cannot decrypt no longer crashes the sync. Fernet `InvalidToken` (the key was rotated, or was never set, so each process made its own) or a corrupted non-ASCII row now leaves the grant `status="error"` with `last_error` starting `Reconnect to sync: ` (the named constant `RECONNECT_PREFIX`), so the connectors panel offers **Reconnect**; before, the exception escaped `run_sync` and the `echo.sync_connector` task and the grant kept looking healthy. A malformed `BYOK_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is recorded as an operator error instead, since a new token would fail the same way. The log names the connector and the tenant prefix only. No new status value, so the API and UI contract are unchanged. Tests in `test_echo_connectors.py`, including the Celery task returning `auth_error` and a patched logger showing neither ciphertext nor secret. *Shipped: 2026-10-25 — PR [#377](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/377).*
- [x] T-3.2.1.11 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #392)* A sync overwrites a connector-derived fact the user edited. Editing the derived row makes it `manual`, and the next sync writes the connector's value over it under the same key. Connector keys are namespaced (`"<Language> (GitHub)"`), so it happens only when the user edits the derived row itself. [D-110](decisions.md#d-110--echo-only-sees-what-the-user-hands-it-no-background-capture-and-the-echo-scope-rulings-2026-10-25) does not cover it
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#409](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/409) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-134](decisions.md#d-134--a-live-manual-fact-holds-its-key-against-every-source-a-401-retries-one-call-a-lease-guards-the-refresh-token-and-a-changed-row-beats-a-refresh-answer-2026-11-04). `echo_service.record_derived_context` is `record_context` for derived facts: a live `manual` row holds its key, asked per fact at write time, so an edit made during a long sync is seen. The connector sync and the export importer both use it, because the importer had the same bug. The run reports `facts_kept_manual`, with a line on the connector panel ("Kept 1 entry as you wrote it."). New column `personal_context.wanted_by` (migration `5f8caf39b5b1`) names the connector or importer that wants the key. It is set when the user edits what a source derived, and when a row typed first turns a source away, so a deleted fact still gets its tombstone. It holds the name because an `import:<id>` source does not outlive undoing that import. A row past its `expires_at` holds nothing: the connector takes the key back. Tests at the router, the worker task and the importer, with and without undoing the import.
- [x] T-3.2.1.12 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #392)* Token-refresh edge cases, to settle before a connector whose provider rotates refresh tokens ships. There is no retry after a 401 in the middle of a sync, so a token revoked or expired early asks for a reconnect. A scheduled sync and "Sync now" can both renew the token, so a rotating provider could answer the second with a spurious `invalid_grant`. Google does not rotate refresh tokens, so neither affects S-3.2.2 or S-3.2.5
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#409](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/409) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-134](decisions.md#d-134--a-live-manual-fact-holds-its-key-against-every-source-a-401-retries-one-call-a-lease-guards-the-refresh-token-and-a-changed-row-beats-a-refresh-answer-2026-11-04). `SyncContext.call(lambda token: ...)`: on a 401 it refreshes once and retries that one call once, so pages already fetched are not fetched again, and `GitHubConnector` makes its sync calls through it. A connector must opt in: a 401 raised outside `ctx.call` still asks for a reconnect. A lease on `echo_connector_credentials` (`refresh_claim`, `refresh_claimed_at`; TTL 120 s, wait 30 s) lets one reader spend a rotating refresh token while the other waits and uses its result, tested with real threads on two database connections. Only a refused refresh, a 401 on a token just issued, or a 401 with nothing to renew with asks for a reconnect. A token-endpoint outage, a claim held past the wait or a later 401 in the same sync ends the sync for the next one to retry. After the provider answers, the stored pair is compared with the pair just spent, so a reconnect during a refresh is not written over; the compare and the write are two steps, so one landing in the milliseconds between them still could be. Tokens are scrubbed, plain and URL-encoded, from logs and `last_error`, and kept out of `repr`. A process that dies between the provider's answer and the store loses a rotated token, and no client can close that gap.
- [x] T-3.2.1.13 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #409)* Two concurrent syncs can collide in `store_events`: both check and then insert, so one can fail on the unique constraint and show "Sync failed: IntegrityError" until the next sync. It heals itself and predates #409. A per-grant sync lock would close it
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#416](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/416) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-142](decisions.md#d-142--a-draft-with-nothing-behind-it-is-refused-a-register-needs-a-saved-draft-and-overlapping-syncs-are-settled-by-the-databases-insert-2026-11-05). New `database.insert_if_absent` (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) replaces the check-then-insert in `store_events` and in `record_context`, because derived facts had the same collision and closing events alone would have moved the failure one line down. Each event is counted once, and a row the user typed in the race window stands. Chosen over a lock or lease: no migration, TTL or heartbeat, and it also covers the importer and share writers. Two overlapping syncs still each call the provider. Tested with two real threads on two connections, through the worker task.
- [x] T-3.2.1.14 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #409)* The delete dialog's "will keep it deleted" line reads `source`, so it says nothing for a `manual` row that has `wanted_by`, though the row still appears under "Kept deleted" afterwards
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#416](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/416) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-142](decisions.md#d-142--a-draft-with-nothing-behind-it-is-refused-a-register-needs-a-saved-draft-and-overlapping-syncs-are-settled-by-the-databases-insert-2026-11-05). `GET` and `POST /echo/context` return `kept_deleted_by`, a display name from `connector_for_fact`, the function the delete itself uses, so the dialog and the delete cannot disagree. A manual row with `wanted_by` now says it will be kept deleted, and the raw `wanted_by` stays on the server. The revoke dialog had the same gap: it now gives a count, reading the same rows `revoke_source` takes, expired ones included.

#### S-3.2.2 ⚫ YouTube likes and subscriptions connector
**PR:** TBD
**Blocked-on (the user, 2026-10-19):** a Google OAuth client only the account owner can create and get verified. Shared with S-3.2.5 (one review covers both):
1. A Google Cloud project with the YouTube Data API v3 and the Google Calendar API enabled.
2. An OAuth consent screen (external user type): app name, support email, homepage, privacy policy URL, authorised domain.
3. A **separate** OAuth client (type "Web application") for connectors, not the sign-in one, so a scope review never touches Google sign-in. Authorised redirect URIs `<APP_BASE_URL>/echo/connectors/youtube_likes/callback` and `<APP_BASE_URL>/echo/connectors/google_calendar/callback`. Its id and secret go in `OAUTH_GOOGLE_CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID` / `_SECRET` (the settings exist since S-3.2.1).
4. Verification for the *sensitive* scopes `youtube.readonly` and `calendar.readonly`: a scope justification per scope, a demo video of the consent flow, and the privacy policy. No CASA assessment, which applies to restricted scopes only. Until verification passes, the app is capped at 100 users for its lifetime, so it must **never be offered to beta users before verification** (the L3 plan's rule).
5. Know the testing-mode limit: in "Testing" publishing status, refresh tokens expire after seven days, so nightly syncs stop after a week until the user reconnects. Verification lifts this.
**Scope-changed 2026-10-19:** a separate connector OAuth client, not the E-5.4 sign-in one (the L3 plan's rule; the board said "reusing"). The runtime (S-3.2.1) is ready: the connector only implements `app/connectors/base.py` in `app/connectors/youtube_likes/`.

- [x] T-3.2.2.5 Refresh expired access tokens: Google's last an hour. The runtime stores `refresh_token` and `expires_at` but has no refresh step yet (GitHub's tokens do not expire). Add one to the runtime or the connector, and record an `invalid_grant` refusal as "reconnect" — *shipped 2026-11-04 in the connector runtime, PR [#392](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/392) ([D-122](decisions.md#d-122--a-deleted-derived-fact-stays-deleted-per-connector-only-a-refused-refresh-asks-for-a-reconnect-restore-re-checks-the-evidence-2026-11-04)). `connectors/base.py` adds an optional `RefreshableConnector.refresh_tokens`; `run_sync` renews a token that has expired or expires within 60 s before calling the provider, stores it encrypted through `_store_tokens`, and keeps the stored refresh token when none comes back. A refusal (`invalid_grant`, `ConnectorError(auth=True)`), a missing refresh token or a connector that cannot refresh asks for a reconnect (`RECONNECT_PREFIX`); a transient failure is an error the next sync retries. Tested with a fake connector at the router and the Celery task, mutation-checked. This connector still has to implement `refresh_tokens`; S-3.2.2 stays ⚫.*

#### S-3.2.4 🟢 GitHub connector
**PR:** [#342](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/342) (merged 2026-10-19)
**Linked decision:** [D-110](decisions.md#d-110--echo-only-sees-what-the-user-hands-it-no-background-capture-and-the-echo-scope-rulings-2026-10-25) (T-3.2.4.3 won't-do, T-3.2.4.4 accepted); [D-122](decisions.md#d-122--a-deleted-derived-fact-stays-deleted-per-connector-only-a-refused-refresh-asks-for-a-reconnect-restore-re-checks-the-evidence-2026-11-04) (T-3.2.4.4 as built)
**Scope-changed 2026-10-19:** two API facts changed the shape. (1) GitHub OAuth apps have no "events read" scope: `read:user` reaches **public** activity only, and private activity would need `repo`, full read-write access to code, which Echo should not ask for. The consent text says public only. (2) Since GitHub's [2025-08 Events API change](https://github.blog/changelog/2025-08-08-upcoming-changes-to-github-events-api-payloads/), push events carry no commit list. A push is recorded as `pushed`, and commit messages come from `GET /repos/{repo}/commits?author=<login>`, which also guarantees they are the user's own words.
- [x] T-3.2.4.1 OAuth with `read:user` and PKCE, using `OAUTH_GITHUB_CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID/SECRET` or falling back to the sign-in app (its callback URL must then be the frontend origin so both redirects fit under it). Events kept: `pushed`, `committed`, `opened_pull_request`, `merged_pull_request`, `reviewed_pull_request`, `opened_issue`, `commented`, `published_release`, `created_repository`, `starred`, `forked`. Text is kept only where the user wrote it: commit messages, PR, issue, review, comment and release text, repository descriptions. Branch names, star targets and other people's words never reach `text_content`. The backfill reads the feed (three pages, its 300-event ceiling), the user's own repositories, and up to 200 commits from each of the 20 most recently pushed non-fork repositories, about 50 requests. A nightly sync (`daily` at 04:00 UTC) reads the feed back to the last seen event, sending `If-None-Match` so an unchanged feed costs nothing against the 5,000-per-hour limit, and walks commits since the last sync for repositories it saw pushes to. A 401 asks the user to reconnect; a spent rate limit just waits for the next sync. Revoke calls `DELETE /applications/{client_id}/grant`
- [x] T-3.2.4.2 Derived `skill` facts from the languages of own non-fork repositories, keyed `"<Language> (GitHub)"` so a sync never overwrites a skill the user wrote, valued as a sentence, confidence 0.4 + 0.1 per extra repository, capped at 0.8
- Tests: `tests/test_services/test_github_connector.py` (10) on synthetic responses in GitHub's REST shapes. They cover registration and contract, the client fallback, PKCE, the code exchange, revoke, the backfill's mapping and text rules, a repository recorded once, forks not walked, empty repositories skipped, language facts, a conditional request on an unchanged feed, an incremental stop at the last event with commits walked since the last sync, 401 versus rate limit, and connect, sync and disconnect end to end through the API. Live: the Echo page with a seeded GitHub grant (panel, timeline, disconnect confirmation); a real GitHub OAuth app was not available here
- ~~T-3.2.4.3 *(proposed 2026-10-19, the user's call)* Opt-in private activity. Reading private repositories needs the `repo` scope, which is full read-write access to code. Shipped public-only; if wanted, ask for it as a separate, clearly worded second consent with its own `consent_version`, never folded into the default~~ ✖ Won't do 2026-10-25: the connector stays public-only, since `repo` grants write access to code (D-110).
- [x] T-3.2.4.4 🟢 *(proposed 2026-10-19, the user's call)* Deleted derived facts stay deleted. A nightly sync re-creates a skill the user forgot (upsert by kind and key, so "Python (GitHub)" comes back). Record a tombstone per (source, kind, key) on delete and have `echo_connector_service._store_facts` skip tombstoned keys. The same applies to every connector's derived facts. *Accepted 2026-10-25 (D-110): a fact the user deleted stays deleted, for every connector.*
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#392](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/392) (batch 3 of 2026-11-03). Linked decision: [D-122](decisions.md#d-122--a-deleted-derived-fact-stays-deleted-per-connector-only-a-refused-refresh-asks-for-a-reconnect-restore-re-checks-the-evidence-2026-11-04). New table `echo_fact_tombstones` (migration `a68953e57c63`), keyed on the connector or importer name, the kind and the key, so a re-import of the same kind of export is covered as well as a re-sync. `echo_service.delete_context` and `revoke_source` write one before deleting a fact that a connector (`connector:<name>`) or an importer (`import:<id>`) derived; a fact the user typed gets none, and disconnect, import undo and share delete record none. `echo_connector_service._store_facts` and `echo_import_service.run_import` skip tombstoned keys (`last_counts.facts` counts only the facts written; an import reports `facts_kept_deleted`). `GET` and `DELETE /echo/fact-tombstones[/{id}]` are Studio-gated and tenant-scoped. The Echo page shows "Kept deleted (N)" with "Allow again", and the delete and revoke dialogs say the fact stays deleted. The table is in `ECHO_TABLES`, so it is exported and erased with the brain. Tests in `test_echo_connectors.py`, `test_echo_event_imports.py` and `test_echo_privacy.py`, mutation-checked. Left out: a "clear all" for tombstones. *2026-11-10 (batch 14): a derived fact whose key holds `/` is now deleted by its route, its tombstone keeps the whole key, and the next sync skips it (T-3.1.1.1, PR [#462](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/462)).*

### E-3.3 🟢 Voice & personality capture (Domain 3)

**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — S-3.3.1 [#333](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/333), S-3.3.2 [#335](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/335), S-3.3.3 [#343](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/343), S-3.3.4 [#346](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/346), with decisions [D-099](decisions.md#d-099--signal-extraction-verbatim-checked-examples-language-free-beliefs-stylometry-without-a-model-2026-10-19) and [D-100](decisions.md#d-100--voice-match-gate-mode-a-passes-on-synthetic-writers-speak-as-me-may-be-built-and-is-enabled-per-user-only-after-the-user-as-judge-test-2026-10-19).
- The acceptance's last clause, the agent retrieving these signals, belongs to E-3.4.
- The harness has shown that clause on synthetic writers.
- Open follow-up tasks: T-3.3.2.7, T-3.3.2.9, T-3.3.2.11 and T-3.3.4.3, which is the user-as-judge run. T-3.3.1.6 shipped 2026-11-04 in PR [#392](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/392). T-3.3.1.7 shipped 2026-11-04 in PR [#409](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/409).

**Scope.** Store user writing samples, Q&A patterns, opinion threads. Train fine-tune adapters on user-tagged content for persona.

**Scope-changed 2026-10-18.** Mode A only in this epic: `voice_signals` as inspectable, editable, exportable, revocable rows, extracted by an LLM pass over the user's own text (notes from E-8.5, questions, journal entries, shares, outgoing messages from connectors), embedded with the personal facts for retrieval. Fine-tuning moves to E-3.9.
**Acceptance.** After a user has written twenty notes and asked fifty questions, the Echo page shows a signals panel listing style and personality signals with the verbatim examples they were learned from; the user deletes one and it never returns; the user exports the profile as JSON; the "speak as me" agent (E-3.4) retrieves these signals and a blind judge prefers drafts with them over drafts without.

#### S-3.3.1 🟢 `voice_signals` schema, CRUD and the signals panel
**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — PR [#333](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/333)

The panel has no undo for "That's not me": re-adding a rejected signal is refused with a 422 that says so, and the profile export lists rejections under `rejected`. A "Rejected (N)" disclosure with **Restore** would clear `suppressed_at` for `suppressed_reason='user'` rows — *shipped 2026-11-04, PR [#392](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/392) ([D-122](decisions.md#d-122--a-deleted-derived-fact-stays-deleted-per-connector-only-a-refused-refresh-asks-for-a-reconnect-restore-re-checks-the-evidence-2026-11-04)). `GET /echo/signals/rejected` (sent without examples) and `POST /echo/signals/{id}/restore`; the signals panel has "Rejected (N)" with Restore. Withdrawing a source now cleans rejected signals too, and they stay rejected. Restore re-checks the evidence against the live inputs (`live_evidence_texts`) and drops what is gone or edited away; a learned signal with none left comes back `source_deleted`. Restoring one rejection lifts every rejection of the same type, wording and language, merging their checked evidence; an equal live signal is kept and the tombstones dropped. Tests in `test_echo_signals.py` (+10) and `echoSignals.test.tsx`, mutation-checked.*
- [x] T-3.3.1.7 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #392)* Sweep stale examples from rejected signals at rest. A signal rejected before #392 can still hold the words of a source deleted while it was rejected, because the old withdrawal skipped rejected rows. Restore re-checks them and the Rejected list sends no examples, so they never come back, but the row keeps them and the full brain export carries every column. A one-off pass applying `live_evidence_texts` to `suppressed_reason='user'` rows would clear them
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#409](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/409) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-134](decisions.md#d-134--a-live-manual-fact-holds-its-key-against-every-source-a-401-retries-one-call-a-lease-guards-the-refresh-token-and-a-changed-row-beats-a-refresh-answer-2026-11-04). `scripts/sweep_rejected_signal_evidence.py`: a dry run by default, `--write` to apply, `--tenant` to narrow, idempotent, printing counts and never the words. It applies restore's own check, and a rejection stays a rejection. **Dry run on the owner's database, read-only: 0 rejections and 0 stale,** so there is nothing to clear on this install today. An audit of every read of rejected rows found one leak: the full brain archive carried every column. `GET /echo/export` now carries only the evidence that still stands, counted in `manifest.withheld.columns.voice_signals`, so the words cannot leak before the sweep runs. That is a second counted exception to the archive's lossless round trip, beside the open voice test.

**As built.** `voice_signals` adds `value_key` (casefolded, whitespace-collapsed, quotes and full stops trimmed), the merge and tombstone key, and `origin` ∈ {user, llm, stylometry}. `suppressed_reason` tells the user's rejection (`user`, permanent) from a signal whose every source was deleted (`source_deleted`, which new evidence may re-derive). `echo_signal_service` owns the closed vocabularies (the schema `Literal`s are pinned to them by a test), `is_suppressed` (the check extraction calls before writing), and `withdraw_source(tenant, "share:<id>" | "note:<id>", signal_ids?)`, which drops a source's examples and provenance and is what `delete_share` calls. A separate router, `routers/echo_signals.py`, keeps this work clear of the intake and connector routes in `routers/echo.py`. `consent_version` is `2026-10-19`, stamped on every row.

#### S-3.3.2 🟢 Signal extraction
**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — PR [#335](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/335). Linked decision: [D-099](decisions.md#d-099--signal-extraction-verbatim-checked-examples-language-free-beliefs-stylometry-without-a-model-2026-10-19). Cost: [measurement note](notes/2026-10-19-signal-extraction-cost.md).

  - Each connector's events with text are one input (`events:<connector>`), sampled like an import. The internal share connector is excluded, since shares are inputs already.
  - Examples cite `event:<id>`.
  - Search queries (`searched_*`) are read by the model but are not prose for stylometry (2-word floor).
  - Events are deleted in bulk outside extraction, so `withdraw_missing_events` takes their words out of every signal in one pass (`echo_signal_service.withdraw_sources`). It runs inside `delete_connector_events`, in `delete_import` (import undo), and at the start of every run.
  - Connector `revoke` now syncs `personal_context_global` after its commit. Revoked facts kept their vectors before, the gap lane D found for Apple Health

  - Stylometry profiles every own-words turn.
  - The model reads at most `ECHO_SIGNALS_IMPORT_SAMPLE` (300) turns of at least `ECHO_SIGNALS_MIN_PASSAGE_WORDS` (4) words: the longest in each equal slice of the chat's timeline.
  - Examples cite the turns (`share:<id>`), so undoing the import through `delete_shares` withdraws them.
  - The cheap trigger counts an import once.
  - Measured on a 130,000-turn chat: before, 65,000 inputs, 65,000 ledger rows (36.7 MB of profiles), 27 s per run without the model and about 80 runs to drain; after, 1 input, 1 ledger row (4.4 KB), 3.8 s, and 300 turns read in about 8 calls.

  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#418](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/418) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-144](decisions.md#d-144--a-fifth-voice-label-other-names-no-language-to-write-in-a-job-fills-gaps-and-only-the-backfill-rewrites-and-the-declared-audio-language-is-a-key-on-the-document-2026-11-05). `language_of` reads `hi_share × named_share`, and a text no label fits reads the new fifth label, `other`, added to `LANGUAGES`, the `Language` literal, the signals panel and the API reference. Draft languages stay four, and the tagger and lexicons are unchanged. **Contrary to this entry, the label does reach prompts,** so the voice test gained a wording for `other`, an amendment to its procedure (T-3.4.1.16). Bengali, Tamil and Urdu notes also read `other` now, beyond the brief. Stored labels stay until their text is edited; the live install holds no such rows.

**As built.** `echo_signal_extraction.py`: `gather_inputs` (own words only), `pending` (new, changed, deleted or retry against the `voice_signal_inputs` ledger), `run` (withdraw first, then stylometry profiles, then batched calls of about 6,000 tokens, then merge, then the cadence sync), and `maybe_queue` (fail-soft; `ECHO_SIGNAL_EXTRACTION` = celery, inline or off; queues at `ECHO_SIGNALS_MIN_NEW_INPUTS` = 10). `echo_stylometry.py` is model-free. Inputs deviated from T-3.3.2.3: a context-only share's annotation is not an input (D-089 is strict). Measured on three synthetic writers: about $0.005 per 100 inputs backlog, $0.02 per 100 incremental, no failed calls, no duplicate rows after the language fix.

#### S-3.3.3 🟢 Personal retrieval collection
**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — PR [#343](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/343)
- [x] T-3.3.3.1 `personal_context_global` Chroma collection (same embedder) holding facts and signals with `tenant_id` metadata; `query_personal_context(tenant_id, text)` with the fail-closed tenant filter the Q&A collection uses
- [x] T-3.3.3.2 Upsert on write, delete on tombstone, backfill on worker start (the `backfill_qa_library` pattern)
- [x] T-3.3.3.3 Tests: isolation; tombstoned signals absent
- [x] T-3.3.3.4 Write vectors with `tenant_id` metadata only into `CHROMA_PERSONAL_CONTEXT_COLLECTION_NAME`, which the full delete already sweeps; add a `test_echo_privacy` case that a fact's vector is gone after `DELETE /echo/brain`

**As built.** `echo_personal_vectors.py`. One vector per live fact (`fact:<id>`) and per live signal (`signal:<id>`), with `tenant_id`, `kind`, `row_id` and, for signals, `signal_type`, `origin`, `weight`, `language` and `register` metadata. **A sync, not per-write bookkeeping:** `sync_tenant` compares the live rows with the stored vectors through a content hash in each vector's metadata, embeds only what is new or changed, and deletes vectors whose row is gone, expired, rejected or suppressed. Every write path calls it after its commit: fact record, delete and revoke; signal create, edit and reject; an extraction run; and a note or share delete. The startup backfill (`backfill`, the `note_index` pattern) runs it for every tenant with rows or vectors. Fail-soft at the callers. `query_personal_context(tenant_id, text, n, kinds, signal_types)` refuses an empty tenant and filters on it, with a defence-in-depth check on every hit.

#### S-3.3.4 🟢 Voice-match evaluation harness
**Pre-registered 2026-10-19:** [docs/notes/2026-10-19-voice-match-preregistration.md](notes/2026-10-19-voice-match-preregistration.md) fixes the material (three synthetic writers, 18 prompts in `backend/tests/fixtures/echo_voice/draft_prompts.json`), the two conditions, the judges (claude-sonnet-5 and gemini-3.6-flash, the latter declared as the substitute for Gemini 3.1 Pro) and the pass rule, before any draft is generated. Tier (iii), the user as judge, is fixed too and waits for 40 of the user's own notes.
**PR:** TBD. The gate for E-3.4; the [D-055](decisions.md#d-055--intelligence-delta-evaluation-methodology-production-input-replay--blind-ab-judging-2026-07-29) method applied to voice.
- [x] T-3.3.4.1 Three tiers, per the plan: (i) an automatic authorship-verification score against the user's held-out texts per language, (ii) an LLM judge that may abstain, used only for the in-UI voice-match number, (iii) a blinded "which of these did you write?" pairwise test with the user as judge, the only credible product gate
- [x] T-3.3.4.2 Pre-registered pass rule before the first run; a quality guard (preference against a neutral draft) so a style win never ships unreadable text
- [ ] T-3.3.4.3 Run tier (iii) for the user once they have 40 live notes in a language: `scripts/voice_match_harness.py user-pairs` on a copy of the database, then `user-score` on their answers. This is the per-user switch for E-3.4 *2026-11-04: with T-3.4.1.8 this runs from the Echo page, with no database copy and no operator, once the install switch is on and the user has 40 notes and readiness. Nobody has run it yet.*

**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — PR [#346](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/346). Linked decision: [D-100](decisions.md#d-100--voice-match-gate-mode-a-passes-on-synthetic-writers-speak-as-me-may-be-built-and-is-enabled-per-user-only-after-the-user-as-judge-test-2026-10-19). Result: [docs/notes/2026-10-19-voice-match-result.md](notes/2026-10-19-voice-match-result.md). **The pre-registered gate passed on three synthetic writers**:
- Voice: V preferred in 36 of 36 verdicts, by both judges for all three writers, with no abstentions.
- Quality guard: 47% (17 V, 17 N, 2 unparsed).
- Tier (i): 15 of 18.
- Cost: $0.31.

It is a ceiling, not a margin. E-3.4 may be built; it is enabled per user only after tier (iii).

### E-3.4 🟢 "Speak as me" agent (Domain 4)

**Scope.** Given an incoming message, draft a response in the user's voice using their accumulated knowledge + context. Privacy: self-host local; SaaS opt-in encrypted. Will use `echo_service.is_ready(user)` as the cold-start gate before activating.

**Scope-changed 2026-10-18.** Pipeline fixed as `classify_intent → retrieve_relevant_knowledge → retrieve_voice_signals → retrieve_personal_context → draft → self_critique_against_voice → finalize`; drafts only, never sends; every draft carries citations, a voice-match score, deviation notes and a disclosure footer.
**Acceptance.** A ready user pastes an incoming message, gets a draft with citations to sources, facts and past Q&As, a voice-match score and deviation notes; editing the draft records the diff as a new signal; a not-ready user gets the readiness meter instead; the daily draft cap holds.

**Shipped:** 2026-10-20 — PR [#357](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/357) (S-3.4.1) and PR [#359](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/359) (S-3.4.2). Built and switched off. It turns on per user once the install switch is on and their voice test passes (T-3.3.4.3); on 2026-10-20 that was nobody. Open follow-ups: T-3.4.1.9 (calibrating the critic) and T-3.4.1.10. T-3.4.1.8 (an in-app voice test) shipped 2026-11-04.

  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#416](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/416) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Both rules are built. A named register needs an earlier saved draft in it: a first draft stops with `409 register_needs_approval` before any model call, and `acknowledge_register` is the go-ahead for that one draft. A question, opinion or recommendation with nothing behind it is refused with `409 ungrounded`, in place of D-102's flag, and so is a message the classifier could not read. It stops before the draft model when retrieval found nothing usable, and at finalize when the draft cites nothing usable after a revise round. Mail grounds nothing. Facts, shares and past answers now meet `RAG_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD` in the retrievers, without which the early stop could not fire for a ready user. The Echo page shows both stops in the server's words, and `docs/personal-brain.md` is updated. No real model ran.
