The filter D-151 put on `uvicorn.access`, `log_redaction._UrlCredentialFilter`, also runs `redact_query_words`, which writes the value of every parameter in `WORDS_QUERY_NAMES` by its length (`q=[41 characters]`); the path, the other parameters and the status stay. Redacted: `q` (`GET /search`, `/notes`, `/echo/shares`, `/author/knowledge-sources`), `search` (`GET /library/videos`, `/shelves/{shelf_id}/documents`) and `focus` (`GET /library/graph`, where a topic node's id is the topic's label). A value is counted as the route reads it: percent-decoded with `+` as a space, running to the next `&`, each repeat by its own length, and a name matches percent-encoded or in any case, so a crafted URL hides more, never less. All 93 query parameters on 38 paths were read from FastAPI's own dependants; the other 86 (ids, enums, numbers, dates, flags, the fixed `confirm` phrase, the OAuth `redirect_uri` and `state`, and `code`, already redacted) are kept by route with a reason in `tests/test_utils/test_access_log_words.py`, whose guard fails on a parameter in neither list. Tested through the real filter with uvicorn's own `AccessFormatter` and the app's `JsonFormatter`, 16 encoding cases, and the real `app.main:app` under the real uvicorn CLI, whose log held none of the words; 14 hand mutations each failed. The full backend suite passed 6,031. Left out: a user's words in a path segment (T-4.11.4.8). **Owner step:** restart the API; `.uvicorn.out.log` holds the words written before until then.
- [ ] T-4.11.4.6 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #447; overlaps OQ-45)* An exception no route handles reaches uvicorn's "Exception in ASGI application" line whole, and a database error there quotes its values: a note, a question, a share. `run_import`'s own `EchoImport` insert and commit sit outside its `try`, so a "database is locked" there would quote the file name and `options_json`, whose `self_name` is a participant's name. Closing it is OQ-45's `hide_parameters`, or a filter on `uvicorn.error` that writes such a traceback by its frames, which would also drop every unhandled error's useful words
  **Shipped (the `run_import` part):** 2026-11-10 — PR [#454](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/454) (batch 12 of 2026-11-10). Linked decision: [D-168](decisions.md#d-168--the-access-log-writes-a-users-words-by-their-length-chosen-by-parameter-name-in-one-filter-a-fail-soft-handler-rolls-back-before-it-reads-and-an-imports-own-row-fails-as-importstopped-2026-11-10). The import's `EchoImport` insert and commit sit in their own `try`; a failure there is logged by `loggable_filename` and `traceback_without_text` and raised as `ImportStopped("Import could not start: <type>")`, which `POST /echo/import` answers as a 500 with those words, writing no row. The tombstone read moved inside the run's `try`. Tested through the route with a chat named after one participant and `self_name` naming the other: neither name reaches a root handler that renders tracebacks, and with the call site back on the whole traceback both do. `docs/api-reference.md` says what the route answers. *2026-11-10 (batch 12 sorting): the general part, a filter on `uvicorn.error` or `hide_parameters`, stays with OQ-45, the owner's.*
- [x] T-4.11.4.7 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #447)* Worker-only lines the API never runs in a request: `newsletter_service` (line 265) and `saved_search_service` (lines 368 and 537) use `logger.exception` around writes that carry a saved search's question or an issue's subject. [D-160](decisions.md#d-160--a-users-words-leave-the-workers-log-at-the-call-site-an-exception-that-can-quote-a-request-is-logged-by-its-frames-and-a-connectors-search-counts-as-a-shared-link-2026-11-09)'s territory, which #442 did not reach
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#454](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/454) (batch 12 of 2026-11-10). Linked decision: [D-168](decisions.md#d-168--the-access-log-writes-a-users-words-by-their-length-chosen-by-parameter-name-in-one-filter-a-fail-soft-handler-rolls-back-before-it-reads-and-an-imports-own-row-fails-as-importstopped-2026-11-10). Four sites go to `logger.error` with `traceback_without_text`: `newsletter_service.deliver`'s notification write, `saved_search_service._notify`'s notification write, `saved_search_service.execute`'s run handler, whose words stay on `last_error`, and the scheduler tick's inbox sync, where a database error would quote `ECHO_IMAP_TENANT_EMAIL`. Every warning, error and exception call in code only a task runs was read against #442's and #447's kept lists, and the PR names each kept line and why. **Found on the way:** both notification handlers read their row after the failed write had expired it, so the handler itself raised `PendingRollbackError`: `deliver` raised out of the task after the email had gone, and a saved search's run already committed as a success was recorded as failed. Both now roll back first and log an id read before the write; `# pragma: no cover` had hidden both. Tested by refusing one statement as SQLite does under another writer's lock, so SQLAlchemy's own `OperationalError` quotes the values: a patched logger at each site sees no word, a mutant on the whole traceback sees them, and the outcomes (`sent`, `ok`, `failed` with the words on `last_error`) are asserted. **Owner step:** restart the worker. Left out: `newsletter_service.deliver`'s other writes, whose database errors reach Celery's own failure line (OQ-45).
- [x] T-4.11.4.8 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #454)* `DELETE /api/v1/echo/context/{kind}/{key}` puts a fact's key, which the user can write, in the URL path, and the access log keeps paths whole, so it writes the key. #454's filter covers query parameters only
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#459](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/459) (batch 13 of 2026-11-10), with T-4.11.4.9. Linked decision: [D-171](decisions.md#d-171--a-users-words-in-a-url-path-are-chosen-by-route-template-in-the-same-filter-a-path-no-template-matches-is-written-whole-and-the-import-script-answers-a-stop-on-one-line-and-names-what-a-part-way-stop-kept-2026-11-10). The one filter on `uvicorn.access`, `log_redaction._UrlCredentialFilter`, also runs `redact_path_words`: it matches each path against the route templates in `log_redaction.WORDS_PATH_PARAMS` and writes a words segment by its length, as `"DELETE /api/v1/echo/context/interest/[25 characters] HTTP/1.1" 401`. The method, the other segments, the query rules and the status stay. All 74 path parameters on the 69 templated routes were read: only `key` on `/api/v1/echo/context/{kind}/{key}` holds what a user typed; the other 73 are ids, a PDF's digest, a session's `jti`, a share token's row id, YouTube ids, or names from a fixed set (a provider, a use case, a connector's name, the context `kind`, and `/echo/sources/{source}`, which is `manual`, a connector's name or `import:<id>`). A kept segment matches as Starlette matches it, `[^/]+`, and only a words value may run over a decoded `/`; the length is counted with `unquote`, not `unquote_plus`; literals match in any case and percent-encoded. A path no template matches is written whole, since an operator reading a 404 needs it as it came. The templates are listed in `log_redaction` because FastAPI 0.162's route table yields no route to a runtime walk, and the guard (`test_access_log_words.py`) holds them to the real routes: it reads every route's template, not the handler's arguments, and fails on a path parameter in neither `WORDS_PATH_PARAMS` nor its kept list, on a stale entry and on one in both; a fixture-app test proves it sees a new parameter, a segment the handler never names included. `test_url_tokens._served_routes` takes an optional app for that. Tested through the real filter with uvicorn's `AccessFormatter` and the app's `JsonFormatter` (six keys, Devanagari, `/`, `?`, `#`, `%` and `+` among them), 20 template cases, and the real `app.main:app` under the real uvicorn CLI, whose log held none of the keys; the mutation checks are in the PR. CLAUDE.md's access-log convention gains a sentence. **Owner step:** restart the API; `.uvicorn.out.log` keeps any fact key written before until then. **Found on the way:** a fact whose key holds `/` cannot be deleted ([S-3.1.1](#s-311--deleting-a-fact-whose-key-holds-)). Left out: Vite's dev proxy (T-4.11.4.10).
- [x] T-4.11.4.9 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #454)* `scripts/echo_import.py` does not catch `ImportStopped`, so a failed run prints its traceback to the operator's own terminal. Not a log, and the CLI is not the route; a one-line message would match the route's 500
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#459](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/459) (batch 13 of 2026-11-10), with T-4.11.4.8. Linked decision: [D-171](decisions.md#d-171--a-users-words-in-a-url-path-are-chosen-by-route-template-in-the-same-filter-a-path-no-template-matches-is-written-whole-and-the-import-script-answers-a-stop-on-one-line-and-names-what-a-part-way-stop-kept-2026-11-10). The script catches `ImportStopped` from the preview and the run, prints on one stderr line the words `POST /echo/import` answers its 500 with (`Preview stopped: ValueError`, `Import could not start: OperationalError`, `Import stopped: OperationalError`) and exits 1; before, the traceback's cause could quote the file, such as a Goodreads rating cell holding words. **Review fixes:** the service's own line of the stop, its frames, still reached the terminal through the app's stderr handler, so `echo_import_service` marks it (`is_stop_log`) and the script filters it from that logger for its own run only; the API's log keeps it, and the service's other lines still show. And a run that stops part-way keeps the shares, events and facts it committed under a failed import, so `ImportStopped` carries `import_id`, `run_import` sets it on an `ExportUnreadable` raised mid-run too, and the script prints a second line naming the import and what it holds, counted from the rows by the new `echo_import_service.stored_by` (which refuses another tenant's import) rather than from `counts_json`, which also counts a rolled-back batch, and says to undo it under Your imports. The docstring lists the exit codes. Every other script that calls a service with a typed stop was read, and none needed the same catch. Tested in `tests/test_scripts/test_echo_import.py` (new): each way an import stops, in process, with stderr pinned line by line, and the real command on a database of its own; the mutation checks are in the PR. The unused `Output` import is gone from `newsletter_service.py`. Left out: the CLI no longer shows where a stopped import failed, which the API's log does; a `--verbose` flag could bring the frames back if the owner wants them.
- [x] T-4.11.4.10 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #459)* Under `scripts/start.ps1` the browser reaches the API through Vite's dev proxy, and when the backend is down or restarting Vite 8.0.3 logs `http proxy error: <req.url>` to `.frontend.*.log`, so a fact key or `?q=` search words sent then land there, percent-encoded. A `configure(proxy)` hook with its own `'error'` listener would not stop that line, since Vite registers its own listener after `configure` and both fire. A `customLogger` in `vite.config.ts` that rewrites the line would, which needs a choice of what to keep: leaving the path out is simplest, since the stack already says the backend refused
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#461](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/461) (batch 14 of 2026-11-10). Linked decision: [D-173](decisions.md#d-173--the-dev-proxys-failed-request-line-names-its-method-and-leaves-the-path-out-through-a-customlogger-that-rewrites-that-line-alone-and-a-stack-loses-only-the-url-vite-was-given-2026-11-10). `frontend/vite.config.ts` sets `customLogger: withoutProxyPaths(createLogger())`, and the logic sits beside it in `frontend/viteProxyLog.ts`: the one line `http proxy error: <url>` becomes `http proxy error: DELETE [path left out]`, keeping the colour codes, the timestamp and the stack, which says how the backend failed (`ECONNREFUSED`, `write ECONNRESET`, `socket hang up`). Vite's line carries only the URL, so a `configure` hook on `/api` (`noteFailedRequestMethod`) records each failed request's method in a `WeakMap` keyed by the error object Vite's own listener then logs; with none recorded the line reads `http proxy error: [path left out]`. A stack that ever holds the URL Vite was given, as given or decoded, loses it too, never a bare `/`. Every other line passes through unchanged with its options, nothing is dropped, and `hasWarned`, `hasErrorLogged` and `clearScreen` are forwarded. **Audit:** of every line Vite 8.0.3's proxy writes, only that one holds a URL; the three `ws proxy` lines and the Origin-header warning name none. The `DEBUG=vite:proxy`, `vite:time` and `http-proxy-3` debug lines write the URL straight to stderr, out of any logger's reach, and are off unless `DEBUG` names them. Tested by `frontend/viteProxyLog.test.ts` (new, 28 tests), up to the production caller: a real Vite dev server built by `createServer` from `vite.config.ts` against a closed port printed both methods and `ECONNREFUSED` and none of the words, while the same server with Vite's plain logger printed both URLs whole. 11 hand mutations each failed. A real check on a spare port, against a closed port, a backend that resets, one that hangs up and a `proxyTimeout`, printed no word and no `/api/v1`; it ran with a scratch `cacheDir`, since `npm run dev` from a worktree would rewrite the shared `node_modules/.vite`. `vitest.config.ts` and `tsconfig.node.json` include the module, and CLAUDE.md's access-log convention gains a sentence. **Consequence:** Vite skips its own `logLevel` and `clearScreen` handling for a custom logger, so `--logLevel` and `--clearScreen` on the command line no longer reach it; nothing in the repo passes either. **Owner step:** none; Vite restarts its dev server when the config changes, and `.frontend.err.log` keeps the lines written before. **Found on the way:** T-4.11.4.11.
- [ ] T-4.11.4.11 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #461; the owner's call)* When Vite 8 detects an agent in its environment (`CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE` and others), it relays the browser's `console.error` and `console.warn` calls and its unhandled errors to the dev server's log (`server.forwardConsole`), so services started from an agent session write them to `.frontend.*.log`. The app makes no console calls of its own, so only library warnings and unhandled errors would land there. `server.forwardConsole: false` in `vite.config.ts` turns it off

**Open questions**
- OQ-44 (from T-4.11.4.1): with `SMTP_HOST` unset, the log fallback writes every email whole, not only the password reset: a newsletter issue sent with **Send now**, from the API, and saved-search alerts, which hold the user's question, from the worker, where they were already written. Should an email that needs no hand-over log a one-line stub instead? #437 keeps the documented behaviour. The owner's call.
- OQ-45 (from T-4.11.4.2): should a database error's text hide its bound values when `DEBUG` is off? `hide_parameters=not settings.DEBUG` on the engine in `backend/app/database.py` is one line, and `DEBUG=true` would behave as now. With `DEBUG` off, a failed write's values, such as a share's text, a note or a signal example on a "database is locked", would then reach no traceback, Celery failure line or stored `error_message` or `last_error`. #442 had it and took it out on review, because the brief kept `DEBUG`'s behaviour; adopting it means updating `test_debug_echo_writes_each_statements_values_and_nothing_else_follows_debug`. The owner's call. *2026-11-09 (batch 10's live check): the only SQL echo in the worker's log is the owner's `DEBUG=true`, and at idle its parameters held no user's words. T-4.11.4.6 is the same question from the API's side.* *2026-11-10 (batch 12): T-4.11.4.6's general part, a filter on `uvicorn.error` or this `hide_parameters`, stays here, and #454 adds a case: `newsletter_service.deliver`'s other writes, such as the issue's output row titled with the reader's title, are not caught, so a database error there reaches Celery's own "raised unexpected" line with its values.*

The heading's "fully closed 2026-05-05" refers to the original scope.

**Why it exists.** Today's PRs must remain forward-compatible with a future public SaaS — multi-tenant, billed, abuse-resistant, hardened auth.
**North-star doc:** [saas-roadmap.md](saas-roadmap.md)

**Status (2026-05-05).** **All code-shippable I-5 work has now landed.** The remaining ⚪ items are correctly deferred to SaaS launch — none has a self-host code path that buys anything today.

- ✅ **E-5.1** Tenancy retrofit — fully closed (4 phases + operator NOT NULL runbook).
- ✅ **E-5.4** Auth hardening — fully closed. 8 tasks across PRs [#156](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/156) / [#163](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/163) / [#164](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/164) / [#165](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/165) / [#166](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/166).
- ✅ **E-5.2** Subscription tiers — fully closed. Schema (#155) + utility (#155) + quota enforcement at hot endpoints (#169) shipped. T-5.2.4 (wire `require_tier` into actual endpoints) is **partial-permanent** — `require_feature("byok_llm_keys")` already gates the BYOK router; future Author Studio (I-6) and Echo (I-3) endpoints will gate when those land.
- ✅ **E-5.5** Abuse prevention — code-shippable parts closed. Rate-limit middleware (#157) + quota metering enforcement (#169) shipped. Remaining: T-5.5.4 Redis backend (🔴 per [D-039](decisions.md#d-039--in-memory-rate-limit-backend-as-the-default-redis-swap-deferred-to-multi-worker-saas-2026-05-04) — gated on multi-worker SaaS), T-5.5.6 content policy / takedown (🔴 — needs M11 public sharing first), T-5.5.7 fraud detection (🔴 — needs production traffic + abuse signal data first).
- ✅ **E-5.6** Background-job isolation — code-shippable parts closed. BYOK foundation (#158), BYOK LLM resolution-path (#162 via ContextVar / [D-041](decisions.md#d-041--contextvar-plumbing-vs-explicit-kwargs-for-cross-cutting-per-user-state-2026-05-05)), Chroma tenant filtering (#161), per-tenant Celery routing (#170). All shipped.

**Remaining design-complete, code-deferred to SaaS launch:**
- **E-5.3** Stripe — pure SaaS; no value for self-host (every user already at "Studio" effectively).
- **E-5.7** Data residency — single-machine install has data in one place by definition.
- **E-5.8** Hosting / infra — operations work (Postgres / Redis Cluster / S3 / CDN provisioning), executed at SaaS launch.
- **E-5.9** Hosted UX — gated on E-5.3.
- **T-5.1.3** Multi-user-per-workspace (`tenants` + `tenant_users` tables) — Team SaaS tier only.
- **T-5.5.4** Redis-backed rate-limit bucket store — gated on multi-worker SaaS deployment per D-039.
- **T-5.5.6** Content policy + takedown workflow — gated on M11 public report sharing.
- **T-5.5.7** Fraud detection / anomalous-pattern alerting — gated on real production traffic.

Full design for each in [`saas-roadmap.md`](saas-roadmap.md); each epic's entry below cross-links the relevant section.

**Decisions captured 2026-05-04 / 2026-05-05:**
- [D-038](decisions.md#d-038--tenancy-retrofit-ships-in-four-phases-audit--additive--backfillwrites--reads--not-null-2026-05-04) — Tenancy retrofit ships in four phases (E-5.1).
- [D-039](decisions.md#d-039--in-memory-rate-limit-backend-as-the-default-redis-swap-deferred-to-multi-worker-saas-2026-05-04) — In-memory rate-limit backend as the default; Redis-swap deferred.
- [D-040](decisions.md#d-040--failed-logins-for-unknown-emails-do-not-create-user-rows-lock-arbitrary-account-defence-2026-05-04) — Failed logins for unknown emails do NOT create User rows.
- [D-041](decisions.md#d-041--contextvar-plumbing-vs-explicit-kwargs-for-cross-cutting-per-user-state-2026-05-05) — ContextVar plumbing for cross-cutting per-user state.
- [D-042](decisions.md#d-042--oauth-first-login-links-to-existing-user-by-email-2026-05-05) — OAuth first-login email-based linking.
- [D-043](decisions.md#d-043--single-shared-fernet-key-for-all-encrypted-at-rest-credentials-2026-05-05) — Single shared Fernet key for all encrypted-at-rest credentials.


### E-5.10 🟢 Per-user isolation (R2)

**Scope-changed 2026-10-25:** 🟡 → 🟢. S-5.10.5, the last open story, shipped in PR [#378](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/378). One question it raised has no ruling yet (OQ-17 below); it is not scheduled work.

**Why it exists.** The user's requirement: work done by one user must not be visible to another in any section. An audit found five live leak classes and, underneath them, a missing ownership model — `documents` is a shared cache with no tenant, and three ingest paths create documents with no job either.
**Linked decision:** [D-063](decisions.md#d-063--per-user-isolation-shared-cache-private-catalogue-2026-07-31), [D-070](decisions.md#d-070--document-visibility-keyed-on-document_id-not-the-legacy-video_id-2026-10-19) (fix for the gap below, shipped 2026-10-19)

#### S-5.10.1 🟢 Close the five leak classes + document_visibility
**Shipped:** 2026-07-31
**Audit 2026-10-19:** the grants only ever work for `source_type='video'`. `document_visibility.video_id` references the legacy, nullable `documents.video_id`, and `grant()` drops falsy ids, so PDFs, pasted pages and every other non-video document are ingested but never granted, and never listed or retrieved. Found by the first frontend caller (PR [#224](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/224)); pinned by a strict xfail; fixed by [S-5.10.4](#s-5104--key-document-visibility-on-document_id--shipped-2026-10-19) on 2026-10-19 (PR [#241](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/241)), which also found that `insert_chunks` had never written a non-video chunk and that an empty visible set searched the whole library. The "0 orphans" below held only because no non-video document existed on that instance.
- [x] T-5.10.1.1 `document_visibility` model + migration `d4e5f6a7b8c0` with backfill (203 grants, 0 orphans)
- [x] T-5.10.1.2 Grants wired at every ingest path incl. PDF/paste **dedup hits**; job path grants over the whole link set at 3 phase boundaries. *2026-10-19: the PDF and paste calls pass `doc.video_id`, which is NULL for those sources, so the wiring is present and the grant is a no-op — see the audit note above.*
- [x] T-5.10.1.3 Library browse scoped; job-topic and job-count joins tenant-filtered
- [x] T-5.10.1.4 Cross-tenant delete closed — tenant is part of the lookup, so a foreign id 404s indistinguishably
- [x] T-5.10.1.5 Dataset exports scoped; signature change makes a missed call site a hard error, not a silent leak
- [x] T-5.10.1.6 WebSocket job-ownership check, fails closed, injectable session
- [x] T-5.10.1.7 Vector retrieval scoped to the visible set; empty list stays empty. *2026-10-19: the agent passed `[]` but `query_collection` turned it into an unrestricted search — the test checked the caller, not the service. Closed under T-5.10.4.8.*
- [x] T-5.10.1.8 11 regression tests asserting absence of foreign data

#### S-5.10.2 🟢 Channels per-tenant — shipped 2026-07-31
Linked decision: [D-065](decisions.md#d-065--channel-subscriptions-become-per-tenant-transliteration-reversed-2026-07-31). User decision: channels become per-tenant.
- [x] T-5.10.2.1 `channel_subscriptions` model + migration `f6a7b8c9d1e2` with backfill (95 rows, 92/3 split matching document visibility)
- [x] T-5.10.2.2 `source_weight` moved per-tenant — it was a documented *user-set* trust score stored globally, so one user re-weighted everyone's retrieval ranking
- [x] T-5.10.2.3 `subscription_service` as the single accessor; channel list, detail, subscribe/unsubscribe/sync and video listing all scoped
- [x] T-5.10.2.4 8 isolation tests: subscribe/weight/sync-timestamp do not cross tenants; video counts show only visible documents
- [x] T-5.10.2.5 `api_quota_log` unscoped (low sensitivity — leaks usage volume only). *Verified 2026-10-18: its columns are id / timestamp / operation / units, with no user or tenant*. *Closed 2026-10-25 by the owner ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): `/health/quota` showing an install-wide total without sign-in is by design; the YouTube Data API quota it reports belongs to the install, not to a user.*
- [x] T-5.10.2.6 Q&A-history Chroma collection — verify tenant filtering matches the transcript collection. *Verified 2026-10-18: `query_qa_collection` restricts to `tenant_id` metadata (T-5.6.6), the counterpart of the transcript collection's visible-set filter*
- [x] T-5.10.2.7 Re-run the D-055 harness: library Q&A now answers from a per-user corpus. *2026-10-19, PR [#326](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/326): exact-input replay of the answer step on the ten E-7.1 questions, `claude-sonnet-5` medium vs `claude-opus-5` high, three blind judges and three-lens verification ([note](notes/2026-10-19-t51027-library-qa-d055-replay.md)). Challenger won 2 of 10 questions; mean relevance 8.63 vs 8.97, faithfulness 6.63 vs 7.77; confirmed unsupported claims 119 vs 54. **No re-tier warranted** under the pre-registered rule. A sensitivity re-judge with each source's publication date in the excerpts (outside the rule) gave challenger 15 / production 11, 6 of 10 questions, relevance +0.03: still no re-tier, and it shows the undated run's 21–8 for production was mostly a harness artifact (judges could not see the dates answers cite). `judge_qa_pairs.py --dates-db` fixes the harness for T-7.1.2.3*

#### S-5.10.3 🟢 Drop the legacy global columns on `channels` — shipped 2026-10-19
**PR:** [#316](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/316). `subscribed`, `source_weight`, `last_synced_at` were retained on `channels` so a partial rollout stayed recoverable; nothing read them any more, so migration `1a6e09517250` drops them.
- [x] T-5.10.3.1 Audit remaining readers and writers. *2026-10-19: no reader anywhere (app, scripts, schemas; routers already went through `subscription_service`). The last writer was `execute_subscription_job`, which set the global `subscribed` and `last_synced_at` — and never the per-tenant ones: `subscription_service.mark_synced` had no caller, so a tenant's sync time was never recorded and the channel page always showed "never synced". The job now writes the job tenant's `channel_subscriptions` row (`set_subscribed`, `mark_synced`); `test_subscription_task.py` asserts it, and that another tenant's row is untouched*
- [x] T-5.10.3.2 Migration `1a6e09517250` drops the three columns (SQLite batch rebuild). Downgrade rebuilds them from `channel_subscriptions`: subscribed if any tenant subscribes, the latest sync, weight back to neutral (a global weight cannot be rebuilt from several tenants' without picking one, and every revision with the columns reads weights per tenant). Round trip in `tests/test_migrations/test_drop_legacy_channel_columns_migration.py`

#### S-5.10.4 🟢 Key document visibility on `document_id` — shipped 2026-10-19
**PR:** [#241](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/241). Proposed as a side-session card on 2026-10-19; landed after the R3/R4 batch (PR #227) because both touch `qa_agent.py` and the library Q&A route.
**Linked decision:** [D-070](decisions.md#d-070--document-visibility-keyed-on-document_id-not-the-legacy-video_id-2026-10-19) (accepted; records the transition choice and six defects found while shipping)
**Acceptance (met).** An uploaded PDF and a pasted page appear in `GET /library/videos`, are found by its `search=`, and are in the set library Q&A retrieves from — for the tenant who ingested them and for nobody else (`tests/test_routers/test_library_visibility_isolation.py`, two tenants). The strict xfail in `tests/test_routers/test_library_paste_urls.py` is un-marked and passes. Non-video chunks are proven to reach a real in-memory Chroma collection and to be scoped by `document_id` (`tests/test_services/test_chroma_document_scope.py`). 1,454 backend tests pass; `npm run build` passes.
**Tasks**
- [x] T-5.10.4.1 Migration `3653e565c1fc` (on top of S-8.1.1's `d8e9f0a1b2c3`; two earlier drafts had taken `c7d8e9f0a1b2` and `d8e9f0a1b2c3`, which S-8.3.1 and S-8.1.1 also took the same day — ids are random now): `document_visibility.document_id` (FK `documents.document_id`, cascade), backfill by joining `documents` on `video_id`, orphans dropped with a logged count, unique moved to `(document_id, tenant_id)`, `video_id` retired via SQLite batch rebuild. #### S-5.10.5 🟢 Remaining readers keyed on the legacy `video_id`
**Shipped:** 2026-10-25 — PR [#378](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/378). Filed 2026-10-19 while shipping S-5.10.4 (PR [#241](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/241)); listed there as follow-ups rather than widened into that PR.
**Linked decision:** [D-070](decisions.md#d-070--document-visibility-keyed-on-document_id-not-the-legacy-video_id-2026-10-19)
**Scope.** Two readers still key on `documents.video_id`, which is NULL for every non-video source, so they silently omit PDFs, pasted pages, Reddit and HN documents; and the D-070 chunk backfill only runs where the API lifespan runs.
**Tasks**
- [x] T-5.10.5.1 `services/dataset_service.py` derives knowledge-export ownership from `JobVideo.video_id` (pre-D-063 shape): non-video documents never export, and nor does any PDF or pasted page, which has no job. Filter through `visibility_service.visible_document_ids` instead; add a test with a PDF that carries a knowledge report — *done: `iter_knowledge_rows` selects through the visible set, keyed on `document_id`, so pasted URLs (YouTube included), uploaded PDFs, documents whose job was deleted and every non-video document export, and a job-linked document with no grant no longer does. Found on the way: the export had never streamed a row. The legacy `Query` adds `unique()` to entity queries unless `Query.yield_per()` is used, so the first matching row raised on SQLAlchemy 2.0.48 as well as 2.1 (PR [#375](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/375) found the 2.1 half); it now selects three columns with `Query.yield_per()`. Four router tests in `test_exports.py` ingest through the real routes (paste, upload, extract, job delete) and check both formats*
- [x] T-5.10.5.2 Run `chroma_service.backfill_chunk_document_ids` on Celery worker startup as well as in the API lifespan: a worker-only start after the upgrade leaves pre-2026-10-19 chunks unretrievable until the API boots once — *done: a `worker_ready` hook (`celery_app.run_startup_backfills`) runs the chunk backfill inline before the consumer drains a task, closes the Chroma client and drops the model it opened (`chroma_service.close_client`, chromadb's class caches included, so prefork children are fork-clean), then queues the Q&A backfill as the `chroma.backfill_qa_library` task. A `worker_process_init` receiver makes a forked child forget any Chroma state it inherited. Before this, CLAUDE.md said the worker ran the Q&A backfill; nothing but the API ran either one. `test_celery_app.py` sends the real signals (7 tests)*
- [x] T-5.10.5.3 `temporal_service.known_eras_for` is keyed on YouTube ids by design (search candidates are not documents yet) — confirm and note it in the function docstring so the next audit does not re-flag it — *done: confirmed (its only caller is the topic search agent, whose candidates are YouTube results; a pasted YouTube link never gets an era, since only job chunking extracts one). The docstring also says the lookup is not tenant-filtered and that no decision covers this, so the note is not a D-063 exemption (OQ-17)*

**Open questions**
- OQ-17 (from S-5.10.5): `known_eras_for` reads the discussed era of any document with that YouTube id, including documents only another tenant ingested. Is that acceptable as shared-cache reuse under [D-063](decisions.md#d-063--per-user-isolation-shared-cache-private-catalogue-2026-07-31), or must it filter through the asker's visible set? The owner's call; nothing changes until it is ruled.

### E-5.1 🟢 `tenant_id` audit + retrofit

**Scope.** Add `tenant_id` / `workspace_id` columns to every user-scoped table; convert today's implicit JWT scoping to an explicit column for future per-tenant rate limiting + multi-workspace.

**Closed 2026-05-04.** Five PRs ship the four-phase tenancy retrofit per the [D-038](decisions.md#d-038--tenancy-retrofit-ships-in-four-phases-audit--additive--backfillwrites--reads--not-null-2026-05-04) sequencing:

- **Phase 0** ([PR #149](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/149)) — [`docs/saas-tenant-id-audit.md`](saas-tenant-id-audit.md). Headline finding: codebase is structurally single-tenant despite JWT auth.
- **Phase 1** ([PR #150](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/150)) — Alembic migration `d5e6f7a8b9c0_add_tenant_id_columns.py` adds NULLABLE `tenant_id String(36)` + index to `jobs` / `qa_exchanges` / `library_qa_exchanges` / `qa_history_exchanges`. ORM models updated. Purely additive — no behaviour change.
- **Phase 2a** ([PR #151](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/151)) — Backfill migration `b2c3d4e5f6a7_backfill_tenant_id.py` sets `tenant_id = first-user.id` WHERE NULL. Every write-side router stamps `tenant_id=current_user.id` from `Depends(get_current_user)`. After this PR, every existing row is attributed AND every new row is correctly attributed.
- **Phase 2b** ([PR #152](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/152)) — `get_job(db, job_id, tenant_id=None)` + `get_jobs(...)` accept the optional filter. Routers thread `tenant_id=current_user.id`. Cross-tenant reads return 404 (not 403) to avoid existence-leak. Codebase is now fully tenant-isolated on user-facing surfaces.
- **Phase 2c** (this PR) — Operator-coordinated runbook [`docs/migration-tenant-id-not-null.md`](migration-tenant-id-not-null.md) + Alembic migration `e6f7a8b9c0d1_tenant_id_not_null.py`. Per [D-032](decisions.md#d-032--operator-coordinated-runbook-vs-automatic-startup-migration-for-data-bearing-identifier-renames-2026-05-03) precedent, the migration ships in the codebase but is not auto-applied — operators run `alembic upgrade head` after running the runbook's pre-flight (verify zero NULL rows / backup / stop writers). Optional ORM-tightening (`Mapped[str | None]` → `Mapped[str]`) deferred to a calm follow-up PR.

**Tasks**
- [x] T-5.1.0 Audit doc — *shipped 2026-05-04 in `docs/saas-tenant-id-audit.md`.*
- [x] T-5.1.1 Phase 1 additive columns + indexes — *shipped 2026-05-04 in [PR #150](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/150).*
- [x] T-5.1.2 Phase 2a/2b — backfill + write-side stamping + read-side filter — *shipped 2026-05-04 in [PR #151](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/151) + [PR #152](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/152).*
- [x] T-5.1.2c Phase 2c — NOT NULL constraint runbook + migration — *shipped 2026-05-04. Operator-coordinated per D-032 precedent; the codebase delivers the safe-execution path, the operator owns the timing.*
- [ ] T-5.1.3 🔴 Phase 3 (deferred) — `tenants` + `tenant_users` tables for multi-user-per-workspace. Only needed for team SaaS tier.

**Linked decision:** [D-038](decisions.md#d-038--tenancy-retrofit-ships-in-four-phases-audit--additive--backfillwrites--reads--not-null-2026-05-04)

Mock payment and the endpoint's shape are unchanged. Implementation note, not part of the ruling: the Subscription page says why the switch is unavailable to anyone else. Tests at both routers. *Accepted 2026-10-25.*
  **Shipped:** 2026-10-25 — PR [#377](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/377). `auth_service.is_operator` is the rule (a set list is the whole rule; with no list, the lowest `created_at`, then `id`, so there is exactly one operator) and `dependencies.require_operator` returns 403 on `PUT /auth/me/tier` and the whole `/admin` router. `GET /auth/me` returns `is_operator`. The Subscription page shows non-operators the plans without Upgrade or Downgrade buttons and a note naming `ADMIN_EMAILS`, ignores the `?upgrade=` link for them, and shows a refused switch as a toast. *From the review:* a listed address belonged to whoever registered it first, and registration checks no email, so (1) `POST /auth/register` refuses a listed address with 403 ("register the account before listing it"); (2) OAuth creates or links an account for a listed address only when the provider verified it (Google's `email_verified`; GitHub's public email, counted as verified); (3) startup logs a warning for every listed address with no account. Tests: `test_operator_gate.py` and `test_auth_service.py`, mutation-checked. Follow-up: T-5.4.10. *Point (2) was replaced 2026-10-29 by T-5.4.10 (PR [#384](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/384), [D-116](decisions.md#d-116--oauth-links-or-creates-an-account-only-from-a-provider-verified-email-and-the-link-records-it-2026-10-29)): every first OAuth link or new account now needs a provider-verified email, and GitHub's public email is no longer counted as verified.*

In `frontend/src/data/tiers.ts`, reels, public report sharing, team workspaces and data residency are marked as planned, and the daily-token lines are dropped (`llm_tokens_per_day` is never enforced). The document caps stay advertised. *Accepted 2026-10-25.*
  **Shipped:** 2026-10-25 — PR [#377](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/377). A `TierFeature.planned` row shows a "Planned" badge, never a check, and is never listed as lost on a downgrade. Reels is split out of the Author Studio row (now "books, sites, decks"). "Priority support" and "Early access to new connectors" on Studio were outside the ruling and are unchanged. Tests: `pricingPage.test.tsx` and `subscriptionPage.test.tsx`
- [x] T-5.2.10 🟢 **Enforce the advertised document caps** (`document_count_cap`: Free 500, Pro 5,000, Studio unlimited), which nothing checks today ([D-111](decisions.md#d-111--operator-only-tier-switching-and-an-honest-pricing-page-2026-10-25)). *Accepted 2026-10-25, after T-5.2.9.*
  **Shipped:** 2026-10-29 — PR [#385](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/385) (batch 2 of 2026-10-25). Linked decision: [D-115](decisions.md#d-115--the-document-cap-counts-visible-documents-a-request-that-names-its-documents-is-all-or-nothing-and-nothing-is-granted-before-approval-2026-10-29). `document_cap_service` counts the tenant's visible documents (`document_visibility`), so a dedup hit on a document they already see never counts and a cached document only someone else sees does. A request that names what it adds is all or nothing, checked before any work: `PUT /jobs/{id}/approve` (only approved documents not yet visible), `POST /library/upload-pdf` (before the bytes are written) and `POST /library/paste-urls` (each distinct new URL once, before any fetch). Subscribe, sync and subscription jobs are refused only when the library is already full; otherwise `execute_subscription_job` stops at the cap, newest uploads first, and the job's note says how many were left out. Refusals are 429 in the `QuotaExceededError` shape plus `requested`, `available` and `message`, shown where each action starts; `GET /auth/quota` reads `documents` usage from visibility and the Subscription page shows a usage line. Behaviour change: visibility is granted for approved links only, at approval, and the search phase grants nothing for any source type (this also removed #382's search-time grant, which had let mixed runs past the cap). Tests: `test_document_caps.py` (28), `test_subscription_document_cap.py` (8), `documentCap.test.ts`, `documentCapRefusals.test.tsx`, `submitJobDocumentCap.test.tsx`, mutation-checked. Known edges, accepted: two requests in the same instant can each pass the check (no lock); grants written earlier for deselected candidates are not revoked; an approved run cancelled before its resume keeps its grants

Today a full library can only be raised by a plan change, and the refusal says so. Its own feature: it needs to decide what happens to the tenant's jobs, notes and Q&A that cite a removed document
- [x] T-5.2.12 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #391)* Record Whisper audio minutes. The transcript fallback and Echo recordings are billed per minute and bypass LangChain, so S-5.2.1 records none of them. Needs a `whisper_seconds` metric at `_transcribe_with_whisper`
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#405](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/405) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-131](decisions.md#d-131--whisper-is-charged-at-the-shared-call-and-usage-is-written-part-way-on-a-timer-after-every-billed-response-2026-11-04). New metric `whisper_seconds`, charged in `youtube_service._whisper_transcribe_with_retry`, the one function every hosted Whisper call goes through, so it also covers podcast audio, which this entry did not name. The amount is each response's `duration`, so a split recording counts each chunk, overlap included, because each is billed. Video and podcast audio go to `ingest` and Echo recordings to `echo_shares`. It shows on `GET /usage` and in "Your usage" (e.g. "90 min of audio transcribed"), with no migration. It is not mirrored into `quota_usage`, so `/auth/quota` is unchanged. Operator scripts open no scope, so their Whisper use stays unrecorded.
- [x] T-5.2.13 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #391)* Write usage part-way through long tasks. A task's usage is written when the task ends, so a worker that dies mid-way through a long subscription sync loses that run's usage
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#405](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/405) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-131](decisions.md#d-131--whisper-is-charged-at-the-shared-call-and-usage-is-written-part-way-on-a-timer-after-every-billed-response-2026-11-04). An open scope writes what it has counted at most every `USAGE_FLUSH_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (new setting, default 60, `0` = only at close). It tries after every model call and every Whisper response, and at `usage_service.checkpoint()` after each video in both extraction loops and each channel in a subscription's uploads walk. It writes through the meter's own session. While the caller's transaction has written, the amounts stay in the ledger, and a try that had to wait does not restart the interval. Amounts leave the ledger before they are written and go back only if that write rolled back, so a checkpoint and the close never write the same amount. Tests: `test_usage_checkpoint.py` and `test_usage_whisper_and_crash.py`, with tasks killed mid-extraction and redelivered. A dead worker still loses what it counted since its last write, at most about one interval unless the caller's open transaction kept making the tries wait. Echo learning runs hold their writes open by design, so theirs is written at the close.

  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#417](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/417) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). A single-user install is unchanged: five, and a sixth refused. The 429 says the limit is theirs and that other people's jobs do not count. **The limit decides refusals, not order,** and there is no install-wide ceiling, so on a shared install the total of active jobs can exceed the old five.

Sync now answers 429 with the sentence `POST /jobs` uses and writes no row. Subscribe always subscribes; at the limit it returns `job_id: null` with a new `sync_note` saying the first sync has not started, why, and that Sync now starts it, which both pages show as a status note. **Found on the way:** a dispatch the broker refused returned the job id and left the row `pending` forever, showing "Syncing…" and holding a place; it now fails the row and returns `None`. A refused sync stays out of the global toast.

A fair share needs per-tenant queues *2026-11-10 (batch 12 sorting): stays proposed; per-tenant queues are an architecture choice.*
- [x] T-5.2.17 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #424)* `POST /jobs` has the orphan #424 fixed for syncs: a failed dispatch returns 500 and leaves a `pending` row counting toward the limit. Fail the row the same way
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#430](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/430) (batch 8 of 2026-11-09). Submit it again in a few minutes.", so it holds no place under the limit, and answers 503 with no exception text; the form keeps what was typed. `PUT /jobs/{id}/approve`: a refused dispatch answers 503 and the job stays awaiting approval with its picks, so Approve works again once the queue is back and Cancel frees the place. The approved documents are now granted only after the resume task is queued, because the D-115 cap counts grants and a refused approval kept them after Cancel (review fix; the resume task re-grants). The census of every other route that writes a row and then dispatches found one more: a voice test the queue refused used up one of `ECHO_VOICE_TESTS_PER_DAY`, so three presses during an outage locked the user out for the day. `tests_today` now leaves out a test that failed with no pairs and the `NOT_QUEUED` sentence. No other route leaves a row counting; the PR lists each.
- [x] T-5.2.18 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #430)* A dispatch that raised after the broker had accepted the message leaves a failed row that a worker may still run, because the tasks do not check for `failed` at start. #424's sync has the same edge
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#436](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/436) (batch 9 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-158](decisions.md#d-158--a-job-task-returns-at-once-on-a-failed-row-a-job-dates-an-undated-row-and-its-stored-chunks-whisper-text-without-segments-is-a-transcription-and-a-manual-dispatchs-503-promises-nothing-2026-11-09). `execute_topic_job`, `execute_channel_job`, `resume_job_after_approval` and `execute_subscription_job` read the row first and return when it is already `failed` (`job_tasks._already_failed`), before the usage scope opens, so a skipped task writes, publishes and charges nothing. Checked first that nothing runs a failed job again on purpose: no route retries a run in place, the scheduler queues no job task, the tasks never call `self.retry`, and a redelivery after a worker died mid-run finds the row where that worker left it, never failed, so it still runs. Only `failed`: `cancelled` and the transient states are unchanged (T-5.2.20). Tested through `POST /jobs` and Sync now with a dispatch that published and lost its reply, then the real task delivered: the row is unchanged byte for byte, nothing is published and `usage_service.metered` is never entered.
- [x] T-5.2.19 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #430)* `POST /saved-searches/{id}/run-now` and `POST /author/newsletter/send-now` still answer a bare 500 on a broker outage. Nothing is left stuck, since neither writes a row before it dispatches
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#436](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/436) (batch 9 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-158](decisions.md#d-158--a-job-task-returns-at-once-on-a-failed-row-a-job-dates-an-undated-row-and-its-stored-chunks-whisper-text-without-segments-is-a-transcription-and-a-manual-dispatchs-503-promises-nothing-2026-11-09). Both routes answer 503 when the dispatch raises, with a sentence both pages already show through `detail`. The broker's error, which can hold its URL and password, goes only to the scrubbed log. Neither route writes before it dispatches (run-now's quota check only reads), so there was nothing to undo. **Review fix:** the sentence promises nothing either way, since the broker may have taken the message before the reply was lost and neither task dedupes a manual trigger. It says the run or issue may or may not happen and asks the reader to check its history or their outputs before pressing again, and `docs/api-reference.md` says the same. No frontend change.
- [x] T-5.2.20 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #436)* Two edges T-5.2.18 left. A cancelled run whose task is delivered anyway comes back, because each entry point writes `searching` or `extracting` before it reads `_is_cancelled`; this happens when the revoke reached no live worker. And a worker that picks a task up before the route has marked the row failed runs on and writes over `failed` at its next status change. The fix is `cancelled` in the start guard and `failed` beside `cancelled` in the per-video checks
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#440](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/440) (batch 10 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-161](decisions.md#d-161--a-job-tasks-status-writes-are-one-conditional-update-the-first-stops-words-win-a-progress-callback-may-end-the-search-and-embedded_in_chroma-follows-the-chunks-2026-11-09). The start guard, now `_already_stopped`, reads `cancelled` beside `failed` before the usage scope opens, so a task delivered after a revoke that reached no live worker writes, publishes and charges nothing. Every status write a job task makes goes through one conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status NOT IN ('failed', 'cancelled')` (`_write_unless_stopped`); refused, `_update_job` raises `_RunStopped`, which each entry point catches before its `except Exception`. The per-item checks, now `_is_stopped`, stop on `failed` too, with a new one before the YouTube search agent. Progress writes take the same condition and are published only when written, and `_handle_failure`, its fallback and `_backstop_orphan` are conditional, so the first stop's words win. `PUT /jobs/{id}/approve` writes `extracting` only over `awaiting_approval` (`update_job_status(..., only_from=...)`), so a resume that already failed is no longer stranded at `extracting`. Checked first that nothing runs a stopped row on purpose. **Found on the way:** `_handle_failure` wrote `failed` over a cancelled run that then raised, and on the solo pool a cancel during the report ended `completed`, since a running task cannot be terminated there; now the completed write is refused and the unused report file is deleted. **Review fixes:** a run cancelled during its YouTube search ran on, published "Found N videos", which flipped the open page back from Cancelled, and fetched every non-video post with its stance calls. The progress callback now raises `search_agent.SearchStopped` when its write is refused, the one callback error `_notify` lets through, so the searches and the ranking call never run; a check follows the agent; "Found N videos" is a guarded write; and `_store_non_video_candidates` checks before each candidate and commits each one, so a cancel lands between fetches and one bad candidate is rolled back alone. `embedded_in_chroma` is set only after `insert_chunks` returns (`_insert_and_mark_embedded`), so a run stopped or raising before the RAG build leaves its documents unflagged for the next job to embed. Tested in `test_stopped_run_stays_stopped.py` and `test_search_agent.py` against the test database and an in-memory Chroma, with fakes for every connector and model and a mutation check for each mechanism; 14 of the first 15 non-mutation cases fail against master's code. CLAUDE.md gains a convention bullet, and `docs/architecture.md` and the approve and cancel entries in `docs/api-reference.md` are updated. Left out: T-5.2.21 to T-5.2.23. Documents older runs left flagged with no chunks are T-5.2.24.
- [x] T-5.2.21 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #440)* A standalone `publish_progress` not tied to a write, such as the sync's "N videos ingested so far", can still go out between a stop and the run's next check. Gating each one costs a read per publish
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#449](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/449) (batch 11 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-163](decisions.md#d-163--a-progress-line-is-written-before-it-is-published-a-cancel-that-loses-the-race-answers-with-the-runs-end-and-revokes-nothing-and-completed-is-a-stopped-status-2026-11-09). A new `job_tasks._report_progress` writes the line to the row in the run's conditional write and publishes it only once written; refused, it raises `_RunStopped` and nothing goes out. All six standalone publishes go through it: the channel job's "Processing channel i/N", the sync's "Resolving channel", "Resolved N channel(s)", "Walking uploads for X" and "N videos ingested so far", which had no check at all, and both extraction paths' "Indexed N new chunks", which now rides on the write it already followed. Nothing outside `job_tasks.py` publishes a job's progress. The row holds each line, cut to 500 characters, so a reload shows what the socket said. **Review fix:** a gate is not free where an `_is_stopped` read stood: it costs a conditional UPDATE, its COMMIT and a refresh SELECT, plus a SELECT first when a commit since the last write expired the job, once per channel or phase; a statement-count test pins it and the docstrings say so. The gap left is between a write's commit and its publish, which the page's 5-second refetch corrects (T-5.2.28).
- [x] T-5.2.22 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #440)* `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel`'s own write is unconditional, so it can write `cancelled` over a row that completed or failed between its read and its write
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#449](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/449) (batch 11 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-163](decisions.md#d-163--a-progress-line-is-written-before-it-is-published-a-cancel-that-loses-the-race-answers-with-the-runs-end-and-revokes-nothing-and-completed-is-a-stopped-status-2026-11-09). `job_service.update_job_status(..., unless_ended=True)` makes the cancel route's write one conditional UPDATE. A run that completed or failed first keeps its end, and the route answers 400 with what it ended as ("This run has already completed, so there is nothing to cancel."), as its first check now does. The task is revoked only once the row says cancelled, with the task id the row holds then; a broker that refuses the revoke is logged by exception type alone, and the route still answers 200. `useCancelJob` refetches the run and the job list when a cancel fails, and the global toast says the server's words once. The cancel entry in `docs/api-reference.md` is updated, and a test holds its condition to `_STOPPED_STATUSES`. The cancel route still publishes no status change on the socket (T-5.2.30).
- [x] T-5.2.23 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #440)* `completed` is not a stopped status, so a redelivery after a worker died between writing `completed` and the ack runs the run again
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#449](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/449) (batch 11 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-163](decisions.md#d-163--a-progress-line-is-written-before-it-is-published-a-cancel-that-loses-the-race-answers-with-the-runs-end-and-revokes-nothing-and-completed-is-a-stopped-status-2026-11-09). Checked first: `task_acks_late=True` is set and `task_reject_on_worker_lost` is not, so a worker that dies before the ack gets its task delivered again. `completed` is a stopped status for job tasks beside `failed` and `cancelled`, at the start guard, the in-run writes, the per-item checks and the failure handler. **Beyond the line:** the topic and channel first phases also leave alone a row at `awaiting_approval` or later, since a first phase delivered again there would search again and write `searching` over the user's review or the running resume, and their orphan backstop fails only `pending` and `searching`. A second delivery whose `completed` write is refused keeps the report file the row points at. Every path that starts a run on purpose was checked and still runs: `POST /jobs`, Sync now and subscribe create a new row, approve resumes from `awaiting_approval`, the scheduler queues no job task, nothing retries, `scripts/regenerate_report.py` dispatches nothing, and a redelivery after a worker died mid-run runs again as before. Tested in `test_run_stops.py` (38 cases) against the test database and an in-memory Chroma, with a mutation check for each mechanism. CLAUDE.md's convention bullet and `docs/architecture.md` are updated. **Owner step:** restart the API and the worker. A run that outlasts Redis's one-hour visibility timeout can still be handed to a second process on a prefork pool (T-5.2.29, the owner's call).
- [x] T-5.2.24 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #440; the owner's call)* Documents that runs before #440 left flagged `embedded_in_chroma` with no chunks in Chroma, after a cancel or a failure between extraction and the RAG build. Every later job reuses such a document as embedded and inserts nothing, so no job or library Q&A retrieves it. Repairing them needs a check of the library against Chroma and a write run, which is the owner's
  **Shipped (the check):** 2026-11-09 — PR [#448](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/448) (batch 11 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-162](decisions.md#d-162--a-document-counts-as-embedded-when-any-chunk-names-it-every-doubt-keeps-the-flag-a-document-with-no-words-keeps-it-and-the-check-embeds-nothing-2026-11-09). `backend/scripts/check_embedded_flags.py`, backed by `app/services/embedded_flag_service.py`, reads the documents table and only the metadata of the global collection's chunks, opened with `get_collection(..., embedding_function=None)`: no chunk text, no model, no LLM or network call, and the collection is never created. A document counts as having chunks when any chunk names it: by `document_id`, by source type and id or `video_id` for a chunk the startup backfill has not stamped, or by chunk id. It reports by source type how many documents are flagged, how many have chunks, and how the rest split: `cached` (the text is in `transcript_cache`), `fetch_again` (the text lived only in the chunks never written) and `empty` (cached text with no words). It prints ids and counts, never titles or text, and the dry run, the default, runs read-only. `--write` is the owner's step: it clears the flag on `cached` and `fetch_again` documents only, re-checks each against Chroma first, clears with a conditional UPDATE, changes nothing in Chroma, and writes nothing on a partial scan, a missing collection or a store whose chunks match no document in this database. **Review fix:** an empty collection while documents are flagged, the likeliest sign of a lost `CHROMA_GLOBAL_COLLECTION_NAME` pin, now stops the dry run and `--write` alike, naming that setting and `CHROMA_PERSIST_DIR`; only `--empty-store-ok` clears against a store that really was emptied. **Live dry run, read-only, 2026-11-09:** 8,567 chunks scanned in `pratidhvani_global`; 254 documents flagged embedded, all videos, every one with chunks; 0 to clear. A SQL count over a read-only snapshot agreed, and `--write` was not run, so the owner's `--write` run has nothing to do today; the script stays for later checks. Tested in `test_check_embedded_flags.py` (16 cases), including the real resume and sync tasks after `--write`. CLAUDE.md gains a key-files row. `--embed` was left out (T-5.2.25).
- [ ] T-5.2.25 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #448)* An `--embed` option for `check_embedded_flags.py`, only if a later dry run finds documents. A cleared document that no job selects again stays out of retrieval until one does, which includes every PDF and any pasted page no search finds again. It would be a second chunking path outside the job task, so it needs an explicit spend flag and estimate (the era call, the English rendering), a re-extraction for a PDF and a fetch for a pasted page *2026-11-10 (batch 12 sorting): waits. The 2026-11-10 dry run found nothing to clear, so an `--embed` option would have nothing to embed; reopen it if a later run finds documents.*
- [x] T-5.2.26 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #448)* A configured collection that holds a few chunks of this database while most sit in the legacy `videoresearchpro_global`, as after a lost pin and a partial re-embed, passes every guard of `check_embedded_flags.py`, and the documents only in the legacy collection would look chunkless. Closing it needs a check across collections, such as refusing when a legacy collection holding chunks of this database exists beside the configured one
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#451](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/451) (batch 12 of 2026-11-10). Linked decision: [D-166](decisions.md#d-166--the-embedded-flag-check-refuses-while-another-collection-holds-chunks-of-this-library-told-apart-by-name-and-by-shape-and-its-advice-follows-the-collections-kind-2026-11-10). `embedded_flag_service` lists the store's collections and reads every one besides the configured collection, metadata only (`list_collections()`, then `get_collection(name, embedding_function=None)`: nothing created, no model loaded). Any collection holding chunks of this database's documents stops the dry run (exit 1) and `--write` alike, with or without `--empty-store-ok` or `--document-ids`, and a collection that cannot be listed, opened or fully scanned stops it too. Collections are told apart by name and by shape together: the Q&A, notes and Echo collections (`echo_vectors.echo_collection_names()`), under their configured names, are never opened; in any other collection a row counts only when its id is `{video_id}:{n}` or `{document_id}:{n}` naming a document here, or its metadata names one and carries an integer `chunk_index`, so a renamed setting's old Q&A or notes collection does not block. The report gives every collection seen and, for each one read, rows scanned, chunks of this database, documents named, `documents_only_here`, `chunks_only_here` and how many a write would have cleared; a missing configured collection's stop also says where the library is. **Review fixes:** the stop's advice follows the collection's kind. A pre-global `job_<id>` collection is merged, never pinned, with `chroma_service.migrate_legacy_per_job_collections()` (the prefix now lives in one place, `LEGACY_PER_JOB_PREFIX` / `is_legacy_per_job_collection`); a copy holding no chunk id the configured collection lacks is a leftover, to delete once Q&A answers from the configured collection; anything else is re-pinned or migrated. The runbook's Rollback step 3 says the check refuses while the unused copy exists, and a new paragraph gives the per-job merge command with a backup first. **Live dry run, read-only:** `pratidhvani_global` 8,567 chunks, 0 of no document here; `qa_library_global` another kind, not read; no `videoresearchpro_global`, no `job_*` collection; 254 documents flagged, all videos with chunks, 0 to clear. Tested in `test_check_embedded_flags.py` (14 new cases, 30 in all) through the script's `main` with in-memory Chroma, including mutation checks for the cross-collection scan, the shape test, the name list, the per-job rule and the chunk-id test. CLAUDE.md's key-files row, `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/migration-code-identifiers.md` section A are updated. No migration, no setting, nothing to restart. Left out: an override flag (T-5.2.31).
- ~~T-5.2.27 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #448)* A cached transcript with no words is chunked into nothing and still flagged `embedded_in_chroma`, before and after #440, because both job loops flag every document they chunk. `check_embedded_flags.py` reports these as `empty` and leaves their flag~~ ✖ Closed 2026-11-10 with no change (batch 12 sorting): an empty document's flag means there is nothing to embed, which [D-162](decisions.md#d-162--a-document-counts-as-embedded-when-any-chunk-names-it-every-doubt-keeps-the-flag-a-document-with-no-words-keeps-it-and-the-check-embeds-nothing-2026-11-09) already keeps (the check leaves an empty document flagged), and clearing it would make every later job read the empty text again. Reopen it if the owner wants empty documents unflagged.
- [x] T-5.2.28 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #449)* The page could drop socket events for a run it already holds as ended, which would close the last gap between a progress write's commit and its publish; today the 5-second refetch corrects it. It lives in `useJobProgress`
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#453](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/453) (batch 12 of 2026-11-10), with T-5.2.30. Linked decision: [D-169](decisions.md#d-169--a-cancel-that-takes-is-announced-after-its-commit-a-page-takes-no-more-events-for-a-run-it-holds-as-ended-the-runs-videos-follow-its-cached-status-and-every-cached-jobs-list-takes-a-runs-events-2026-11-10). `useJobProgress` drops every progress, status or error event for a run whose cached status is `completed`, `failed` or `cancelled`, in the run's own query and in each row of each cached jobs list, and a late event refetches nothing. A refetch is not an event, so it still sets the cache either way. Checked first that an ended status reaches the cache only on the server's word: the `useJob` and `useJobs` fetches, `useCancelJob`'s and `useApproveJob`'s answers, and socket events, each published after the write that ends the row. Nothing in the frontend writes an ended status, and CLAUDE.md's convention now says the guard holds only while that stays true (no optimistic cancel). **Found on the way:** neither progress hook ever updated a jobs list. Both wrote to `['jobs', undefined]`, a key no list has, since `useJobs` keys each filter and page apart, so the jobs page learned of every change only at its 10-second refetch. Both now fold each event into every cached list (`setQueriesData({ queryKey: ['jobs'] })`), row by row and guarded, and `useAllJobsProgress` refetches the lists when an event ends a row. **Review fix:** a dropped event also dropped the end-of-run videos refetch. The `jobVideos` query now follows the run's cached status: it is refetched when that status moves to `awaiting_approval` or to an end, whatever moved it (an event, the 5-second poll or this tab's cancel answer), and a page that opens on an ended run fetches its videos once. Tested in `useJobProgress.test.tsx` (14 cases) with the real `wsClient` over a fake WebSocket, mutation-checked by hand. `docs/architecture.md` is updated. Left out: T-5.2.33 and T-5.2.35.
- [ ] T-5.2.29 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #449; the owner's call)* With `task_acks_late` and the Redis transport's default one-hour visibility timeout, a run that has not acked within the hour can be handed to a second worker process while the first still runs, and a sync can take hours. T-5.2.23's writes keep a completed row completed and its report, but the second copy still spends. The owner's single solo worker cannot take it; a prefork pool with several processes can. The fix is `broker_transport_options={"visibility_timeout": ...}` longer than the longest run, or a per-run lease: a deployment setting *2026-11-10 (batch 12 sorting): the owner's.* *2026-11-10 (batch 13): PR [#457](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/457) keeps an Author output's end, content and citations from a second live delivery, and T-5.2.36 names what that delivery can still do to a completed output's files.*
- [x] T-5.2.30 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #449)* `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel` publishes no status change on the socket, so another open tab learns of a cancel only at its next refetch. An Author output's cancel does publish one
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#453](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/453) (batch 12 of 2026-11-10), with T-5.2.28. Linked decision: [D-169](decisions.md#d-169--a-cancel-that-takes-is-announced-after-its-commit-a-page-takes-no-more-events-for-a-run-it-holds-as-ended-the-runs-videos-follow-its-cached-status-and-every-cached-jobs-list-takes-a-runs-events-2026-11-10). Once its conditional write has made the row cancelled, the cancel route publishes a `job_status_change` to `cancelled` on `job_progress:{job_id}`, the event a run's own transitions send: after the commit, so no page reads `cancelled` before the row says it, and before the revoke, which can wait on a broker that is down. A cancel that loses the race to the run's end, or reaches a run already ended, publishes nothing. A failed publish never fails the cancel: `_announce_cancelled` logs it by exception type, and the three run-event publishers in `progress_service` now log a Redis error by type too, where they logged its text. A status change to `cancelled` refetches the run and the lists, as `completed` and `failed` already did. Tested in `test_cancel_announced.py` (23 cases) through the real route, with a fake Redis client and a patched revoke, on one timeline of every `UPDATE jobs`, COMMIT and publish; its mutation cases recompile the production code with an edit and check each main check refuses it. `docs/api-reference.md` now gives the three event shapes the server really sends (`job_progress`, `job_status_change`, `job_error`) in place of ones it never sent, and `docs/architecture.md` and CLAUDE.md's convention are updated. No migration, no setting, no route change. **Owner step:** restart the API and reload the frontend. Left out: T-5.2.32 and T-5.2.34.
- [x] T-5.2.32 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #453)* A second cancel that raced the first answers 200, as it did before #453, and now publishes the same `cancelled` event a second time; it also revokes a second time, as before. The duplicate is harmless, since it is true and a tab that holds the run as cancelled drops it. Telling the two cancels apart would need `update_job_status` to return its row count, which changes its contract for every caller
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#457](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/457) (batch 13 of 2026-11-10), with T-5.2.33 to T-5.2.35. Linked decision: [D-172](decisions.md#d-172--only-the-cancel-whose-own-write-took-announces-it-an-author-output-stops-at-the-write-and-keeps-nothing-when-it-ends-unfinished-a-failure-refetches-the-run-and-a-refetch-in-flight-at-an-end-is-cancelled-rather-than-out-ranked-2026-11-10). The new `job_service.write_job_status` returns `StatusWrite(job, written)`, `written` from the conditional UPDATE's row count, and `update_job_status` delegates to it and still returns the row, so the approve route and the existing tests keep their contract. The cancel route publishes only when its own write took. A second cancel that read the run active and found it cancelled by the first still answers 200 with the cancelled run and still revokes, as before; a cancel of a run already cancelled still answers 400 at the first check. Tested in `test_cancel_announced.py` (29 cases, 6 new) through the real route: two racing cancels, the second run in full inside the first's write, publish once, and `write_job_status` reports its own write and moves `updated_at`; mutation cases for the route announcing whatever its write did and for `written` always true. `test_run_stops.py`'s race helper now patches `write_job_status`. The mutation helper #453 wrote moved to `backend/tests/mutation.py`, shared by both files.
- [x] T-5.2.33 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #453)* A refetch already in flight when a cancel commits can return the run as active after the cancel's event has applied. The cache then reads active until the next refetch, about 5 seconds later. A refetch must win in both directions, so closing it would mean comparing the fetched row's `updated_at` with the event's, which is a bigger change
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#457](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/457) (batch 13 of 2026-11-10), with T-5.2.32, T-5.2.34 and T-5.2.35. Linked decision: [D-172](decisions.md#d-172--only-the-cancel-whose-own-write-took-announces-it-an-author-output-stops-at-the-write-and-keeps-nothing-when-it-ends-unfinished-a-failure-refetches-the-run-and-a-refetch-in-flight-at-an-end-is-cancelled-rather-than-out-ranked-2026-11-10). Closed without `updated_at`. Measured first: in a tab that hears the end by its event, there was no gap, since the event's refetch cancels the fetch in flight and discards its answer (`cancelRefetch`, React Query's default); a vitest pins it. The one open path was the tab that pressed Cancel when the HTTP answer arrives before the socket event: the answer stored `cancelled`, the event was then dropped as late, and the stale poll showed the run active for up to 5 seconds, reproduced in vitest before the fix. `useCancelJob` and `useApproveJob`, which had the same race with the approval list coming back, now store their answer through `storeAnswer` in `hooks/useJobs.ts`, which first cancels a fetch of the run in flight. Checked as the item asked that every status write bumps `updated_at`, the conditional `Query.update` included, and a test pins it; comparing it was rejected because it is wall-clock time from two processes, so a clock step back or skew could make a page drop the true row indefinitely, and on Windows two writes a few milliseconds apart can tie. D-169 point 4 stands with that addition.
- [x] T-5.2.34 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #453)* Author outputs (`useOutputsProgress`) have the same commit-then-publish gap: `output_service.report_progress` checks the status, commits, then publishes. #453's item named only `useJobProgress`, so outputs are unchanged
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#457](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/457) (batch 13 of 2026-11-10), with T-5.2.32, T-5.2.33 and T-5.2.35. Linked decision: [D-172](decisions.md#d-172--only-the-cancel-whose-own-write-took-announces-it-an-author-output-stops-at-the-write-and-keeps-nothing-when-it-ends-unfinished-a-failure-refetches-the-run-and-a-refetch-in-flight-at-an-end-is-cancelled-rather-than-out-ranked-2026-11-10). Wider than the line: outputs followed the run's stop rule at the start and in the cancel, but every write was a read followed by an unconditional one, so `report_progress` could publish `generating` after a cancel, the run's `completed` could overwrite a cancel that landed between its check and its write, and `cancel_output` could write `cancelled` over an output that had just finished. Now every output status and progress write is one conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status NOT IN TERMINAL_STATUSES` (`output_service._write_unless_ended`), with the run's pending content flushed into the same transaction and rolled back on refusal, and each event published after its commit. `transition_to` returns whether it wrote, and the unused `current_status` read is gone. `cancel_output` revokes only after its write takes and answers 400 when the output finished between its read and its write. A refused progress write on a row another delivery ended raises the new `OutputStopped`, and the run stops. On the page, `applyOutputProgress` leaves an ended row as it is and `useOutputsProgress` counts the cached copies that took an event, so an event every copy drops refetches nothing; `skipped` counts as an end. Nothing in the frontend writes an output's status itself, so the drop is safe. **Review fixes:** a book commits its manuscript, citations and EPUB path with its 92% and 95% progress reports, so an output cancelled, failed, skipped or crashed after them kept that content and `GET .../content` still served it. The cancel's write and the run's own `failed` or `skipped` write now clear `content_text`, `content_path` and the citations in the same transaction (`drop_content`), so the content is gone the moment the row reads ended, not after a PDF render of up to 180 seconds; a run that finds an end someone else wrote clears them with one write conditional on that end and removes the folder (`_clear_unfinished`). A completed output keeps everything. `POST /author/outputs` writes `Queued` only over `pending` (`record_queued`), so a worker that failed the output first keeps its words. Tested in `test_output_ends.py` (47 cases, new) on a timeline of every `UPDATE outputs`, COMMIT and publish, with the real service, the real cancel route and the book's real order, and `useOutputsProgress.test.tsx` (5 cases, new) with the studio list and the reader live; 11 of the first 18 non-mutation cases fail against master's `output_service.py`, and the 8 added on review fail against the PR's first push. `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/api-reference.md` say so. Left out: two live deliveries of one output can still replace a completed output's files (T-5.2.36).

Since #453's review fix it refetches the run's videos. The 5-second refetch fills in the rest
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-10 — PR [#457](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/457) (batch 13 of 2026-11-10), with T-5.2.32 to T-5.2.34. Linked decision: [D-172](decisions.md#d-172--only-the-cancel-whose-own-write-took-announces-it-an-author-output-stops-at-the-write-and-keeps-nothing-when-it-ends-unfinished-a-failure-refetches-the-run-and-a-refetch-in-flight-at-an-end-is-cancelled-rather-than-out-ranked-2026-11-10). A `job_error` counts as an end for refetching: it refetches the run and the jobs lists, once. The videos are still fetched once, by #453's status watch, not again by the event. Tested in `useJobProgress.test.tsx` (19 cases, 5 new) with the real `wsClient` over a fake WebSocket, including nothing refetched for a run already ended; hand mutations are in the PR. CLAUDE.md's WebSocket convention, `docs/architecture.md` and the socket events in `docs/api-reference.md` are updated. No migration, no setting, no route change. **Owner step:** restart the API and the worker and reload the frontend.
- [ ] T-5.2.36 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-10, from #457; the owner's call, with T-5.2.29)* If two live deliveries of one Author output task run at once, T-5.2.29's situation, a file the second writes into the output's one folder after the first completed, before its next progress report (a site's zip and a book's PDF are written after their last report), replaces the first's, and the completed row then pairs its text with the second run's file. #457 keeps the first delivery's end, content and citations, and removes the second's late files where the first ended failed or skipped. Closing it needs per-delivery staging and a move after the conditional `completed` write, or T-5.2.29's longer visibility timeout or per-run lease, which makes it moot

The runbook's recovery path is a backup of `data/chroma/`, and #451 set aside an override flag such as `--other-collection-ok NAME`; whether one is wanted is the owner's call

#### S-5.2.1 🟢 Record usage per user: LLM tokens by feature and YouTube Data API units
**Filed and shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#391](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/391) (batch 3 of 2026-11-03). Linked decision: [D-119](decisions.md#d-119--usage-is-metered-at-a-scope-that-names-the-tenant-and-written-in-a-session-of-its-own-2026-11-04). It had no board entry before. D-111 dropped the daily-token line from the pricing page because nothing measured it; this measures it and limits nothing.
- [x] T-5.2.1.1 `usage_records` (migration `6cf03630d943`, additive): one row per (tenant, UTC day, feature, metric), with metrics `llm_tokens_in`, `llm_tokens_out`, `llm_calls` and `youtube_units`
- [x] T-5.2.1.2 The metering scope `usage_service.metered(db, tenant_id, feature=None)`. `byok_context` opens one; `get_llm_for` attaches a LangChain callback to every client; `quota_service.record` charges each YouTube request's units to the open scope, while the install-wide `api_quota_log` and `/health/quota` are unchanged. A call's feature comes from `USE_CASE_FEATURES` (the whole registry, test-enforced) unless an entry point names one, and the outermost wins. YouTube units go by kind of call: `youtube_search` (every `search.list`), `video_metadata`, `channel_sync`
