The hand-written token recording in the job Q&A router and `suggestion_service` is removed, so nothing is counted twice; the per-exchange token columns stay
- [x] T-5.2.1.4 The meter writes through its own session and never commits or rolls back the caller's; when the caller's transaction has written, it waits for commit, rollback or close (a review fix). Daily totals also go to `quota_usage`, so `GET /auth/quota` counts every feature
- [x] T-5.2.1.5 `GET /api/v1/usage`: the caller's own usage today and over the last 30 UTC days, by feature, with totals and `metered_since`. "Your usage" on the Subscription page, with an empty state and a note that nothing there is limited
- [x] T-5.2.1.6 Found on the way: concurrent report map batches (S-1.14.19) ran on pool threads with an empty context, ignoring the user's BYOK key and model overrides. `_map_in_context` runs each batch in a copy of the caller's context
- Tests: `test_usage_metering.py` (18), `test_usage_metering_tasks.py` (11) and `test_usage_service.py` (28), at the production callers with only the network replaced, mutation-checked, six of them pinned as `test_mutation_*`; `subscriptionPage.test.tsx` for the table. **Owner step:** restart the API, the worker and beat; the API applies the migration at startup. Nothing to backfill. **Owner step done 2026-11-04:** migration `6cf03630d943` applied and the services restarted.

### E-5.3 ⚪ Stripe integration

**Scope.** Subscription, metered overage, team billing.

**Design-complete 2026-05-04.** Full design lives in [`docs/saas-roadmap.md` §3](saas-roadmap.md#3-billing) — provider, customer / subscription objects, webhook endpoint shape, metered-overage rules, self-host kill switch (`BILLING_ENABLED=false`). **Code deferred until SaaS launch is funded** — self-host has no use for billing (everyone's tier is operator-controlled), so shipping Stripe today buys nothing while costing maintenance. Schema fields (`tenants.billing_customer_id`, `tenants.billing_subscription_id`) will land alongside the `tenants` table when T-5.1.3 ships.

**Tasks**
- [ ] T-5.3.1 ⚪ **Replace mock-payment with Stripe Checkout (resolves D-050).** When SaaS launches, swap `PUT /api/v1/auth/me/tier`'s implementation from "trust the request" to "verify Stripe webhook signature on `checkout.session.completed` → update tier". Frontend swaps the `MockPaymentModal` for a Stripe Checkout redirect; the rest of the in-app `/account/subscription` UI is unchanged (TierCard grid, downgrade modal, capability-aware nav). Self-host operators retain the existing mock-payment path via `ENABLE_SELF_SERVICE_TIER_FLIP=true` flag (default in SaaS: `false`). Blocked-on the rest of E-5.3 landing first (Stripe customer/subscription objects, webhook endpoint, kill switch).

A deferred task does not reopen the epic, as E-5.5 keeps its deferred work.

**Scope-changed 2026-10-25:** 🟡 → 🟢. T-5.4.9 shipped in PR [#232](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/232) on 2026-10-19 with `tests/test_services/test_jwt_pinning.py` (alg `none`, other HMAC variants, wrong secret and expiry all rejected); every task under this epic is closed again. The reopening note below is kept for the audit trail.

**Scope-changed 2026-10-19:** 🟢 → 🟡. Research for M11 found that `python-jose` 3.5.0 (the latest release, 2025-05-28) carries CVE-2026-85394 (CVSS 9.3, published 2026-09-03, no fixed version): a DER-encoded public key is accepted as an HMAC secret, an incomplete fix of CVE-2024-33663. The app decodes JWTs with it in `auth_service.py`, `mfa_service.py` and `middleware/rate_limit.py`. All three sites pin `algorithms=[settings.JWT_ALGORITHM]`, which neutralises the known confusion path, but the dependency is unfixed and effectively unmaintained.
- [x] T-5.4.9 🟢 **Shipped 2026-10-19 — PR [#232](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/232).** Found on the way: the dev `.env` had `JWT_SECRET=` blank and python-jose had been signing every token with an empty HMAC key; PyJWT refuses, and a config validator now maps blank to the dev default with a loud warning. Replace `python-jose` with `PyJWT` (MIT, actively released) at the three call sites, keep `algorithms` pinned, keep the `jti` session semantics; one PR, tests unchanged. M11 share tokens must never be JWTs regardless

**Scope.** OAuth (Google / GitHub), MFA, session management, password reset, account lockout, audit log.

**Phase 1 shipped 2026-05-04.** The defensive primitives — credential-stuffing defence (lockout), self-service recovery (password reset), and observability (audit log) — ship in one PR. Remaining auth-hardening work is OAuth and MFA, deferred to dedicated PRs since each has substantial provider-specific complexity.

- **Audit log** — new `audit_log` table + `app/services/audit_service.py` with the canonical `Event` enum (USER_REGISTERED / LOGIN_SUCCESS / LOGIN_FAILURE / LOGIN_LOCKED_OUT / ACCOUNT_LOCKED / PASSWORD_RESET_REQUESTED / PASSWORD_RESET_COMPLETED / PASSWORD_RESET_INVALID_TOKEN). Recorded on every auth event with IP, user-agent, structured metadata. `GET /api/v1/auth/audit-log` returns the current user's events (newest first; capped at 500/page).
- **Account lockout** — `users.failed_login_attempts INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` + `users.locked_until DATETIME NULL`. After `LOCKOUT_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` (default 5) failures, the account is locked for `LOCKOUT_DURATION_MIN` (default 15) minutes. Successful login resets both columns. **Unknown emails do NOT create User rows** (a critical-correctness check — otherwise an attacker could lock arbitrary accounts by trying any email). `authenticate_user_v2` returns a structured `AuthOutcome` so the router can audit lockouts separately from invalid creds while still serving a generic 401.
- **Password reset** — new `password_reset_tokens` table (single-use, SHA-256 hashed; raw secret never stored). `POST /api/v1/auth/password-reset/request` returns 200 unconditionally (never leaks whether the email exists); on self-host the secret is returned in `debug_secret` and logged so operators can hand it off out-of-band when SMTP isn't configured. `POST /api/v1/auth/password-reset/confirm` rotates the password + clears any active lockout. Tokens expire after `PASSWORD_RESET_TOKEN_TTL_MIN` (default 30) minutes.

22 new tests; backend suite 801 → 823.

**Tasks**

New tables `oauth_states` (10 min TTL, single-use, S256 PKCE verifier persisted) + `oauth_identities` (`(provider, provider_user_id)` unique). New endpoints: `GET /auth/oauth/providers`, `GET /auth/oauth/{provider}/start`, `GET /auth/oauth/{provider}/callback`. First OAuth login finds-or-creates the User by email; existing-email users get the identity linked to their existing account (no duplicates). All callback failures return generic 401 (don't leak which step failed). 14 new tests with mocked provider responses; backend suite 915 → 929. **E-5.4 fully closed** — all 8 tasks done. **Linked decision:** [D-042](decisions.md#d-042--oauth-first-login-links-to-existing-user-by-email-2026-05-05) — email-based linking trade-off + provider verification expectations.*
- [x] T-5.4.6 MFA (TOTP) — *shipped 2026-05-05. RFC 6238 TOTP via `pyotp`. New `mfa_secrets` table (encrypted secret + JSON-hashed recovery codes; Alembic `d1e2f3a4b5c6_mfa_secrets_table.py`). Endpoints: `POST /auth/mfa/enroll` (returns `secret` + `provisioning_uri` for QR rendering), `POST /auth/mfa/verify-enrollment` (validates first TOTP code → enabled=True; returns 10 single-use recovery codes ONCE), `GET /auth/mfa/status`, `DELETE /auth/mfa` (requires valid TOTP / recovery code), `POST /auth/login/mfa` (second-step using short-lived 5-min mfa_token from /auth/login). Login flow change: when MFA is enabled, `/auth/login` returns `MfaRequiredResponse` instead of `TokenResponse`; client must call `/auth/login/mfa` with the returned mfa_token + a code to receive the real access token. ±1 TOTP window for clock skew. Encryption reuses BYOK Fernet key (see [D-043](decisions.md#d-043--single-shared-fernet-key-for-all-encrypted-at-rest-credentials-2026-05-05) for the single-shared-key rationale). 13 new tests; backend suite 902 → 915.* *2026-11-04 (S-5.4.1, [D-121](decisions.md#d-121--reset-codes-never-appear-in-a-response-two-step-verification-guards-every-sign-in-and-tokens-say-what-they-are-2026-11-04)): the second-step ticket carries `typ: "mfa_step"` and authenticates nothing but `/auth/login/mfa`; before, it worked as an access token for its five minutes. OAuth sign-ins now stop at the same step.*
- [x] T-5.4.7 Session management (revoke individual sessions, list active sessions, logout everywhere) — *shipped 2026-05-05. New `sessions` table keyed on JWT `jti` claim (Alembic `c0d1e2f3a4b5_sessions_table.py`); login writes a row + captures IP/User-Agent; `dependencies.get_current_user` validates the row is not revoked on every authenticated request. New endpoints: `GET /auth/sessions`, `DELETE /auth/sessions/{jti}` (404 on cross-user — existence-leak posture), `DELETE /auth/sessions?keep_current=true|false` (logout everywhere with the "keep current" UX), `POST /auth/logout`. Revocation is via `revoked_at` timestamp (audit trail preserved). Pre-T-5.4.7 tokens (no jti claim, no session row) keep working until they expire — back-compat. 12 new tests; backend suite 890 → 902.*
- [x] T-5.4.8 SMTP integration (deliver password-reset secrets via email) — *shipped 2026-05-05. New `app/services/email_service.py` with pluggable SMTP backend (host / port / username / password / SSL / STARTTLS / from-address config) + log-fallback when `SMTP_HOST` unset. Password-reset endpoint now: (a) renders email via `render_password_reset_email`, (b) sends via `email_service.send_email` (SMTP when configured, log otherwise), (c) returns `debug_secret` ONLY when SMTP is unconfigured (self-host operator handoff). On SaaS / SMTP-configured deployments, the secret is never in the response — email is the only delivery channel. 11 new tests; backend suite 879 → 890.* *Superseded 2026-11-04 by S-5.4.1 (PR [#389](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/389), [D-121](decisions.md#d-121--reset-codes-never-appear-in-a-response-two-step-verification-guards-every-sign-in-and-tokens-say-what-they-are-2026-11-04)): `debug_secret` is gone. With SMTP unset the reset code goes only to the server log, and the email names the `/reset-password` page.*

Linked decision: [D-116](decisions.md#d-116--oauth-links-or-creates-an-account-only-from-a-provider-verified-email-and-the-link-records-it-2026-10-29), which amends [D-042](decisions.md#d-042--oauth-first-login-links-to-existing-user-by-email-2026-05-05) by adopting its option (B'). A first OAuth link or a new OAuth account needs a provider-verified email: Google's `email_verified` claim (missing counts as unverified), GitHub's primary address in `GET /user/emails` with `verified: true` (the `/user` public email is never trusted; `user:email` was already in the scope). The refusal is a 403 with one plain message whether or not an account has the address, checked before any lookup, and audited as `login_failure` with `reason: oauth_email_unverified`. A failed `/user/emails` lookup is the generic, retryable 401 (a review fix), and a returning user matched by provider id is unaffected. The link records the verification in the new nullable `oauth_identities.email_verified_at` (migration `066d3f4b4d4d`; NULL marks earlier links). `docs/api-reference.md` documents the three OAuth endpoints. No frontend change: there is no OAuth sign-in UI yet. Tests: `test_oauth.py` (verified-email section) and `test_operator_gate.py`, mutation-checked. **Owner step done 2026-11-03:** the services were restarted on master and the migration applied.
- [ ] T-5.4.11 🔴 *(filed 2026-10-29 by the review of T-5.4.10, PR [#384](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/384); **deferred 2026-11-03** by the owner: "keep email verification for later")* **Pre-account takeover through password sign-up.** `POST /auth/register` verifies no email, so someone can register a password account with another person's address before that person uses the app. When the real owner later signs in with Google or GitHub, the address is verified, so the identity links to that account by email ([D-042](decisions.md#d-042--oauth-first-login-links-to-existing-user-by-email-2026-05-05), [D-116](decisions.md#d-116--oauth-links-or-creates-an-account-only-from-a-provider-verified-email-and-the-link-records-it-2026-10-29)), and the registrant keeps the password and can read what the owner adds. Two options, the owner's call when this is picked up: (a) OAuth never links automatically to an existing password account; or (b) on link, the account's password is cleared and its sessions are revoked (the reset flow, which proves the address, sets a new one; this ends password sign-in for people who registered honestly and then add Google). Email verification at sign-up would close it at the source and is deferred with it. `ADMIN_EMAILS` addresses are already safe: registration refuses them (T-5.2.8)

- [x] T-5.4.13 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #389)* Cap the attempts per second-factor ticket. `/auth/login/mfa` has only the per-IP limit for signed-out callers, so one five-minute ticket can be tried from many addresses
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#399](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/399) (batch 4 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-124](decisions.md#d-124--second-factor-attempts-count-on-the-ticket-one-bearer-check-for-http-and-the-socket-and-a-validation-error-never-quotes-what-was-sent-2026-11-04). The ticket now has a `jti`, and `POST /auth/login/mfa` counts every code against it in the new table `mfa_step_tickets` (migration `f748717dcb01`), never against the IP. The fifth miss and every try after it are refused, even for the right code, and the sign-in page goes back to the first step. Beyond the spec, a ticket buys one session, and a ticket with no `jti` is refused rather than left uncounted. The increment is one conditional UPDATE, so two racing requests cannot both take the last try
- [x] T-5.4.14 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #389)* Change the password while signed in. There is no endpoint, so the Security page links to the reset flow instead
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#399](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/399) (batch 4 of 2026-11-04). It has its own per-IP bucket at the sign-in rate and answers 400 or 429, never 401, because the web client signs out on a 401. A new password bcrypt cannot take (a NUL character or a lone surrogate) gets a 400. New column `users.has_password` marks the accounts Google or GitHub created, which get a 400 pointing to "Forgot it?"; the backfill marks an account whose first identity came within 60 seconds of it, with no completed reset and no `user_registered` event (on the owner's database, 2 users and 0 marked). A Password card on `/account/security` replaces the link to the reset flow. From the review: FastAPI's 422 quoted the rejected body, so a handler in `app/main.py` now drops `input` from every validation error on every route

- [x] T-5.4.16 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #399)* `DELETE /auth/mfa` has no attempt cap. It checks a code too, and only the per-user bucket limits it, so a stolen session can guess at up to 6,000 a minute. It takes no ticket, so T-5.4.13 does not cover it
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#411](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/411) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-138](decisions.md#d-138--code-attempts-count-on-a-shared-row-sockets-end-with-their-session-one-reader-takes-the-client-address-from-the-right-and-a-rotated-key-is-reported-2026-11-05). `DELETE /auth/mfa` reuses T-5.4.13's count on a `turn-off:{user_id}` row in `mfa_step_tickets`, so there is no migration: five codes per user per 15 minutes, the window fixed at the first code. The fifth miss and everything after it answer 429 with no code checked, even the right one. A right code clears the count, sign-in is untouched, and a refused code is recorded as `mfa_disable_failed`, up to the cap. From the review: `POST /auth/mfa/verify-enrollment` never checked `row.enabled`, so it was an uncounted way to guess the live secret, and a match replaced the recovery codes. It now answers 409 before any code is checked. With the encryption key broken, a code that cannot be a recovery code checked nothing, so its attempt goes back (`refund_disable_attempt`), and a recovery-shaped miss stays counted. **Behaviour change:** a user whose first confirm succeeded but whose response was lost can no longer get recovery codes by confirming again. They turn two-step off and set it up afresh.

Failed second factors could count toward the account lockout, which changes who gets locked out

  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#411](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/411) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-138](decisions.md#d-138--code-attempts-count-on-a-shared-row-sockets-end-with-their-session-one-reader-takes-the-client-address-from-the-right-and-a-rotated-key-is-reported-2026-11-05). Measured first: a NUL was a bare 500 on register and on reset with a good code, and at login a 401 with bcrypt's exception in the log. A lone surrogate was already a 422 on these routes. Register and reset now refuse a NUL with the fixed 400 the change route uses, checked first so the reset code is not spent, and `verify_password` refuses such a password before bcrypt sees it, so login is a plain 401 that logs nothing. `ResetPasswordPage` said "code not valid" for every 400 and now matches the code's own detail.
- [x] T-5.4.19 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #397 and #399)* Close a socket that is already open when its session is revoked. A session revoked from another device keeps its socket until it closes. The page's next HTTP call gets a 401 and signs out, this browser's own sign-out closes its socket (T-8.3.3.8), and the notification doorbell carries no text, so such a socket learns only that something arrived
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#411](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/411) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-138](decisions.md#d-138--code-attempts-count-on-a-shared-row-sockets-end-with-their-session-one-reader-takes-the-client-address-from-the-right-and-a-rotated-key-is-reported-2026-11-05). Every revocation path (`revoke_session`, `revoke_all_sessions`, `confirm_password_reset`, `change_password`) publishes the `jti`s on `session_revoked:{tenant_id}` after its commit, and each API process closes the sockets it holds on those sessions with 1008. Pub/sub keeps nothing, so the manager also reads the sessions table once a minute while a socket is open, which is what closes it with Redis down. Publishes are bounded at two seconds, closes run outside the listener loop, and the web client does not reconnect after a 1008. End to end on real uvicorn processes: a revocation on one process closed a socket on another in 20 ms or less, and with no Redis the socket closed at 57 seconds. A password reset closing a socket is covered at the router only.

It honours `state.from` with its query and fragment, for password, two-step and OAuth; OAuth keeps the page in the tab's `sessionStorage` for the trip. Honouring it reliably needed two more changes: a sign-out, here or in another tab, leaves no `from` (`signedOut`), and the marketing **Sign in** carries no page, so the onboarding gate decides. The query cache now clears in `AuthProvider` whenever a token goes away. The share pickup at `/` is unchanged. `shell.spec.ts` covers what jsdom cannot, including two real tabs.

- [x] T-5.4.22 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-05, from #414)* A session that expires while a page is open still ends at `/`, because the 401 interceptor does a hard `window.location.href = '/login'` with no `from`. This is probably the most common real deep link, such as an old tab or an email link after 24 hours
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#423](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/423) (batch 7 of 2026-11-05). Linked decision: [D-148](decisions.md#d-148--a-401-ends-a-session-in-react-and-keeps-the-page-in-this-tab-a-waiting-share-gets-a-link-a-mutation-that-answers-its-own-failures-says-so-and-tabs-opens-on-focus-2026-11-05). It amends [D-140](decisions.md#d-140--a-sign-in-makes-one-redirect-the-query-cache-ends-with-the-session-the-error-screen-replaces-the-page-and-signed-out-pricing-leads-to-free-2026-11-05)'s consequence. The interceptor no longer navigates: `services/sessionExpiry.ts` removes the token and tells `AuthProvider`, `ProtectedRoute` goes to `/login` with `state.from`, and `PublicOnlyRoute` comes back through `signInReturn`'s checks. A 401 counts only for the token it refused, and never on the sign-in calls or `/auth/logout`; one during a sign-out counts as the sign-out. The streaming ask, which uses `fetch`, ends a session the same way, and neither it nor the global toast says anything of a 401. A 401 that finds no token in storage ends a session this tab still holds, as a sign-out. With no reload, the session-end effect also clears the Echo thread and the toasts, which fixes a sign-out leaving the last account's Echo thread behind. Across tabs, the tab that met the 401 keeps its page and another gives its page up (D-140 point 2, OQ-41); `shell.spec.ts` drives this in two real tabs. A page restored from the back/forward cache finds out at its next request. No real expired JWT was used.

Taken into batch 7 by the main session, because it is a credential in a log; the owner may reverse it.*
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#425](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/425) (batch 7 of 2026-11-05). Linked decision: [D-151](decisions.md#d-151--the-sockets-token-is-a-subprotocol-provider-errors-are-scrubbed-as-each-log-record-is-made-and-json-logs-hold-their-format-from-the-first-line-2026-11-09). `wsClient.ts` opens `new WebSocket(url, ['bearer', token])`. `/ws/jobs` reads the token from the handshake's subprotocol list, authenticates it exactly as before, and answers `bearer` alone, so the token is never reflected. `?token=` is retired, and `test_url_tokens.py` holds every route, the socket included, to no credential in a URL. A filter on `uvicorn.error` and `uvicorn.access` (`app/utils/log_redaction.py`) redacts credential query values and anything shaped like a JWT, under both log formats and at `--log-level debug`, proven with the real uvicorn command line. Checked once in Chromium and WebKit against a scratch API. **Owner steps:** restart the services and reload open tabs; delete `.uvicorn.log`.

#### S-5.4.1 🟢 Account security page: sessions, two-step verification, sign-out, password reset, account activity
**Filed and shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#389](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/389) (batch 3 of 2026-11-03). Linked decision: [D-121](decisions.md#d-121--reset-codes-never-appear-in-a-response-two-step-verification-guards-every-sign-in-and-tokens-say-what-they-are-2026-11-04). The UI for T-5.4.1, T-5.4.3, T-5.4.5, T-5.4.6 and T-5.4.7, which shipped backend-only in May 2026; it had no board entry before.
- [x] T-5.4.1.1 `/account/security` (Account menu "Security", and a palette command): active sessions only, this device first, sign out one or every other; two-step setup (the key in groups of four and the `otpauth://` link, each copyable, no QR code; a first code turns it on; ten recovery codes shown once) and turning it off with a code; a link to the reset flow; account activity, 20 a page. The key and the codes live only in the card's state and survive a failed background refetch (a review fix)
- [x] T-5.4.1.2 Sign-in: the second-factor step (authenticator or recovery code, `POST /auth/login/mfa`), with its own messages for a wrong code and an expired step; "Forgot your password?"; OAuth buttons only for the providers `GET /auth/oauth/providers` names, returning to `/login/oauth/:provider/callback`, which exchanges `code` and `state` once and drops them from the address bar. A 401 on a public sign-in page stays on the page
- [x] T-5.4.1.3 Sign-out calls `POST /auth/logout` first (abandoned after 5 s), then clears local state and the React Query cache; a failed call still signs the browser out
- [x] T-5.4.1.4 `/forgot-password` and `/reset-password`. The request page shows only its own text, and the reset page never reads a code from the URL. The reset email names the bare `{APP_BASE_URL}/reset-password` page
- [x] T-5.4.1.5 Server: `POST /auth/password-reset/request` no longer returns `debug_secret` (an account takeover and an address oracle on any install without SMTP); an OAuth sign-in to an account with two-step on stops at the second factor; `GET /auth/sessions` returns `active` and `expires_at`; `GET /auth/audit-log?scope=account` reads `audit_service.ACCOUNT_EVENTS`
- [x] T-5.4.1.6 Review blocker, older than this PR: the second-factor ticket worked as a bearer token for its five minutes. Tokens now carry `typ` (`access` or `mfa_step`), and every bearer check, the `/ws/jobs` handshake included, decodes through `auth_service.decode_access_token`; tokens issued before the claim still work
- Tests: `test_account_security.py`, `test_auth_hardening.py`, `test_jwt_pinning.py` and `test_email_service.py`, and vitest `signInFlows`, `accountSecurity` and `authSignIn`, all mutation-checked. The routes live in `frontend/src/routes/index.tsx`. **Owner steps:** restart the backend; set `APP_BASE_URL` to the frontend's public origin; without SMTP, a reset code is only in `.uvicorn.*.log` ("Password reset request"); the OAuth buttons need `OAUTH_GOOGLE_*` / `OAUTH_GITHUB_*` and `<frontend origin>/login/oauth/<provider>/callback` registered with each provider.

### E-5.5 🟢 Abuse prevention

**Scope-changed 2026-11-05 (II):** stays 🟢. T-5.5.9 shipped in PR [#420](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/420) ([D-143](decisions.md#d-143--the-apis-content-security-policy-is-locked-by-default-oauth-redirects-are-compared-as-origins-a-socket-ends-at-its-tokens-expiry-and-a-shared-link-is-logged-by-host-alone-2026-11-05)).

**Scope-changed 2026-11-05:** stays 🟢. T-5.5.8 shipped in PR [#411](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/411) ([D-138](decisions.md#d-138--code-attempts-count-on-a-shared-row-sockets-end-with-their-session-one-reader-takes-the-client-address-from-the-right-and-a-rotated-key-is-reported-2026-11-05)), so the per-IP limits count a real address. T-5.5.9 is proposed.

**Scope.** Rate limits, fraud detection, content policy, takedown process for shared reports.

**Phase 1 shipped 2026-05-04.** Rate-limit middleware enforces per-route + per-tier caps via an in-memory sliding-window counter. Fraud detection / content policy / takedown remain ⚪ (each is a SaaS-launch concern).

- **`backend/app/services/rate_limit_service.py`** — sliding-window counter with `RateLimit(requests, window_sec)` config + `check_and_consume(key, limit) -> (allowed, count, retry_after_sec)`. Thread-safe via `threading.Lock`; auto-prunes expired buckets. In-memory by design (single-process self-host); Redis-backed swap is one-function for SaaS multi-worker deployment per the docstring.
- **`backend/app/middleware/rate_limit.py`** — FastAPI middleware applied early. Three-tier strategy: (1) sensitive endpoints (`/auth/login`, `/auth/password-reset/{request,confirm}`, `/auth/register`) get tight per-IP buckets that fire BEFORE auth so they defend against credential-stuffing / brute-force / mass-reset attacks; (2) authenticated routes consume from per-user buckets sized by tier (Free/Pro/Studio scale 60/600/6000 req/min); (3) unauthenticated GETs use a per-IP fallback. Returns `429` with `Retry-After`, `X-RateLimit-Limit`, `X-RateLimit-Remaining`. Disabled in tests via `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED=False` (set in conftest); individual tests opt back in.
- **JWT pre-parse for bucket key** — middleware decodes the bearer token to extract user_id without a DB lookup; stable bucket key without slowing the request path.
- **Config knobs** — `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED`, `RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN_FREE/PRO/STUDIO`, `RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN_UNAUTH`, `RATE_LIMIT_LOGIN_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_RESET_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_REGISTER_PER_MIN`.

12 new tests; backend suite 823 → 835.

**Tasks**
- [x] T-5.5.1 In-memory sliding-window rate-limit service — *shipped 2026-05-04. See [D-039](decisions.md#d-039--in-memory-rate-limit-backend-as-the-default-redis-swap-deferred-to-multi-worker-saas-2026-05-04) for the in-memory-vs-Redis design decision.*
- [x] T-5.5.2 FastAPI middleware with per-route + per-tier strategy — *shipped 2026-05-04*.

In-memory backend is correct for single-worker self-host; swapping to Redis prematurely is gold-plating without a consumer.*
- [x] T-5.5.5 Quota enforcement — *shipped 2026-05-05 (combined with T-5.2.5; see that entry for full detail). 429 with `Retry-After` header + structured `detail` body (`{error, resource, consumed, limit, retry_after_sec, retry_at}`) on cap-exceeded. enforce_quota_or_raise runs BEFORE the expensive agent at every hot endpoint.*

Blocks on [M11 public report sharing](feature-roadmap.md#m11--public-report-sharing-) — there's no shared-report surface to apply takedown logic to until M11 ships.* *2026-10-25 ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): M11 is deferred too. If sharing is ever built, the takedown and revoke hooks are built inside S-8.11.2, which breaks the circular dependency.*
- [ ] T-5.5.7 🔴 Fraud detection (anomalous-pattern alerting) — *deferred. Needs real production traffic + accumulated abuse signal data before pattern definitions are useful. Premature without it.*
- [x] T-5.5.8 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #399)* The per-IP limits trust `X-Forwarded-For`: `_client_ip` in the rate limiter reads the header from any caller, so a client that sends a new value each time is never limited, as #399's end-to-end run did. It needs a trusted-proxy setting. Until then the ticket cap and the account lockout are the real bounds, not the bucket
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#411](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/411) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-138](decisions.md#d-138--code-attempts-count-on-a-shared-row-sockets-end-with-their-session-one-reader-takes-the-client-address-from-the-right-and-a-rotated-key-is-reported-2026-11-05). New setting `TRUSTED_PROXY_IPS`, default empty, and `app/utils/client_ip.py` as the one reader of the header and the peer, used by the rate limiter, the audit log and the address on a session; a test pins that nothing else reads them. Empty: the socket peer. Peer trusted: every line of the header is walked from the right and the first address that is not trusted wins. A malformed header or peer falls back to the peer. uvicorn's own proxy handling rewrites the peer for callers at 127.0.0.1 before the app runs, which would have kept #399's bypass alive on a local install, so `scripts/start.ps1` and `restart_services.ps1` pass `--no-proxy-headers`. End to end at the sign-in limit with a rotating header: 429 with the flag, and 14 of 14 let through under uvicorn's default. The README has a new section, "Running behind a reverse proxy". No real proxy has been put in front of the app.
- [x] T-5.5.9 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-05, from #411)* Proxy edges T-5.5.8 left out: IPv6 callers are bucketed per address, not per /64, and `1.2.3.4:5678` in the header reads as malformed, so a proxy that appends a port would read as the proxy
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#420](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/420) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-143](decisions.md#d-143--the-apis-content-security-policy-is-locked-by-default-oauth-redirects-are-compared-as-origins-a-socket-ends-at-its-tokens-expiry-and-a-shared-link-is-logged-by-host-alone-2026-11-05). `rate_limit_bucket` counts IPv6 per /64 for the limiter only, so the audit log and the session keep the full address, without the port. `1.2.3.4:5678` and `[2001:db8::1]:443` read as their address. An out-of-range port or a bracketed IPv4 is malformed, and a bare IPv6 address is never split. No real reverse proxy was used.

**Scope.** Celery queues per tenant or per tier; per-tenant LLM keys (BYOK pattern, reuse from D-009); per-tenant ChromaDB tenancy.

**BYOK foundation shipped 2026-05-04.** Per-user, per-provider API keys with encryption-at-rest. Power users on the Studio tier can route their LLM calls to their own provider account.

- **`backend/app/models/user_credential.py`** — `(user_id, provider)` unique; `encrypted_secret Text` (Fernet ciphertext, plaintext never persisted); `label`, `created_at`, `updated_at`.
- **`backend/app/services/byok_service.py`** — `cryptography.fernet.Fernet`-based encrypt/decrypt keyed off `BYOK_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. CRUD: `set_credential`, `get_credential`, `list_for_user`, `delete_credential`. Provider validation against `SUPPORTED_PROVIDERS` (`openai` / `anthropic` / `google` / `local` — matches `llm_routing.py` provider table). Encryption-key rotation tolerance: `get_credential` returns `None` (with warning) when ciphertext is undecryptable so the consumer falls back to install-wide env-var keys.
- **`backend/app/routers/credentials.py`** — REST endpoints under `/api/v1/auth/credentials/*`, gated on `require_feature("byok_llm_keys")` (Studio-tier only). PUT for upsert; GET for list (metadata only — never the plaintext); DELETE for removal; `/providers` for the supported set.
- **20 new tests** covering encryption round-trip + non-determinism, CRUD upsert semantics, cross-user isolation, provider validation, key-rotation tolerance, auth + tier gating on every endpoint, plaintext-not-leaked invariant.

Backend suite 835 → 855.

- [x] T-5.6.11 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #399)* A rotated key is still not reported. With a valid key that cannot read the stored secret (`InvalidToken`), a TOTP code reads as a wrong code. T-5.6.9 is about a malformed key, and rotation is a different failure
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#411](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/411) (batch 5 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-138](decisions.md#d-138--code-attempts-count-on-a-shared-row-sockets-end-with-their-session-one-reader-takes-the-client-address-from-the-right-and-a-rotated-key-is-reported-2026-11-05). New `StoredSecretUnreadableError`, a subclass of `EncryptionConfigurationError`, so every path that handles a malformed key handles this too: a 503 naming the cause, with recovery codes still working. A rotated key, an unset key after a restart and a corrupt row read the same. The log line names the account and no exception text, so neither the secret nor the key reaches it.
- [x] T-5.6.12 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #405)* Report map batches on the pool read through the task's `Session` for the BYOK lookup (`_resolve_byok_api_key`, reached from `get_llm_for`), since T-5.2.1.6. That is concurrent use of one `Session`, and the lookup falls back to the install's key if it raises
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#417](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/417) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-146](decisions.md#d-146--a-pool-never-carries-the-session-max_concurrent_jobs-is-per-user-a-key-check-says-good-or-bad-only-when-the-provider-did-and-whispers-key-and-count-hold-on-every-sync-2026-11-05). `_map_in_context` opens `llm_service.byok_keys_for_pool()`, which reads every provider's key once on the task's thread and takes the session out of the context the threads copy. The tenant stays, for the meter, and `get_llm_for` finds the key already read. A mutation test on a real pool shows the same run landing on the install's key without it.
- [x] T-5.6.13 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-04, from #405)* The YouTube fallback and podcast audio always use the install's `OPENAI_API_KEY`, while Echo recordings use the tenant's BYOK key. The seconds are recorded either way, as tokens are
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#417](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/417) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-146](decisions.md#d-146--a-pool-never-carries-the-session-max_concurrent_jobs-is-per-user-a-key-check-says-good-or-bad-only-when-the-provider-did-and-whispers-key-and-count-hold-on-every-sync-2026-11-05). `llm_service.whisper_api_key` is the one rule for the YouTube fallback, podcast audio and Echo recordings: the tenant's key when they brought one, else the install's, with no fallback from a refused tenant key. `_run_extraction_and_rag` opens `byok_context` around `fetch_text`, or a sync would have stayed on the install's key. The podcast gate and the template warning count the tenant's key too, and `whisper_error_text` names auth errors by type alone, because a failure line can now hold a tenant's key. **Beyond the task (OQ-36):** a sync passed no Whisper budget, so it now holds to `WHISPER_MAX_PER_JOB` on every key and says so in a `whisper_budget_spent` run note. That reverses what #415 reported; `0` now does turn Whisper off on syncs.
- [x] T-5.6.14 🟢 *(filed and shipped 2026-11-05, from #417)* `langchain-anthropic` was never declared in `requirements.txt`, though Q&A answers default to Anthropic, so on a clean install every Anthropic call would fail at the client. CI showed it; the dev venv has it. A clean install resolves `anthropic` 2.5.0 against the dev venv's 0.120.0, and the tests pass on both, against a loopback provider only.
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#417](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/417) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). **Owner step:** reinstall requirements on any clean environment.
- [ ] T-5.6.15 ⚪ *(proposed 2026-11-05, from #417; an owner step)* Press **Verify key** once per provider. `gpt-5.6-luna`, `claude-haiku-4-5` and `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, the check models, were never tried live. If a good key says "Not checked" and it is not limited to certain models, change that entry in `llm_service.KEY_CHECK_MODELS`
- [x] T-5.6.16 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-05, from #417)* Model-call error logs outside Whisper can still carry a refused key's fragment. Older than #417 and not limited to these paths
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#425](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/425) (batch 7 of 2026-11-05). Linked decision: [D-151](decisions.md#d-151--the-sockets-token-is-a-subprotocol-provider-errors-are-scrubbed-as-each-log-record-is-made-and-json-logs-hold-their-format-from-the-first-line-2026-11-09). #417's Whisper rule is generalised as `log_redaction.provider_error_text`: a refusal from `openai`, `anthropic` or `google`, or anything it caused, goes by its type, and any other text has the shapes of a key taken out (`scrub_keys`). A log-record factory applies it to every record in every process that imports `app`, tracebacks from `logger.exception` included, and to the text a dict, list, tuple or bytes argument will be logged as. `ProbeResult.error` is scrubbed where it is made, so `/health/llm` and the probe's failure line carry no fragment. It also closes a leak nobody had filed: a YouTube `HttpError` holds the install's `YOUTUBE_API_KEY` in its URL. Not covered: anything that is not a log record, such as stored rows (T-5.6.17) and Celery's result backend. No real provider refused a key in the tests.
- [x] T-5.6.17 🟢 *(proposed 2026-11-09, from #425)* Provider exception text still reaches stored rows and messages, which are not logs: `jobs.error_message` and the socket's error event (`_handle_failure(db, job_id, str(e))`, four call sites), `report_agent`'s map notes in `job_report_intermediates` and its "Channel narrative generation failed: {e}" in a report, and `outputs.error_message`. A tenant can read some of them, and the key quoted can be the install's. `provider_error_text(e)` is the fix at each
  **Shipped:** 2026-11-09 — PR [#430](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/430) (batch 8 of 2026-11-09). Linked decision: [D-155](decisions.md#d-155--stored-exception-text-goes-through-error_summary-an-empty-whisper-answer-is-charged-a-refused-dispatch-fails-a-new-run-and-leaves-an-approval-waiting-and-a-start-the-queue-refused-uses-up-nothing-2026-11-09). New `log_redaction.error_summary(exc, limit=None)` returns `Class: words`, the words through `provider_error_text`, so a key's shapes are taken out and a provider's refusal, or anything it caused, goes by its class alone; the limit applies after the scrub. It is used at the four `_handle_failure` calls in `job_tasks.py` (`jobs.error_message` and the socket's error event; `_handle_failure` also scrubs whatever it is given), `report_agent`'s map notes and its channel-narrative failure line, now HTML-escaped too, `output_service`'s `outputs.error_message` and skipped message, `book_cohesion._call`'s record error (the output's usage, the book's notes, the deck's outline failure, the newsletter's featured note), Author Studio's queue failure, a saved search's `last_error` and run `error`, the scheduler's `last_error` and `connector_dispatch`'s errors. Every other site that stores error text was reviewed and stores only fixed sentences, our own exception text or a type name; the PR lists each. As a result `jobs.error_message` from an exception now starts with its class name (OQ-43). Tested at each production caller with fake exceptions quoting a key in every shape `provider_error_text` knows. Celery's result backend still keeps a failed task's traceback in Redis.

#### S-5.6.1 🟢 BYOK keys panel and a system status page
**Filed and shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#388](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/388) (batch 3 of 2026-11-03). Linked decision: [D-118](decisions.md#d-118--a-provider-key-is-stored-only-for-a-provider-a-call-reads-it-for-the-settings-report-the-encryption-keys-state-never-the-key-2026-11-04). The UI for T-5.6.1 to T-5.6.4, and a viewer for the health endpoints; it had no board entry before.
- [x] T-5.6.1.1 "Your API keys" on `/account/ai-models`, Studio only: one row per provider a stored key is used for (`byok_service.RESOLVED_PROVIDERS`: OpenAI, Anthropic, Google), with whether a key is set, its label and its last change. Add or replace through a key field that is emptied at submit and sent once in a direct call, never through a React Query mutation, a toast or the console; remove after a confirmation. A stored `local` key shows as "Not used" with Remove only. Free and Pro see a plan note and never call `/auth/credentials`
- [x] T-5.6.1.2 `GET /settings/llm` gains `byok: {providers, keys_persist, key_invalid}`, configuration only. The panel warns when the encryption key is unset (keys die at a restart and the worker cannot read them) or malformed (saving is off)
- [x] T-5.6.1.3 Credentials API: an over-long key gets a 400 that does not quote it (FastAPI's 422 echoed it as `input`); a malformed encryption key gives a 503 naming the cause; `PUT` refuses a provider no call reads (`local`) with a 400 while `DELETE` still accepts it; `GET /auth/credentials/providers` lists `RESOLVED_PROVIDERS`
- [x] T-5.6.1.4 The key field stays out of browser password managers: `autocomplete="one-time-code"`, no `<form>`, and Enter handled on both inputs (a review fix)
- [x] T-5.6.1.5 `/account/system` (Account menu "System status", and a palette command): `/health/llm` per use case with errors, measured warnings and a problems-only filter, `/health/scheduler` and `/health/quota`, each failing on its own. "Restart services" for operators only (`is_operator`; `require_operator` on the server), which waits out the restart before pinging `/health` (`utils/waitForApi.ts`)
- Tests: `test_byok_panel.py` and `test_credentials.py`, and vitest `byokKeysPanel`, `systemStatusPage` and `waitForApi`, mutation-checked. A live check on a scratch backend found the placeholder key in none of the DOM, browser storage, console or server logs. **Owner steps:** restart the backend; once, in Chrome or Edge on a Studio account, add a placeholder key and check that no "Save password?" prompt appears (headless tests cannot see it); set `BYOK_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (T-5.6.7) and restart the API and the worker so saved keys survive a restart.
- OQ-21 (from S-5.6.1): should `/health/llm`, `/health/scheduler` and `/health/quota` require sign-in, or be operator-only? They are unauthenticated today, and `/health/llm`'s error strings can carry provider error text. #388 did not change that exposure; the status page is open to every signed-in user because the endpoints are. The owner's call; nothing changes until it is ruled. *2026-11-09 (#425): `ProbeResult.error` is scrubbed of key shapes where it is made, so `/health/llm` no longer carries a key fragment. Who may read the endpoints is still open.*
- OQ-36 (from T-5.6.13): do you want the Whisper cap on subscription syncs? #415 told the owner `WHISPER_MAX_PER_JOB` "still does not apply to subscription jobs", to keep today's behaviour. #417 applied it the same day, on every key, because a sync had no budget and a tenant's key would have borne it. A channel with many uncaptioned videos now reads 50 recordings per sync, says so in a run note, and the next sync reads 50 more. For the old behaviour, raise `WHISPER_MAX_PER_JOB`. Capping is the recoverable side: money spent past a cap does not come back, and a capped video is read by a later sync.
- OQ-43 (from T-5.6.17): `jobs.error_message` from an exception now reads `RuntimeError: ...`, its class first, so that a refusal, which keeps only its class, still says something. That is a user-visible change. Would you rather a non-refusal show its scrubbed words alone? The owner's call.

### E-5.7 ⚪ Data residency

**Scope.** Region-selectable storage (EU / US / etc.).

**Design-complete 2026-05-04.** Full design in [`docs/saas-roadmap.md` §6 → Data residency](saas-roadmap.md#data-residency). Pure-SaaS-launch concern — a single-machine self-host install has its data in exactly one place by definition. The `tenants.region` column lands with T-5.1.3; region-specific stack provisioning is operations work, not code work. **No code change today.**

### E-5.8 ⚪ Hosting / infra

**Scope.** Postgres for SQLite, Redis cluster, ChromaDB managed or pgvector, S3 for reports, CDN for static.

**Design-complete 2026-05-04.** Full target topology in [`docs/saas-roadmap.md` §6 → SaaS (target)](saas-roadmap.md#saas-target). The matrix is the *target*; every row is a swap from the self-host equivalent. None of these swaps make sense for a single-machine self-host install. **The few code touches that ARE forward-looking** (e.g. Postgres compatibility on the SQLAlchemy layer, S3-not-local-disk for outputs) **are tracked under their respective L1/L2 epics**, not E-5.8. The scope of E-5.8 itself is *operations work* (Cloud SQL provisioning, Redis Cluster setup, CDN config), executed at SaaS launch time, not code work.

### E-5.9 ⚪ Hosted UX

**Scope.** Landing page, signup, billing portal, support, status page.

**Design-complete 2026-05-04.** Full design in [`docs/saas-roadmap.md` §7](saas-roadmap.md#7-hosted-ux). The marketing landing page already ships ([E-2.5](#e-25--marketing-landing-page-warm-editorial)) — the only piece of "hosted UX" with code today. The signup flow / billing portal / status page / docs site are all SaaS-launch-time work, gated on E-5.3 (billing) and SaaS infrastructure (E-5.8). **No code change today.**

## I-6 🟡 Author Studio (output generation L2)

**Why it exists.** The accumulated library is rich enough to produce books, sites, decks, newsletters, reels — not just answers. Closes the loop: ingest → understand → produce.
**North-star doc:** [feature-roadmap.md L2](feature-roadmap.md#l2--author-studio-output-generation-) · **Plan:** [`docs/plans/l2-author-studio.md`](plans/l2-author-studio.md) · **Sequence:** [`docs/plans/build-order.md`](plans/build-order.md)
**Decision links:** [D-044](decisions.md#d-044--foundation-first-then-concrete-implementations-deferred-for-i-3--i-6-2026-05-05), [D-069](decisions.md#d-069--shipped-backend-surfaces-ingest-as-a-library-tab-author-studio-as-one-page-readiness-as-a-gate-mean-2026-10-19), [D-093](decisions.md#d-093--book-cohesion-pass-ships-opt-in-none-stays-the-default-after-two-blind-rounds-2026-10-19)
**Scope-changed 2026-11-10:** batch 13 of 2026-11-10 changed how an output ends, filed under E-5.2 beside the run's own stop rule (T-5.2.34; PR [#457](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/457), [D-172](decisions.md#d-172--only-the-cancel-whose-own-write-took-announces-it-an-author-output-stops-at-the-write-and-keeps-nothing-when-it-ends-unfinished-a-failure-refetches-the-run-and-a-refetch-in-flight-at-an-end-is-cancelled-rather-than-out-ranked-2026-11-10)). Every output status and progress write is one conditional write, a cancel never lands on an output that had finished, and an output cancelled, failed or skipped keeps no manuscript, citations or files, so a book cancelled after its last progress report no longer serves its text. Two live deliveries of one output can still replace a completed output's files (T-5.2.36, the owner's call). I-6 stays 🟡.
**Scope-changed 2026-11-05:** T-6.1.3.7 and T-6.7.1.7, two small follow-ups, shipped in PR [#414](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/414), and the book Markdown work in PR [#421](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/421) ([D-147](decisions.md#d-147--the-rendering-parser-decides-what-is-a-heading-in-book-markdown-a-rewrite-parses-what-it-will-emit-and-the-suites-tiktoken-file-lives-at-a-fixed-path-2026-11-05), T-8.8.6.2) closes a way answers could start EPUB files. I-6 stays 🟡 for the deferred reel (E-6.5).
**Scope-changed 2026-11-04:** S-6.3.2 shipped in PR [#398](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/398) ([D-126](decisions.md#d-126--a-knowledge-report-is-cited-as-its-whole-document-and-an-outputter-can-refuse-a-source-before-its-row-exists-2026-11-04)): a deck can be planned from knowledge reports, alone or beside runs, which finishes E-6.3's original scope. I-6 stays 🟡 for the deferred reel (E-6.5).
**Progress 2026-10-19 (Phase 3).** Shipped: the generation runtime (E-6.7, #281 #284 #293), books complete (E-6.1, #298 #303 #310), decks (E-6.3, #314), sites (S-6.2.1, #315) and the newsletter with scheduled delivery (E-6.4, #334 #337). Model-written framing for books and the newsletter's featured section are opt-in, not the default ([D-093](decisions.md#d-093--book-cohesion-pass-ships-opt-in-none-stays-the-default-after-two-blind-rounds-2026-10-19), [D-097](decisions.md#d-097--newsletter-featured-section-ships-opt-in-the-rest-of-an-issue-is-listed-from-the-database-2026-10-19)). Remaining: site publishing (S-6.2.2) and the reel (E-6.5, waits on the TTS decision). *2026-10-25 ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)): site publishing is won't-do, the reel is deferred, and L2 plan questions 3 and 4 (book template variants, a hosted site preview) are deferred.*
**Status:** 🟡 foundation shipped 2026-05-05. Schema + Outputter Protocol + lifecycle + REST surface + tier gating + first concrete outputter (Book v1 Markdown) all live. Sites / decks / newsletters / reels are E-6.2/.3/.4/.5 follow-ups that plug into the same registry. Per [D-044](decisions.md#d-044--foundation-first-then-concrete-implementations-deferred-for-i-3--i-6-2026-05-05), the foundation ships with one minimum-viable outputter (deterministic Book v1) so the abstraction is validated end-to-end with real content; LLM cohesion + the other kinds are individually-scoped follow-ups.

**Planned 2026-10-18.** Every epic below now carries a scope, an acceptance statement and PR-sized stories; the research behind the choices (PDF and EPUB toolchains, site generation without Node, python-pptx, scheduler, TTS providers, the B-roll licensing risk) is in the plan. Two corrections from the code audit:
- The stub `/author` page of 2026-10-18 **could not produce a successful book**: it posted `{kind, title}` with no `source_ids`, and `BookMarkdownOutputter.generate` raises `OutputError` on an empty list, so every output created from the UI landed as `failed`. PR [#224](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/224) replaced it on 2026-10-19 with a chapter picker ([D-069](decisions.md#d-069--shipped-backend-surfaces-ingest-as-a-library-tab-author-studio-as-one-page-readiness-as-a-gate-mean-2026-10-19)); S-6.1.1 now carries only the remainder.
- The roadmap's `output_citations` table and `GET /outputs/{id}/download` were never built; `outputs` has `content_text` / `content_path` only, generation is synchronous inside the request, and there is no Celery task for outputs. Carried by E-6.7.

### E-6.1 🟢 Books (long-form Markdown → PDF / EPUB)

**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — S-6.1.1 to S-6.1.5 are merged; S-6.1.6 is a proposed follow-up outside the acceptance. Book template variants (L2 plan question 3) are deferred ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)). The acceptance holds with framing chosen (`cohesion=full`); the default book has no model-written framing, per [D-093](decisions.md#d-093--book-cohesion-pass-ships-opt-in-none-stays-the-default-after-two-blind-rounds-2026-10-19).

**v1 shipped 2026-05-05** in PR #173. `BookMarkdownOutputter` performs deterministic structural concatenation of selected job reports + per-job Q&A history into a Markdown manuscript with TOC. No LLM in v1 — that's the point: validate the schema + lifecycle + REST surface end-to-end before adding cohesion.

**Scope (2026-10-18).** Make the book real: a source picker, a true Markdown source for reports, an LLM cohesion pass, endnote citations with deep links, and PDF / EPUB downloads. Books are the first Author kind to finish because every other kind reuses its pieces (Markdown source, citation contract, async generation).
**Acceptance.** A user picks three completed jobs, titles the book, and gets a manuscript with an LLM-written introduction, ordered chapters with transitions, a glossary, numbered endnotes that deep-link to timestamps, and a downloadable PDF and EPUB; generation runs in the worker with progress; a failed chapter is disclosed in the manuscript rather than silently dropped (the D-056 posture).

**Tasks (epic-level, pre-2026-10-18 numbering kept)**
- [x] T-6.1.1 v1 deterministic Markdown bundle — *shipped 2026-05-05*.
- [x] T-6.1.2 🟢 LLM-driven cohesion (chapter ordering, transition prose, auto-generated introductions, glossary extraction) — *shipped 2026-10-19 as S-6.1.3, PR [#310](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/310), opt-in per [D-093](decisions.md#d-093--book-cohesion-pass-ships-opt-in-none-stays-the-default-after-two-blind-rounds-2026-10-19).*
- [x] T-6.1.3 🟢 PDF conversion — *shipped 2026-10-19 as S-6.1.5, PR [#303](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/303).* *Was: now S-6.1.5 (the plan rejects `anthropic-skills:docx` and `pypandoc` as the primary path; see plan).*
- [x] T-6.1.4 🟢 EPUB conversion — *shipped 2026-10-19 as S-6.1.5, PR [#303](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/303).*
- [x] T-6.1.5 🟢 Frontend "Author Studio" surface — *shipped 2026-10-19 PR [#224](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/224). Scope moved from "a Pro+ button on a job page" to the full `/author` page, because a book spans several runs. Kinds come from `GET /author/kinds`: `available` kinds get a create flow, the rest render as "Coming later · E-6.x" with no button (a 501 is never offered). The create modal names the book, picks chapters from the user's finished topic / channel runs (runs without a report or not yet completed are listed but disabled, so the 501 / empty-chapter paths are unreachable from the UI), and toggles `include_qa` / `include_toc`. Outputs list with kind / status filters, failed rows show `error_message` in full, pending rows explain they were created when their kind had no generator. Reader modal renders the Markdown at reading measure; Download fetches `/content` with the bearer header and saves a blob (`<a href>` cannot carry the token); Delete confirms first. Tier-locked state when `has('author_studio')` is false or the API 403s. Linked decision: [D-069](decisions.md#d-069--shipped-backend-surfaces-ingest-as-a-library-tab-author-studio-as-one-page-readiness-as-a-gate-mean-2026-10-19). What remains of the original scope: the job-page shortcut and the server-side guard (S-6.1.1) and reading outputs in the shared reader shell (S-8.8.3).*

#### S-6.1.2 🟢 Markdown source for reports
**PR:** [#255](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/255) — *shipped 2026-10-19.* Shared enabler for the book, the reading room (E-8.8) and public sharing (E-8.11). Converter: `markdownify` (MIT) through a `ReportMarkdownConverter` subclass; html2text rejected as GPL-3.0. The tenant-scoped job lookup S-5.10.6 added to the outputter is kept. Linked decision: [D-079](decisions.md#d-079--report-markdown-source-on-markdownify-new-report-routes-are-header-bearer-only-2026-10-19).
- [x] T-6.1.2.1 `report_service.report_markdown(job)` / `html_to_markdown(html)`: drops the document head and stylesheet, ATX headings, hyphen bullets, no `*`/`_` escaping, no autolinks (a citation stays `[text](href)` with its `&t=`), the statistics grid as a one-row table, `[VISUAL @ …]` spans verbatim; cached at `<report_path>.md`, rewritten when older than the HTML; an unwritable cache degrades to converting every time; `delete_report` removes the cache too — *shipped 2026-10-19*

#### S-6.1.3 🟢 LLM cohesion pass
**PR:** [#310](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/310) — *shipped 2026-10-19, opt-in.* Absorbs T-6.1.2. Judged blind in two rounds against the `none` book ([notes](notes/2026-10-19-book-cohesion-judging.md)): three judges preferred the framed edition in 18 of 18 verdicts, but each round drew 27 flagged framing statements against a pre-registered limit of 3, so `none` stays the default ([D-093](decisions.md#d-093--book-cohesion-pass-ships-opt-in-none-stays-the-default-after-two-blind-rounds-2026-10-19)). Cost per book $0.09-0.17. Found by the trial and fixed: prose returned as JSON broke on paragraph breaks and quotes (now marked sections), and a reordered book's prose cited the user's chapter numbers.

- [x] T-6.1.3.3 Chapter bodies are never sent to or rewritten by a model; model text is stripped of links, footnote markers, headings, HTML and tables before it is spliced in; the front matter names the models; a failed call leaves the lower level and a processing note says which call failed — *shipped 2026-10-19*

  **Shipped:** 2026-11-05 — PR [#414](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/414) (batch 6 of 2026-11-04). A Markdown paragraph that is all emphasis takes no drop cap, so the cap moves to the book's first real paragraph.

**PR:** [#298](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/298) — *shipped 2026-10-19.* Endnotes are GFM footnotes (`[^n]`), which the reading room (remark-gfm), GitHub and the EPUB/PDF converters all render as linked superscripts with back-links; endnote `n` is always `output_citations` position `n`. - [x] T-6.1.4.2 `cite_references` cites each answer from its stored `references` (`Sources: [1:35](url)[^31], …`); links in the answer prose are reduced to text; a reference resolves to a document only if it is among the run's visible documents, otherwise it keeps its own link, title and channel — *shipped 2026-10-19*

Title")`, a port of the reader's slug, so `#1-first-topic` (and Devanagari ids) exist in the reader, on GitHub and in the EPUB/PDF HTML.* Table-of-contents links resolve: today the TOC links `#first-topic` while the chapter heading is `## 1. First Topic`, whose generated id differs; fold into the no-dead-links test. #### S-6.1.5 🟢 PDF and EPUB downloads
**PR:** [#303](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/303) — *shipped 2026-10-19.* Absorbs T-6.1.3 and T-6.1.4. Every book is built as EPUB 3 by default (no native dependency) and as PDF when the server has a converter. **This machine has no Pango**, so WeasyPrint installs but cannot load (`OSError: cannot load library 'libgobject-2.0-0'`); the PDF here comes from the fallback, Chromium 156 through Playwright 1.64, which prints the stylesheet's page-number margin boxes (Chromium 131 and later) but not running chapter titles or contents page numbers. The WeasyPrint branch is tested only against a stand-in module. Found while testing: book tests would have written EPUBs into the checkout's `data/outputs`; the suite now gets a temporary `OUTPUTS_DIR` and runs with `AUTHOR_PDF_ENGINE=off`.
- [x] T-6.1.5.1 `book_html`: markdown-it-py (CommonMark, GFM tables, strikethrough, footnotes; raw HTML off; images become their alt text) with the reader's heading ids (`heading_slug`, `-2` for duplicates) and ARIA roles on endnotes; `assets/book.css` mirrors the reader's type (Source Serif, Fraunces, a Devanagari face, local fallbacks). `book_pdf` prints it with WeasyPrint, else Chromium (`AUTHOR_PDF_ENGINE` auto / weasyprint / chromium / off), A5, a page per chapter, page numbers, PDF outline and tagged PDF; neither engine fetches from the network. `content_path` is the PDF when there is one, else the EPUB; both are served by E-6.7's download endpoint — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.1.5.2 `book_epub`: hand-rolled EPUB 3 (`zipfile` + lxml): `mimetype` first and stored, `container.xml`, `content.opf` (urn:uuid of the output id, `dcterms:modified`), `nav.xhtml` with toc and landmarks in place of the manuscript's own contents section, one XHTML file per chapter, `epub:type` noteref / endnotes / endnote, cross-file links rewritten, every file parsed back as XML before it is written. markdown-it-py, mdit-py-plugins (MIT) and lxml (BSD-3-Clause) declared in `requirements.txt` with their licences — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.1.5.3 WeasyPrint and Playwright live in `requirements-author.txt` with the Pango steps for Windows and Linux. `parameters.formats` picks the files (default: EPUB, plus PDF when a converter exists); the router resolves it before the row exists and writes it back, so an explicit PDF with no converter is 501 with what to install and leaves no row. `GET /author/kinds` reports per kind what can be built and why not; the create modal offers EPUB and PDF and shows the reason when PDF is unavailable. A worker without a converter fails the row with the same message before building chapters — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.1.5.4 `test_book_formats.py` (16 tests) on two chapters of the real smoke-test report: the EPUB container, manifest, spine and nav are valid and every in-book link (60 endnote references, back-links, navigation) lands on an element that exists; the PDF has an outline, contents links that jump to the chapter pages, its endnote URLs, every font embedded, and a Devanagari quote extractable as text; the PDF engine fetches nothing from a local HTTP server the document points at; 501 and worker-failure paths; engine order. EPUBCheck (Java) was not run — *shipped 2026-10-19*

### E-6.2 🟢 Static personal-wiki site

`kind="site"` ships with `SiteOutputter` (S-6.2.1, PR [#315](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/315), 2026-10-19); publishing integrations (S-6.2.2) will not be built (D-112).

**Scope-changed 2026-10-25:** 🟡 → 🟢. Sites are download only, with no one-click publishing ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)), so S-6.2.2 is won't-do and the epic is shipped. A hosted site preview under the app's origin (L2 plan question 4) is deferred. The zip also covers report sharing while M11 is deferred: `source_ids` accepts `job:<id>`.

**Scope (2026-10-18).** A downloadable static site built from a shelf or a set of jobs: one page per report (reader typography), one per knowledge artifact, an index, tag pages, client-side search, citation links out to sources. Built by the backend with Jinja2 templates, **not** by Astro or 11ty: the plan rejects a Node toolchain inside the Python worker. Hosting integrations are a later story.
**Acceptance.** A user picks a shelf, generates a site, downloads a zip, opens `index.html` from disk and can read every report, search by title, and follow citations; the site has no app chrome and makes no network calls except to sources.

#### S-6.2.1 🟢 `SiteOutputter` (zip of static HTML)
**PR:** [#315](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/315) — *shipped 2026-10-19.* No model is called. Found on the first live site: page names took eight id characters, and two seed runs (`lanec-job-1`, `lanec-job-2`) shared them, so paths are now allocated unique within the site. Knowledge artifacts appear on their source's page when one was extracted; there is no separate knowledge page.
- [x] T-6.2.1.1 `source_ids` take `shelf:<id>`, `job:<id>` or a bare run id; shelves, runs, documents (D-063 visibility), tags and Q&A all resolve inside the caller's library; anything else is left out and named on the index and about pages; nothing publishable fails the output with the reason — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.2.1.2 Jinja2 templates (autoescaped) under `app/templates/site/`: index, report (the run's report and Q&A, citations as endnotes linking both to the source's page and to the moment in the video), source (backlinks with moments, tags, the knowledge report when extracted), tag, about, search; one stylesheet built from the book stylesheet plus layout and a dark scheme, no web fonts — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.2.1.3 Search: the index is a script (`assets/search-index.js` sets `window.SITE_INDEX`) because browsers will not load JSON from a page opened from disk; a 40-line vanilla script ranks title hits first; no CDN — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.2.1.4 The zip is the primary artifact (`format=zip`), with a Markdown contents page in `content_text` for the reader; `output_citations` holds every citation on every report page — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.2.1.5 `test_site_outputter.py` (5 tests): a shelf with two real-report runs, a tag and Q&A becomes a site whose every relative link and fragment resolves inside the zip, whose only absolute links go to video hosts, with ASCII file names, backlinks, a search index listing every page and no `fetch`; another user's shelf and run are left out and said so; nothing publishable fails; same-topic runs get distinct pages — *shipped 2026-10-19*

### E-6.3 🟢 Slides (PPTX)

`kind="deck"` ships with `DeckOutputter` (S-6.3.1, PR [#314](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/314), 2026-10-19). A deck from knowledge reports followed (S-6.3.2, PR [#398](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/398), 2026-11-04).

**Scope (2026-10-18).** A deck from one job or a knowledge artifact: an LLM-planned outline (8 to 15 slides), each slide carrying its citations in the notes and a sources slide at the end, rendered with `python-pptx` (the `anthropic-skills:pptx` skill named in the roadmap is a harness skill, not a library the worker can call).
**Acceptance.** A user picks a job, gets a `.pptx` whose every content slide has at least one citation in its notes, and the sources slide lists every cited source with a link.

#### S-6.3.1 🟢 `DeckOutputter`
**PR:** [#314](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/314) — *shipped 2026-10-19.* Sources are the user's own runs (tenant-scoped like books). **Scope-changed 2026-10-19:** a knowledge artifact as the deck's source is not in this story; runs only. Found on the first real deck: 80 timestamped citations made ten sources slides, so a source is now a video listed once with its cited moments as links (19 slides instead of 26). A real two-run deck cost $0.31-0.39 on gpt-5.5.
- [x] T-6.3.1.1 `author_deck_outline` (openai gpt-5.5 medium, guidance entry): the reports reach the model with every RESOLVED citation as a marker `[S12]` (unresolved ones lose theirs, so a slide can only cite what the sources slide can link); JSON with section, bullets and quote slides citing markers; `VISUAL_ANNOTATION_CONTRACT`, English output, quote rendering and time contracts injected — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.3.1.2 `deck_render`: python-pptx (MIT), 16:9, one template drawn in code in the `docs/branding.md` palette (paper, ink, oxblood rules, gold quote bar), Georgia with Nirmala UI as the complex-script face; title (model disclosed), section, bullets, quote and sources slides; rendered through PowerPoint and checked by eye — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.3.1.3 Each content slide: source numbers in its footer, and in its notes every source with the exact moments it cites and their links; the sources slide(s) list each video once with its moments in time order as links; `output_citations` holds one row per cited moment in order of first use; a slide citing nothing is dropped and the count is shown on the last slide; `content_text` is a Markdown rendering for the reader — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.3.1.4 `test_deck_outputter.py` (6 tests, model patched): the file opens; 3 to 15 content slides; every content slide's notes carry a YouTube link and its text no marker, link or footnote; every cited moment is linked from the sources slide, one entry per video, moments in time order; first-use numbering; uncited slides dropped and disclosed; a plan with nothing cited, a failed call and another user's run each fail the output; the estimate endpoint calls no model and writes nothing — *shipped 2026-10-19*

#### S-6.3.2 🟢 Deck from a knowledge artifact
**Shipped:** 2026-11-04 — PR [#398](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/398) (batch 4 of 2026-11-04). Linked decision: [D-126](decisions.md#d-126--a-knowledge-report-is-cited-as-its-whole-document-and-an-outputter-can-refuse-a-source-before-its-row-exists-2026-11-04). The half of E-6.3's scope that S-6.3.1 left out (scope change 2026-10-19): a deck planned from one document's knowledge report rather than from runs. It works alone or beside runs. No migration and no new setting.

A new outputter hook `check_sources` runs on create and on estimate, before any row is written: 404 for a document the caller cannot see (the same answer as one that does not exist), 409 for one with no report. `DECK_SYSTEM` and the run-only prompt are unchanged*

- [x] T-6.3.2.3 Tests: the same acceptance as S-6.3.1 (every content slide cites a linked source; uncited slides dropped and disclosed) — *shipped 2026-11-04: `test_deck_knowledge.py` (11 tests) through `POST /author/outputs` or the worker task, vitest `authorDeckKnowledge` and `authorOutputs`, 24 mutations caught. In a deck from one knowledge report every slide cites the same source, so the acceptance holds but says less than it does for runs: it shows the model cited what it was given, not that each bullet is supported*
- A knowledge report links to the source itself, or, for an uploaded PDF, to its report in the app (`APP_BASE_URL/knowledge/<document_id>`). A document that drops out between create and the worker is left out and disclosed on the last slide; if nothing is left, the output fails with the reason. Limits: nothing checks a quote slide against any text, so the prompt note is the only guard, and no real model has run on it; a table written without a leading pipe reads as one paragraph (the knowledge prompt does not ask for tables); the filter folds case for ASCII only on SQLite.

### E-6.4 🟢 Newsletter / digest (recurring scheduled output)

`kind="newsletter"` ships with `NewsletterOutputter` (S-6.4.1, PR [#334](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/334)) and subscription and scheduled delivery (S-6.4.2, PR [#337](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/337)).
**Shipped:** 2026-10-19 — PRs [#334](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/334), [#337](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/337)

**Scope (2026-10-18).** A periodic email digest: what entered the library since the last issue, new Q&As, a featured deep-dive (one report section or knowledge artifact), and pinned saved-search changes (E-8.3). Runs on the scheduler E-8.3 builds (S-8.3.1); sends through the existing `email_service`.
**Acceptance.** A user enables a weekly digest; an issue is generated and emailed every week with only material newer than the previous issue; an issue with nothing new is skipped and says so in the outputs list; the digest is also readable in-app as an output.

#### S-6.4.1 🟢 `NewsletterOutputter`
**PR:** [#334](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/334) — *shipped 2026-10-19.* Linked decision: [D-097](decisions.md#d-097--newsletter-featured-section-ships-opt-in-the-rest-of-an-issue-is-listed-from-the-database-2026-10-19). The featured section was judged blind against the report's own summary under a rule fixed in advance ([notes](notes/2026-10-19-newsletter-featured-judging.md)). It was preferred in 12 of 12 verdicts but drew 16 unsupported-claim flags against a limit of 4, so it is **opt-in**. Three generation defects were found and fixed before judging: excerpts with no citation markers, invented markers that resolved, and doubled timestamps. The plan's premailer was not needed; a tag-to-style map inlines the email styles.
- [x] T-6.4.1.1 `parameters` carry `since` / `until` (ISO; `since` defaults to the reader's last issue, else seven days) and `sections` (featured, library, knowledge, questions, saved_searches, outputs; unknown names refused); lists are documents new to this reader by visibility grant (D-063), knowledge reports, the reader's job / library / history Q&A, saved searches whose answer changed, and completed outputs, each in `(since, until]`; the resolved window, counts and featured source are written back to the row — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.4.1.2 `author_newsletter_compose` (anthropic claude-sonnet-5 low, guidance entry) writes the featured deep-dive only, from one report or knowledge report: one new in the window, else the least recently featured (resurfacing the reader's own library). The excerpt is the summary plus the report's cited points, and only markers shown to the model can become links. A section citing nothing, or a failed call, falls back to the source's own summary with a processing note. **Off by default** per [D-097](decisions.md#d-097--newsletter-featured-section-ships-opt-in-the-rest-of-an-issue-is-listed-from-the-database-2026-10-19). About $0.016 per issue — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.4.1.3 `templates/newsletter/email.html`: a table layout with inline styles, a tag-to-style map applied to the rendered issue, and no `<style>`, `<link>`, images or scripts; links are absolute to `APP_BASE_URL`. The email is `artifact.html` (Download HTML); `content_text` is the Markdown issue (the reader and the email's plain-text part) — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.4.1.4 `test_newsletter_outputter.py` (12 tests): the window boundaries (a grant exactly at `since` is out, one at `until` in); an empty issue ends with the new `skipped` status and its reason, with no files; section toggles and unknown names; featured markers linked, unknown and unshown ones dropped, citations recorded; both fallbacks disclosed; resurfacing; a self-contained email; `since` defaulting to the last issue; router validation and the estimate; featured off by default with no model call — *shipped 2026-10-19*

#### S-6.4.2 🟢 Subscription and delivery
**PR:** [#337](https://github.com/khoks/VideoResearchPro/pull/337) — *shipped 2026-10-19.* Linked decision: [D-077](decisions.md#d-077--scheduler-one-static-beat-entry-over-table-rows-beat-is-its-own-process-with-no-pidfile-2026-10-19) (the scheduler it runs on). Idempotency is a unique `(subscription_id, period_key)` row claimed **before** the issue is made, so a retried tick or a duplicate worker generates and sends nothing. The period is the slot's ISO week, date or month in the subscription's own timezone.
- [x] T-6.4.2.1 `newsletter_subscriptions` (one per reader: title, cadence, sections, enabled, `last_issue_at`) and `newsletter_sends` (one per period, with status `sent` / `email_failed` / `skipped` / `failed`); `GET` / `PUT` / `DELETE /author/newsletter` and `POST /author/newsletter/send-now`; a **Digest by email** card on the Author page (on/off, cadence, sections, Send one now, Unsubscribe, recent sends) — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.4.2.2 Scheduler kind `newsletter` → `newsletter_service.deliver`: claim the period, make the issue over `(last_issue_at, now]`, email it (HTML with a plain-text part and a deterministic `Message-ID`, through the fail-soft `email_service`), and post a `newsletter_issue` notification. A skipped issue sends nothing, and `last_issue_at` advances either way so windows never overlap. The plan is re-checked at run time, so a reader without Author Studio gets no issue. "Send now" uses its own period key — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.4.2.3 `test_newsletter_delivery.py` (10 tests): one issue and one email per period under a retried tick; the next period continues where the last ended and a period with nothing new is skipped; period keys follow the subscription timezone; turning off or unsubscribing stops the next run; a plan without Author Studio stops issues; the tick passes its slot; the kind is registered and autodiscovered; the HTML part and `Message-ID`; the router round trip — *shipped 2026-10-19*
- [x] T-6.4.2.4 Register the `newsletter` scheduler kind from its own task module and add the module to `celery_app.autodiscover_tasks`, as T-8.3.2.5 does for saved searches. Filed 2026-10-19 by S-8.3.1; [D-077](decisions.md#d-077--scheduler-one-static-beat-entry-over-table-rows-beat-is-its-own-process-with-no-pidfile-2026-10-19). `app/tasks/newsletter_tasks.py` registers it; the task gets the slot it was due at, which decides the period — *shipped 2026-10-19*

### E-6.5 🔴 Video / reel (TTS narration + stills)

`kind="reel"` reserved; outputter pending. Heaviest of the kinds — requires a TTS provider + an ffmpeg pipeline.

**Scope (2026-10-18).** A short vertical video: an LLM-written script from a knowledge artifact or report section, narrated by TTS, over **stills the app already owns** (R1 `visual_frames` captured by E-1.18, generated title cards, and source thumbnails) with captions burned in. The roadmap's "clip-stitched B-roll from source videos" is **not** in v1: re-publishing downloaded YouTube video is a Terms-of-Service risk the plan records, and the stills path needs no new downloads.
**Status.** 🔴 deferred (was ⚪ proposed). Ships last; the TTS provider choice (local model for self-host, paid API for SaaS) is an open question in the plan. **Deferred 2026-10-25** ([D-112](decisions.md#d-112--the-remaining-rulings-of-2026-10-25-map-batching-public-sharing-the-code-mixing-threshold-and-the-quick-calls-2026-10-25)). When built: hosted TTS by default, local TTS as opt-in, a Hindi listening test, and no B-roll (L2 plan questions 1 and 2).
**Acceptance.** A user picks a knowledge artifact, gets a 60 to 90 second 1080×1920 MP4 with narration, captions and a sources end-card; generation cost is shown beforehand; a missing TTS provider yields 501 with setup instructions.

#### S-6.5.2 ⚪ Narration
- [ ] T-6.5.2.1 `tts_service` provider abstraction (`openai` / `local`), per-use-case config like `llm_routing`, cached audio per scene
#### S-6.5.3 ⚪ Assembly
- [ ] T-6.5.3.1 ffmpeg pipeline: stills + Ken Burns pan, captions, narration mix, end-card; `content_path` MP4; download through E-6.7
